# Decision log

Append-only (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:82`). One entry per decision: the question, the choice, the reason, and the source lines it rests on (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:542`). Decisions are made by Claude under BUILD_BRIEF §16 and never block the build; the plain-language versions are in `docs/for-casey-review.md`.

Citations are `path:LINE`. "Changes file" means `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md`; "v9.3" means `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md`.

## K2 (spec), October 27, 2026

- **D-001** Question: what format do requirements in `docs/spec/` take? The Kit Plan asks for IDs like REQ-MONEY-012 with Given/When/Then criteria (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:30`); the brief only says "numbered requirements, one file per area" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:80`). Choice: one testable sentence per requirement, with a stable area-prefixed ID (for example `TXT-001`) and one or more `path:LINE` citations, checked by `scripts/trace_spec.py`. Given/When/Then cases are written per ID in K5 test design. Reason: the brief outranks the Kit Plan on how (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:7`); a one-sentence rule is the smallest testable unit and K5 owns test cases (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`).
- **D-002** Question: K2 is "one area per session" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:595`); this task wrote every area in one session. Choice: accept one session for all areas, with the traceability script as the check. Reason: the runner assigned K2 as one task; the K3 conflict review (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:597`) re-reads every area with a fresh reviewer, which covers the risk the per-area rule guards against.
- **D-003** Question: quiet hours. v9.3 says 9 p.m.–8 a.m. (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:200`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:264`); the changes file says no non-urgent texts after 8 p.m. in the stricter of Arizona and area-code time (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:121`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:446`). Choice: non-urgent texts are sent only when the local time is between 8:00 a.m. and 8:00 p.m. in **both** Arizona time and the recipient's area-code zone. The v9.3 exceptions stay: urgent safety texts, STOP/HELP/START replies, pick codes, and direct replies within 15 minutes of the person's own text. Reason: the changes file wins on the window; it is silent on replies the person just asked for, so v9.3 still applies there.
- **D-004** Question: the "business clock". v9.3 runs timers on 8 a.m.–9 p.m. (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:179`), but texts may no longer go out after 8 p.m. (D-003) and the quote window counts every day (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:66`). Choice: business hours are 8:00 a.m.–8:00 p.m. Arizona time, every day of the week; "business hour" timers (confirm, card fix, bank approval) count only time inside that window. Reason: a business can't be asked to act in a window when we can't text it; every-day counting follows the changes file.
- **D-005** Question: does an opt-out word close jobs? v9.3 says any opt-out word, including bare CANCEL, closes jobs not yet with a contractor (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:163`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:199`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:215`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:260`). The changes file says a single opt-out word is an opt-out, not a job cancel, and "cancel" alone never closes a job (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:112`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:159`). Choice: an opt-out stops all texts to that number and changes no job status; the homeowner's jobs carry on through the quote page and email and end by CLOSE, the close button, a cancel-intent message or their own timeouts. Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-006** Question: are natural-language opt-outs ("please stop texting me") still honored, given the changes file defines single-word opt-outs (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:158`)? Choice: yes, as in v9.3 (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:261`); a message that merely contains an opt-out word in another sense ("can stop by at 3") is not an opt-out (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:188`). Reason: consent may be revoked by any reasonable means; honoring more revocations is the legally safest option.
- **D-007** Question: the opt-out confirmation wording differs: "To use us again, text START." (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:95`) vs "To use Ask Hava again, text START." (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:158`). Choice: "To use Ask Hava again, text START." Reason: section 7 is the later, more specific legal text and names the brand.
- **D-008** Question: the changes file says the full consent text is the first message on every path (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:113`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:445`) and also that the emergency safety text is sent before the consent text (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`). Choice: when an emergency keyword matches, the safety text goes first and the consent text is the first message after it; on every other path the consent text is the first message. Reason: both lines are in the authoritative file; the safety line is the specific exception.
- **D-009** Question: fee tier boundaries and the v9.3 "$149 major job" rule (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:343`). Choice: the fee basis is the chosen quote's amount; under $500.00 is $25; $500.00 up to and including $1,500.00 is $75; over $1,500.00 is $199 (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:59`). No AI flag changes the tier. Reason: the changes file sets tiers by the quote only; "$500–$1,500" reads as inclusive at both ends.
- **D-010** Question: how is "4 months of their price" applied to a recurring price not stated per month (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:60`)? Choice: convert to a monthly amount first (weekly × 52 ÷ 12, every two weeks × 26 ÷ 12, quarterly ÷ 3, yearly ÷ 12; a range uses its midpoint first), then multiply by 4. K2b worked examples pin the cases. Reason: simplest rule that keeps the 4-month meaning; changeable as a catalog setting.
- **D-011** Question: when does a job show quotes? v9.3 waits for 3 quotes or 4 business hours (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:171`); the changes file texts the homeowner at the first quote and lets them pick any time, with businesses given up to 24 hours (every day) to quote (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:82`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:83`). Choice: the job moves to Quotes ready at the first quote; quotes are accepted until 24 calendar hours after the job card was sent or until a pick is confirmed, whichever is first. Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-012** Question: "one update text every few hours" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:84`) has no number. Choice: at most one quote-count update text per job every 3 hours, measured from the delivery time of the previous update text; the gap is a setting. Reason: smallest concrete value consistent with "few"; easy to change later.
- **D-013** Question: retention periods differ between v9.3 (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:435`–`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:439`) and the brief (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:354`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:359`). Choice: the brief's decided defaults (contact details 180 days after close unless a dispute or complaint; photos 30 days; consent, opt-out and message logs 5 years; ledger and audit 7 years). Message bodies still shrink to the template ID after 30 days (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:438`) while the log entry itself is kept 5 years. Scrubbed job text is kept 3 years (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:437`), since the brief gives no period. Reason: the changes file is silent; the brief is the decision layer and is stricter on privacy.
- **D-014** Question: sign-in methods differ between v9.3's portal table (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:373`–`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:375`) and the brief (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:315`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:317`). Choice: businesses sign in with a single-use emailed link (15 minutes) plus an SMS code on a new device, 30-day sessions; the owner signs in with an argon2id password plus a passkey or TOTP, sessions at most 12 hours; homeowners keep v9.3's phone plus 6-digit code, 30 days per device. Reason: the brief wins on how (security); it is silent on the homeowner portal and on the owner session length, so v9.3's values fill those gaps.
- **D-015** Question: the pick-code "cooldown" length (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:312`). Choice: 15 minutes, per v9.3 (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:190`). Reason: brief silent on the number; v9.3 fills it.
- **D-016** Question: when is a business paused for complaints? v9.3 pauses on any complaint (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:462`); the changes file pauses only after 3 complaints in 90 days (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:91`). Choice: 3 complaints in 90 days; every complaint still alerts Casey. Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-017** Question: how are licenses checked? v9.3 uses browser lookups twice a day (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:69`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:128`); the changes file uses the ROC's daily downloadable lists plus a live lookup at each pick (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:38`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:250`). Choice: the changes file's method; v9.3's "never guess" rules stay (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:130`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:194`). Reason: the changes file wins; it is silent on failure handling.
- **D-018** Question: v9.3's "web chat" status banner says "use the chat on this site" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:134`) while revision note 1 removes the old /chat links (`sources/03_business_and_design/Ask_Hava_Prototype_Revision_Notes_for_ChatGPT.md:28`). Choice: the old directory-era /chat page and its links go; the request web chat on askhava.com is built as the fallback channel. Reason: the two refer to different things; the changes file requires web chat (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:177`).
- **D-019** Question: v9.3 gives every business one free first win (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:325`). Choice: only businesses that join in the first 90 days after launch get a free first win, keyed by ROC number, city-list entity or Stripe card fingerprint and checked when used (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:67`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:116`). "Launch" is the date of Gate C, stored as a setting. Reason: the changes file wins; Gate C is the first day a real business can join.
- **D-020** Question: the Spanish opt-out list says "PARAR, ALTO, CANCELAR, BAJA, etc." (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:114`). Choice: the single-word list is exactly parar, alto, cancelar, baja (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:443`), kept in settings so words can be added; longer Spanish revocations are caught by D-006. Reason: the brief names the list; the settings list makes additions a configuration change.
- **D-021** Question: which consent replies count? The changes file says "Reply OK" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:213`); v9.3 also accepts Yes, Sure and Sí (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:608`). Choice: accept OK, Yes, Sure and Sí (any case), and store the exact reply. Reason: compatible; v9.3 fixed a real bug where natural replies were ignored.
- **D-022** Question: the brand and HELP contact in v9.3 use "Havasu Home Quotes" and hhq.example (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:259`). Choice: the HELP reply names Ask Hava and gives the help email and phone number from settings, plus the STOP instruction. Reason: the brand is superseded (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:24`).
- **D-023** Question: quote-card insurance wording: "Insurance on file: Yes / No" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:48`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:144`) vs "Insurance (business-reported): Yes/No" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:190`). Choice: "Insurance (business-reported): Yes/No". Reason: section 7 is the later legal wording and the brief repeats it (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:452`).
- **D-024** Question: v9.3's app host is Render (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:66`) and it has a backup person (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:464`). Choice: Railway Pro if its Backups tab shows point-in-time recovery, otherwise Render (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:72`); no backup person, so alerts wait for Casey and safe mode starts after 24 hours (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:73`). Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-025** Question: how do quiet hours apply when a number's area code maps to no known time zone (D-003)? Choice: use the hours that are inside 8 a.m.–8 p.m. in every US time zone at that moment. Reason: "stricter" can't be computed without a zone, so the legally safest window is used (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`; `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`).
- **D-026** Question: masked calls "after 2 bad PINs read back first name and street before connecting" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:110`). Read to an unknown caller, that would release contact details outside a confirmed, paid pick (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:151`). Choice: the read-back happens only on calls from a phone linked to a business whose pick for that job is confirmed and paid (or a recorded free win); the caller confirms with a key press before connecting. Unlinked phones are refused after bad PINs and hear no details (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:189`). Reason: keeps G1's fix while keeping the vault invariant.
- **D-027** Question: the split-job check's "short window" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:208`) has no number. Choice: 30 days, as a setting. Reason: legally safest choice among short windows (a longer window catches more split jobs); easy to change.
- **D-028** Question: v9.3's business setting "maximum cards per week" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:396`) vs "every qualified opted-in business gets every matching card (no cap)" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`). Choice: no weekly maximum; a business controls volume with its job-type checklist, towns and pause. Reason: the changes file wins; smallest scope.
- **D-029** Question: INT-014 asks "Which fits best?" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:121`) while the brief bans "best" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:454`). Choice: the ban covers claims about or rankings of businesses; the neutral job-type prompt to the homeowner stays and is exempt from the word scan. Reason: the brief's rule is about claims on businesses, and the prompt describes no business.
- **D-030** Question: v9.3 moves an Open job with no quote to "No quotes yet" at 9 p.m. (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:171`) but sends the text at 6 p.m. (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:210`). Choice: both happen at 6 p.m. Reason: the 8 p.m. quiet-hours limit (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`) and one consistent state and text.

## K2b (category list and worked examples), October 27, 2026

- **D-031** Question: which categories go in the K2b list? The financial model uses 25 launch categories (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:33`), listed in the simulation brief (`sources/06_briefs_sent_to_other_ais/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Full_Business_Simulation_for_Grok.md:79`–`sources/06_briefs_sent_to_other_ais/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Full_Business_Simulation_for_Grok.md:103`); legal decision 4 says each decided job "is one row in the category list" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:240`), and section 7 adds routing rows (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:204`–`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:209`). Choice: `docs/catalog/categories.yaml` holds 39 rows: the 25 model categories, plus dryer-vent cleaning, plug-in holiday lights, estate cleanout, donation pickup, septic pumping, alarm systems, security cameras, home inspection and home watch, plus the Grok class-mapping trades (water treatment R-54, gutters R-45, insulation R-40, pavers and irrigation R-21, epoxy floors R-8 or R-34). Garage doors and junk removal are listed and off (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:249`); the eight "left out" kinds are listed as not offered (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:34`). Pest control is on but marked not advertised (7.1 item 3). Reason: the changes file requires every one of these rows; the K2b deliverable is the confirmed list (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:31`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:536`).
- **D-032** Question: license rules the sources leave open or mark "to confirm in K2b". Choice: solar screens take R-65, the class Grok mapped for window film and solar screens (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:209`), with no handymen; boat repair and boat detailing need no license and are not construction-type (vessel work), while dock, boat-lift and seawall work is refused there and goes to ROC licensees; security cameras list R-67, with broader classes added when the ROC scope table is loaded (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:204`); garage doors stay off with no class (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:240`). The `handyman_allowed` flags follow the simulation's `hm` column (`sources/04_reviews/grok_v93_simulation/code/config.py:13`–`sources/04_reviews/grok_v93_simulation/code/config.py:37`: plumbing, pool repair, handyman, appliance repair and garage doors, which is off) plus security cameras (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:204`); every other category goes only to its licensed class. Gas appliance repair (gas dryer, range or oven) is refused for handymen and goes to R-63 only (LIC-021). Each row carries a `review` note naming its source and anything an attorney may still confirm. Reason: legally safest choice in each case (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:536`); switching a row is a logged settings change (LIC-002).
- **D-033** Question: the 4-month conversion and range midpoints can produce fractions of a cent (for example $35 weekly × 52 ÷ 12 × 4 = $606.666…, or a $1,500.00–$1,500.01 range with midpoint $1,500.005). Choice: compute exactly, then round the fee basis **down** to the whole cent before choosing the tier. This changes a fee only at the $1,500 edge, where the $75 tier includes $1,500.00: $4,500.01 a year gives $1,500.0033…, kept at $75 (W30), and the $1,500.005 midpoint is kept at $75 (W31). At the $500 edge it never changes the fee, because anything under $500.00 stays under it after rounding down (W17). Reason: the sources are silent; rounding down never charges a business a higher tier because of a fraction of a cent (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:59`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:60`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:61`).
- **D-034** Question: the set tier for trip-fee-plus-estimate quotes per category (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:61`). Choice: the simulation's small/medium/large trip tiers (`sources/04_reviews/grok_v93_simulation/code/config.py:13`–`sources/04_reviews/grok_v93_simulation/code/config.py:37`) map to $25/$75/$199; new rows take $25 unless the typical job is clearly $500–$1,500 (estate cleanout, insulation, pavers, epoxy floors: $75). Reason: matches the typical job size; each value is a catalog setting.
- **D-035** Question: who does the $1,000 handyman limit apply to (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:41`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:45`)? Choice: to a construction-type quote from a business working under the handyman exemption, or from any business in a no-license construction-type category; it is judged on the top of a range, and $1,000.00 exactly is refused ("under $1,000"). It never applies to non-construction categories (a $1,200 car detail is charged $75, W29). A trip-fee quote has no full price to test, so the handyman's per-quote certification covers it (LIC-025). Reason: follows the statute's "under" wording and the changes file's construction-only scope.
- **D-036** Question: how are the worked examples self-checked (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:536`)? Choice: `scripts/check_catalog.py` (beside `scripts/trace_spec.py`) restates the fee rules as constants, recomputes all 32 examples, checks the YAML's own `fee_rules` block (tiers, 4 months, monthly conversion, midpoint, rounding, handyman limit) against them, checks each category's fields, the off and not-offered lists and that pest control is not advertised, and scans both files for superseded values (via `trace_spec.SUPERSEDED`) and banned wording ("commission", "vetted", "best"). Tests in `tests/test_check_catalog.py` feed it broken copies (2-month multiplier, wrong tier, rounding up, old fee, old brand, garage doors on, pest control advertised). New library: **PyYAML 6.0.2** (MIT license), used only to read the catalog with `safe_load`; `pip-audit 2.9.0` on `pyyaml==6.0.2` reported "No known vulnerabilities found". Money is written as quoted strings and parsed with `fractions.Fraction`, never floats. Reason: the catalog is YAML by the brief's layout (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:81`), and Python's standard library has no YAML reader.
- **D-037** Question: is every quote in a recurring category counted as 4 months (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:60`)? A house cleaner's move-out clean or a pool service's green-pool cleanup is a one-time job. Choice: a quote with a period (weekly, monthly, etc.) uses the 4-month rule; a quote with no period is a one-time job at face value, allowed in a recurring category only for job types listed in that category's `one_time_job_types` (house cleaning: deep and move-out cleaning; pool cleaning: green-pool cleanup; landscape: yard cleanup; pest control: termites; home watch: storm check). A period on a quote in a non-recurring category is an error. Worked example W32: a $300 move-out clean has basis $300.00 and fee $25. Reason: the changes file counts recurring **services** as 4 months; a one-time job is not a recurring service, and the list keeps the face-value path from being used to dodge the rule.

## K3 (clarify and check for conflicts), October 27, 2026

K3 re-read every `docs/spec/` area, the catalog, the changes file, the brief and v9.3 against each other. Each item below was open or in conflict and was decided by the §16 rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`): changes file, then v9.3, then other sources; where all are silent, legally safest, then simplest to change later, then smallest scope. Spec lines that changed name the decision.

- **D-038** Question: `docs/sources.md:29` and the Kit Plan (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:32`, `sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:96`) say K3 questions and brief-vs-changes conflicts go to Casey; the brief says decide and log (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:533`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:539`). Choice: no questions to Casey; every item is decided and logged here. `docs/sources.md:29` is not a K3-owned file, so its wording is left for the next task that owns it. Reason: the brief is Casey's later standing instruction and outranks both (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:7`).
- **D-039** Question (brief vs changes file, a *how* item): the changes file names the required checks `ci`, `twin`, `security`, `a11y`, auto-merge on an up-to-date branch, and a `kit-state` branch (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:296`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:297`); the brief names `Ci`, `Twin`, `Ally` with security inside `Ci`, leaves "up to date" off in favour of the tree-hash check, and uses `claude/kit-state` (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:259`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:26`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:97`). Choice: the brief's names and mechanism. Reason: the brief wins on how (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:539`).
- **D-040** Question: the build plan tests the real AI "at Gate B" (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:33`); the brief tests it in Phase 2 step 5, before "Finished" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:194`). Choice: Phase 2 step 5, from the Railway test project, fake data only. Reason: the brief outranks the build plan on how.
- **D-041** Question: the changes file says register the Twilio campaign only when the full site is live, request page included, so the reviewer sees exactly how opt-in works (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:173`); the build plan puts the full site live only at Gate C (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:149`) while Gate C needs the registration done (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:239`), and no live phone number may be used before Gate C (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:68`). Choice: the Stage 0.5 publication (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:550`), which already carries the privacy policy, SMS terms and opt-in description for the reviewers (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:27`), puts every public page of the final site live as static pages: home, how it works, services (no pest page), the request page showing the full consent text and opt-in flow, terms, privacy policy, SMS terms and help, with a "requests open soon" note. No texting number is shown before Gate C (the page says the number appears when requests open), and nothing takes requests, sends texts or charges until Gate C. The account checklist orders Twilio registration after it. Reason: breaks the circle without any live use before Gate C (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:61`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:68`); showing no number means no inbound text goes unanswered.
- **D-042** Question: D-011's 24-hour quote window vs v9.3's 5-day "No quotes yet" wait (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:172`): once every card's 24 hours pass, no quote can arrive, and a card held overnight by quiet hours would lose part of its window. Choice: each business's 24 hours start when its own card is actually sent (after any quiet-hours hold); a business that becomes eligible while the job is Open or No quotes yet (newly active, unpaused, or newly ticked a matching job type) gets the card then, with its own 24 hours. Reason: "every qualified opted-in business gets every matching card" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`) and "up to 24 hours to quote" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:83`) both hold, and the 5-day wait keeps a purpose.
- **D-043** Question: "Quotes stop being accepted once a pick is confirmed" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:85`): confirmed by the homeowner's code or by the business's Confirm? The same line speaks of businesses "who quoted after the pick". Choice: quotes are accepted (inside each card's window) while the job is Open, No quotes yet, Quotes ready or Awaiting confirm, and refused once the picked business taps Confirm. Reason: the line expects quotes after the homeowner's pick; "confirm" is the business step in v9.3 (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:174`).
- **D-044** Question: when do quoters get "This job is no longer active." (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:85`)? Choice: once, when the job reaches a final status (Done, Closed or Cancelled); a business whose quote is refused after Confirm (D-043) gets it at once. Reason: the line says "whenever the job ends"; sending it at Connected would be wrong when a no-show sends the job back to Quotes ready (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:176`).
- **D-045** Question: when a pick fails and the job returns to Quotes ready, does the 3-day no-pick timer (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:173`) restart, and can the failed quote be chosen again? The twin sets the timer only on the first entry (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:993`), which can leave a job waiting with no timeout. Choice: every entry into Quotes ready starts a fresh 3-day timer; the failed pick's quote shows "no longer available" and can't be chosen again; if no choosable quote is left the job closes and the homeowner is told, as the twin does (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:1131`). Reason: every waiting state has a timeout (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`).
- **D-046** Question: v9.3 moves a Connected job only on a DONE, NO or PROBLEM reply at day 3 or day 14 (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:176`), so a homeowner who never answers leaves it waiting forever. Choice: with no such reply 7 days after the day-14 check-in, the job moves to Closed, with no review request. Reason: every waiting state has a timeout (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`); Closed claims nothing about the work.
- **D-047** Question: is a business refunded when it never calls within 72 hours (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:176`)? The sources list no such refund. Choice: no refund; the call line closes, the no-show is recorded and Casey is alerted; there is no automatic pause for no-shows. The business terms say so (TERMS-005). Reason: the fee is charged for the introduction and never adjusted afterwards (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:238`); smallest scope.
- **D-048** Question: on a homeowner cancel, v9.3 refunds "before any call" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:335`); the changes file refunds "only if they haven't talked to the homeowner" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:98`). Choice: refund unless a call has already counted as a connection (a person answered and it lasted 45 seconds or more, CALL-007). Reason: the changes file wins; "talked" uses the same test as the unreachable-homeowner refund.
- **D-049** Question: the brief lets the homeowner choose email contact at the pick (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:408`), but the unreachable-homeowner refund is judged from the call log (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:334`). Choice: when the homeowner chose email, the business portal shows "homeowner prefers email" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:409`) and the business is told to email; the masked line still opens, for the call attempts the refund rule counts; the same call-log refund rule applies whatever contact method was chosen. Reason: simplest rule to code and to state exactly in the terms (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:212`); a business that emails instead of calling can be refunded while still reaching the homeowner, which costs only the fee and is listed for review.
- **D-050** Question: after an opt-out (D-005) or in web-and-email mode (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:177`) the homeowner can't get the texted pick code the brief requires (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:312`). Choice: when Ask Hava can't text the homeowner, the pick code goes by email to an address the homeowner gave in their own text or web-chat conversation, with the same limits (6 digits, 10 minutes, 5 tries, 15-minute cooldown); an email typed on the quote page never receives codes. A "2 YES" text from an opted-out homeowner's own phone still picks, with the confirmation by email and on the page. Reason: keeps the "page plus the homeowner's own channel" rule that stops forwarded links (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:590`) and sends no text after an opt-out.
- **D-051** Question: how does consent work on the web-chat path (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:113`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:213`)? Choice: the chat shows the full consent text first (version stored) and the person agrees by typing OK or pressing an agree button; a phone number entered there gets the full consent text as its first text and no other text until it replies OK by text, while the request carries on by web and email. Reason: legally safest: a number typed on a web page may not belong to the person typing it, so texting waits for that phone's own OK.
- **D-052** Question: users must be 18 or older (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:213`), but nothing says where this is stated. Choice: the consent text and the web request page both say "You must be 18 or older", beside "Reply OK to agree to the Terms and start". Reason: legally safest; the person sees it before agreeing.
- **D-053** Question: "A STOP returner who texts again is treated as a new request" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:95`) vs v9.3's "the carrier blocks every text until they send START" with email or a call-back instead (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:213`). Choice: no text goes to an opted-out number until it texts START; a message without START is answered by email or a call-back alert (CON-016); after START the number is a new request starting with the consent text (CON-015). Reason: legally safest, and the carrier blocks the reply anyway; the changes line is read as "texts again with START".
- **D-054** Question: after-hours emergencies (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:87`) vs no non-urgent texts after 8 p.m. (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:446`, which excepts urgent safety texts): an emergency job card held until 8 a.m. makes the "after-hours emergencies" tick useless. Choice: for a job the homeowner chose as an emergency, its job cards and confirm requests go at any hour, but at night only to businesses that ticked "after-hours emergencies" (the rest get the card at 8 a.m.); the homeowner's texts about it go at night only for the pick, confirm and connect steps (pick code, "business confirmed", who is calling). All other texts on that job keep quiet hours, and business-hour timers still follow D-004. QH-005 now points to QH-004 for these exceptions. Reason: the changes file bars only non-urgent texts (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`); these texts exist because the homeowner said, after the safety text, that the repair is urgent and chose emergency service (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`), so they are urgent in that line's sense, and the businesses that receive them opted in to after-hours work (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:87`).
- **D-055** Question: v9.3 closes emergency requests early (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:162`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:170`); the changes file continues them after the homeowner says they're safe (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`). Choice: an emergency is never a reason to close early. Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-056** Question: v9.3 re-checks a license before a card if the last check is over 12 hours old (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:128`) and again at Confirm (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:222`); the changes file uses daily lists plus a single throttled live lookup at each pick (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:38`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:250`), and other agencies need their own lookups (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:39`). Choice: every license is checked daily, from the agency's published list where one exists and otherwise by one throttled live lookup per business; a card goes only if the latest daily check showed Active; one live lookup at the pick; Confirm re-checks the stored results (latest daily check, the pick lookup, end and bond dates, account status) with no second live lookup; if the pick lookup fails, a daily result under 24 hours old is used (as LIC-008), otherwise the pick waits and retries until the confirm deadline. Reason: the changes file wins on method; the v9.3 12-hour rule would force a live lookup for almost every card.
- **D-057** Question: the license line is written only for the ROC (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:192`), but pest, alarm, inspection and septic cards also show a license (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:203`). Choice: "<Agency> license #___ showed Active on the <agency> website when we checked on [date]. Check it yourself." Reason: says exactly what was checked (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:194`).
- **D-058** Question: v9.3 lets the sub-licenses R-39R (air conditioning only) and R-37R (drains only) receive only their own job types (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:136`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:137`); the catalog gives `hvac_repair` R-39R although that row includes furnace repair (`docs/catalog/categories.yaml:66`), and gives `plumbing_repair` no R-37R (`docs/catalog/categories.yaml:48`). Choice: license coverage is judged per job type (CARD-001): R-39R gets only air-conditioning job types and R-37R only drain and sewer job types. The catalog is not a K3 file; the next task that owns it adds per-job-type scope (R-37R to drain clearing, R-39R limited to cooling) before any routing code is written. Reason: v9.3's table is the only source; sending a furnace job to an AC-only license would be outside the license.
- **D-059** Question: the identity chain sends codes to the contacts "on the state record" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:428`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:93`), but no-license businesses have only the city license list (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:37`). Choice: every identity code or letter goes to a contact point from a public record, never one the business typed: the state record for licensed businesses, and for no-license businesses the phone, email or address on Lake Havasu City's list (the only list used at launch). If the list has only an address, the mailed letter is the identity step; with no usable record contact, the sign-up waits in "needs review". Reason: legally safest; a typed-in phone proves nothing about identity.
- **D-060** Question: does every sign-up wait for Casey's approval? v9.3 has Casey review sign-ups with a checklist and gives the owner approval rights (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:617`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:375`), and the changes file assumes owner approval exists while saying it never activates on its own (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:115`). Choice: every business needs all three: Casey's approval in the owner portal, the completed identity chain, and the license or city-list match. Flagged sign-ups (record mismatch, the letter path, fraud signals) are marked as such in his queue. Reason: no source drops the owner review, so v9.3 stands where the changes file only adds conditions.
- **D-061** Question: v9.3 tracks insurance expiry and the twin pauses businesses on it (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:90`, `sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:1578`); the changes file shows insurance yes/no, business-reported and not required (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:64`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:190`). Choice: a yes/no the business reports, shown on no-license cards only; never verified, tracked for expiry or used to pause. Reason: the changes file wins; checking it would imply a verification the cards don't claim.
- **D-062** Question: what happens when a business texts STOP? Cards go only to "opted-in" businesses (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`). Choice: the business is paused with the reason "texts stopped" and emailed how to resume (text START, then unpause in the portal). Reason: legally safest; no texts after an opt-out (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:158`).
- **D-063** Question: every text kind has a Spanish template (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:416`), but nothing sets a business's text language. Choice: business texts are English unless the business picks Spanish in its portal settings. Reason: makes every Spanish business template reachable (no unused code, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:488`) with one setting.
- **D-064** Question: the service voice is "Ask Hava: …" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:28`), but no rule says every text names the brand. Choice: every text the app composes, English and Spanish, begins "Ask Hava:"; Twilio Verify codes, whose wording Twilio sets, use the Verify service friendly name "Ask Hava". Reason: legally safest; carriers expect the sender named, and v9.3's first-text rule already names the brand (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:257`).
- **D-065** Question: which language is the first consent text in, before any language is known? Choice: Spanish when the first message reads as Spanish (keyword rules, plus the AI where allowed), otherwise English. Reason: v9.3 gives Spanish speakers every text in Spanish (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:266`); smallest scope.
- **D-066** Question: Spanish opt-out words are honored (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:114`), but HELP is English only (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:259`). Choice: a text that is only AYUDA gets the HELP reply in Spanish. Reason: legally safest; matches the Spanish opt-out rule.
- **D-067** Question: Spanish templates are drafts for native-speaker review (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:702`); is that a build stop? Choice: no; it is a Gate C item in Part 2 of the account checklist, as the build plan lists it (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:239`). Reason: not one of the §16 stops (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:546`).
- **D-068** Question: "2 YES" by text (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`) when the homeowner has several jobs with quotes, and with no contact-method choice (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:408`). Choice: a text pick works when exactly one job is in Quotes ready; with more than one, Ask Hava first asks which job (numbered, as for CLOSE). A text pick uses the masked call unless the homeowner changes it on the quote page before the business confirms. The page's review step (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:406`) applies to page picks; the reply to a text pick says what happens next. Reason: mirrors "Close which?" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:112`); smallest scope.
- **D-069** Question: what counts as "repeat work from the same customer" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:69`), given contact details are purged 180 days after a job closes (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:354`)? Choice: $0 when the same homeowner record already had a confirmed introduction (paid, free win or repeat) to the same business account, at any property, until the 180-day contact purge (PRIV-007); no hash of the phone is kept after a purge to extend it; a repeat pick never uses up a free first win; Casey can refund a missed repeat through the owner portal. Reason: privacy beats a longer match; "always free" holds for as long as Ask Hava can know.
- **D-070** Question: "quotes keep landing just under $500 or $1,500" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:62`) has no numbers. Choice: flag (no automatic action) a business when at least 3 of its last 10 quotes have a fee basis within 5% under a tier edge ($475.00–$499.99 or $1,425.00–$1,499.99); both numbers are settings. Reason: smallest concrete rule; a flag only, so easy to tune.
- **D-071** Question: how is a "final bill well above the quote" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:62`) detected? Choice: every day-3 PROBLEM reply is flagged to Casey with its text; no automatic amount comparison and no fee change. Reason: the fee is never adjusted for the final bill (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:238`); smallest scope.
- **D-072** Question: the flat-fee switch (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:63`) names no amounts, and the card authorization quotes the tiers (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:212`). Choice: in flat mode each job type's fee defaults to its category's set trip-fee tier and is editable; after any fee-mode change, a business must accept a new authorization version before its next charge and can't confirm until it does. Reason: legally safest: no charge under terms the business never agreed to.
- **D-073** Question: v9.3 lets businesses save a bank account (ACH) (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:64`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:326`); the changes file and brief speak only of cards (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:212`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:363`). Choice: cards only at launch. Reason: bank debits settle over days, which breaks the 1-business-hour payment window and release-after-payment; smallest scope.
- **D-074** Question: the brief gives the owner portal "release" power (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:316`) but no contact detail may leave the vault except on a confirmed, paid pick (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:151`). Choice: the owner's release only completes or re-sends a release for a pick already confirmed and paid, or a recorded free win; it can never release otherwise. Reason: keeps the vault invariant; both lines are the brief's.
- **D-075** Question: safe mode starts when a "High" alert goes unacknowledged for 24 hours (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:375`), but no list says which alerts are High. Choice: High means the OPS-004 alerts (delivery under 95%, number suspended, Stripe or webhook errors over 30 minutes, app down, ledger difference) and any critical alert (for example a possible emergency from an opted-out number); complaints, disputes and other alerts don't start the safe-mode clock. Reason: v9.3's alert table (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:457`–`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:464`); pausing every charge over one unanswered complaint would be out of proportion.
- **D-076** Question: alerts reach Casey by text (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:68`); do quiet hours hold them? Choice: High and critical alerts go to Casey at any hour, by text and email; other alerts go by email and the owner portal. Reason: an urgent alert is urgent (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`).
- **D-077** Question: masked numbers come "from a pool per active job" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:110`) with no size, and v9.3 assumed one number (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:60`). Choice: a fixed pool sized by Casey in settings; the app never buys numbers itself; a job gets a number no other active job of the same homeowner or business is using when one is free, and otherwise shares one with PIN routing (CALL-004) and an alert to Casey to add numbers. Reason: no purchases without Casey's go (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:61`); smallest scope.
- **D-078** Question: v9.3 texts "Reply LINK 4321 to add this phone" after a blocked call (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:216`), but an unlinked staff phone never signed up and may not be texted (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:258`). Choice: the blocked caller hears the PIN prompt; the LINK text goes to the business's signed-up main phone and names the last 4 digits of the blocked number. Reason: legally safest; only consenting numbers are texted.
- **D-079** Question: contact details are purged 180 days after a job closes (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:354`), but consent and opt-out logs are kept 5 years after the last message to a number (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:356`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:183`) and an opt-out must keep being honored. Choice: at the purge, name, address, email and photos go; the phone number stays, encrypted in the vault, only with its consent and opt-out records for the consent-log period, and is used only to honor opt-outs and prove consent. The privacy policy says so (TERMS-020), as part of "what is collected" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:214`). Reason: legally safest; without the number the opt-out and consent records prove nothing.
- **D-080** Question: until zero retention is signed, v9.3 stops only the AI jobs that see homeowner details (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:298`), while the changes file and brief send the app's AI only to the zero-retention organization (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:58`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:307`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:112`). Choice: while the live app handles real requests without a signed zero-retention agreement, it makes no AI call of any kind (quote reading, the business-intent second opinion and the weekly summary included); businesses quote through the structured quote form and intake uses keyword rules and homeowner questions. The Phase 2 test of the real AI from the Railway test project with fake data only (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:194`, D-040) is allowed, since no homeowner detail exists there. Reason: the changes file wins; one switch is simpler than a per-job rule.
- **D-081** Question: how do handymen "certify each quote" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:241`), and what is "overlapping scope" in the split-job check (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:208`)? Choice: handyman quotes go through the portal quote form with a required certification box (a text quote from a handyman gets the form link); overlapping scope means the same category or a shared job type at the same property within 30 days (D-027). Reason: a stored, versioned certification per quote; the broader overlap test is legally safer.
- **D-082** Question: "Moves leaving Arizona are refused" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:206`); what about moves into Arizona? Choice: any move that starts or ends outside Arizona is refused. Reason: the stated reason is interstate broker rules, which apply in both directions; legally safest.
- **D-083** Question: v9.3 sends a review request after Done (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:177`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:263`); public reviews would rank businesses (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:454`). Choice: the request asks for private feedback to Ask Hava only, never shown publicly or on quote cards and never used to order quotes. Reason: no rankings, and "every eligible quote shown, in the order received" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:189`).
- **D-084** Question: may the quote page sort or filter quotes, and what happens to a quote whose business becomes ineligible? Choice: arrival order only, with no sorting or filtering; such a quote shows "no longer available" and can't be chosen; numbers never change. Reason: arrival order keeps "1 YES" stable (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:123`); eligibility is re-checked at the pick (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:222`).
- **D-085** Question: v9.3 gives businesses their own "quiet times" setting (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:396`); does the app-wide window (D-003) replace it? Choice: kept: a business may set its own quiet times, which can only narrow the app-wide 8 a.m.–8 p.m. window, never widen it. Reason: v9.3 governs where the changes file is silent, and a stricter per-business window can't break the changes file's quiet-hours rule (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`).
- **D-086** Question: "out of area" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:170`) has no defined area. Choice: the service area is a towns list in settings, starting with Lake Havasu City; requests from other towns close early. Reason: the campaign describes a Lake Havasu City service (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:179`); adding a town is a settings change.
- **D-087** Question: Google Search Console "stays verified" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:26`) while old DNS records are removed (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:137`). Choice: the DNS records document keeps any existing google-site-verification record, and the new site carries a verification tag if one is used. Reason: the changes file requires the verification to survive.
- **D-088** Question: v9.3 registers as "Customer Care / Mixed", asks Twilio support first, and uses its own privacy clause (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:61`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:243`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:253`). Choice: Customer Care, Low-Volume Standard, no pre-clearance request, and the changes file's privacy sentence (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:160`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:179`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:180`). Reason: the changes file wins.
- **D-089** Question: v9.3 asks for a card only at the second win, after the free first one (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:326`); a business joining after the first 90 days has no free win (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:67`). Choice: no card at sign-up; a business with no usable free win saves a card, accepting the card authorization (stored with version and time), before or when it taps Confirm, with the usual 1 business hour (FEE-020). Reason: keeps v9.3's no-card sign-up; the authorization is accepted where the card is saved (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:212`).

**Spec changes from K3** (each changed line names its decision): `docs/spec/quotes-pick.md` PICK-001, PICK-008, PICK-020, PICK-026 changed, PICK-029 to PICK-031 added; `job-cards.md` CARD-015 changed, CARD-018 added; `lifecycle.md` JOB-016, JOB-017 changed, JOB-025, JOB-026 added; `fees.md` FEE-007, FEE-013, FEE-014, FEE-022, FEE-023, FEE-033 changed, FEE-035, FEE-036 added; `consent.md` CON-004, CON-015, CON-018 changed, CON-025, CON-026 added; `quiet-hours.md` QH-004 changed; `licensing.md` LIC-011, LIC-017, LIC-023 changed, LIC-034 to LIC-036 added; `businesses.md` BIZ-002, BIZ-008, BIZ-010 changed, BIZ-016 to BIZ-018 added; `intake.md` INT-022 changed, INT-024 added; `calls.md` CALL-011 changed, CALL-017 added; `security.md` SEC-006; `privacy.md` PRIV-007; `ai.md` AI-008; `ops-recovery.md` OPS-001; `wording.md` WORD-008; `terms.md` TERMS-018 to TERMS-020 added (TERMS-017 was drafted on this branch only, moved to `website.md` as WEB-017 and never merged); `website.md` WEB-016, WEB-017 added; `quiet-hours.md` QH-005; `businesses.md` BIZ-010 again; `calls.md` CALL-018 added. D-038 to D-040 change no spec line (process only).

**Follow-ups for K6 to queue** (files K3 does not own; each needs its own task):

1. `docs/catalog/categories.yaml`: add per-job-type license scope so R-37R receives only drain and sewer job types (add it to `plumbing_repair` for drain clearing) and R-39R receives only air-conditioning job types in `hvac_repair` and `hvac_replacement` (not furnace repair), with `scripts/check_catalog.py` checking it; must land before any routing code (D-058, LIC-035).
2. `docs/sources.md:29`: replace "stop and ask Casey" with the §16 rule, decide and log (D-038).

## K4 (architecture, threat model and stack versions), October 27, 2026

K4 finalizes the module boundaries fixed by `BUILD_BRIEF.md:124`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:152` in `docs/architecture.md` and writes `docs/threat-model.md`. Each item below is a *how* choice the brief leaves open, decided by the §16 rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`).

- **D-090** Question: the brief's module list (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:128`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:146`) has no owner for the shared job table and timers (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:108`), settings, the database session, derived IDs or rate limits, which every module needs. Choice: a shared kernel package `core` that is not a business module and owns only `core.task` (task queue and timers) and `core.rate_limit`; everything else stays in the brief's modules. Reason: putting these in any business module would make every other module depend on it and create cycles; the module boundaries themselves are unchanged.
- **D-091** Question: how is "no module reaches into another's tables" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:126`) enforced, and how does a lower module tell a higher one something (for example Stripe's payment result reaching `picks`)? Choice: one Postgres schema per module; modules import only lower layers' `api.py` (layers in `docs/architecture.md` section 4); an import-and-schema test built on Python's `ast` (no new library) fails on cross-schema foreign keys, imports of another module's internals and upward imports; upward signals are `core.task` events carrying IDs only, with handlers registered in `app/main.py`. Reason: makes the boundary checkable by a test in `Ci`; events keep the import graph one-way.
- **D-092** Question: how are append-only records (ledger, audit log, consent and opt-out events; `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:94`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:96`) protected from edits? Choice: two database roles: a migration role that owns the schemas and runs Alembic, and an app role used by `web` and `worker` with no `UPDATE` or `DELETE` on append-only tables. Reason: an application bug or injection can't rewrite money or audit history (threat T6); smallest change that adds a database-level guard.
- **D-093** Question: PRIV-004 lets only "the messaging and release code" read the vault (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:85`), but masked calls must dial the homeowner (CALL-002), the homeowner portal shows the homeowner their own data (PRIV-021), Casey's call-back alerts need a number to call (INT-023, CON-016), and raw homeowner text may contain names and addresses while intake must match emergency words on every message (INT-002). Choice: (1) the vault holds homeowner message text only (texts and web-chat lines, both ways, in `privacy.message_body`); business text is not vault data, and a business's quote stays in `quotes.quote` as its original wording (PICK-003, PICK-007). (2) Raw homeowner text is seen only by `privacy.api.read_inbound()`, called by `messaging` after dedupe: it stores the text, stores contact details the person gave in the contact record, matches the emergency stems and the sole-word opt-out, HELP and START words (lists passed in by `messaging` from `catalog`), and returns flags plus `ScrubbedText`; `intake` works only from those. Order: dedupe, emergency check, then opt-out, HELP and START; an emergency from an opted-out number still raises the critical alert (CON-017). `messaging` tells business phones from homeowners by a blind-indexed sender directory that `businesses` keeps current. (3) Plaintext vault reads only through `privacy.api`, each audit-logged with its purpose, by: `messaging` (sending, and handing raw inbound text to the reader); `picks` (the release, including the email the homeowner chose to share, PICK-018; the business portal sees released details only through `picks.api`); `calls` (bridging a call; the number goes straight to the voice adapter); `portals` homeowner realm (the signed-in homeowner's own record); `portals` owner realm (the contact on a call-back alert only; `ops.alert` stores the contact ID, never the number, PRIV-006); and `privacy` itself (reader, scrub, purge). (4) At 30 days an outbound text keeps its template ID; an inbound text, having none, keeps only its kind, time, sender reference and job ID, and both its raw text and its scrubbed copy are deleted (TXT-020). (5) Fields are AES-256-GCM with the table, column and row as associated data and a key version; phone lookup uses an HMAC blind index under a separate key. Reason: keeps PRIV-001's "only in the vault" true for free text, keeps raw text out of `intake`, and keeps the reader list as narrow as the features allow.
- **D-094** Question: where do photos, the off-database records (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:690`) and the nightly encrypted export "to separate storage" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:469`) live? The sources name no storage vendor. Choice: photos are stored encrypted in the `privacy` schema in PostgreSQL; the off-database stream and the nightly export go to an object-storage bucket behind a `storage` adapter (stand-in: a local directory). The provider is chosen at the account checkpoint and listed in `docs/account-checklist.md`, with Cloudflare R2 as the default because askhava.com's DNS is already on Cloudflare (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:25`); separate buckets and keys for test and live. The task that builds the adapter logs its client library as its own new-library entry. Reason: photos in the database need no extra vendor and are covered by backups and purge; the export and stream must survive loss of the database host, so they can't live there.
- **D-095** Question: INT-019 closes a job left in Intake at 72 hours with a reminder at 24 hours (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`) without naming the status, but a number whose consent is still pending may receive nothing except the consent text (CON-025); and is "Not covered" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:160`) a waiting state? Choice: the 72-hour Intake close moves the job to Closed early with the reason "no reply". When consent was recorded, the 24-hour reminder and the closing text "Text us any time to restart" go out as INT-019 says; when consent is still pending, no reminder and no closing text are sent, the job closes silently at 72 hours, and a web-chat visitor who comes back just sees the request closed. Not covered is a final status, like Done, Closed, Closed early and Cancelled. Reason: v9.3 moves Intake only to Open or Closed early (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:170`); consent-first wins over a reminder to a number that never agreed (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:113`); the homeowner is told at once when no business covers the job (INT-017), so nothing waits in Not covered.
- **D-096** Question: which exact versions does K4 pin (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:121`)? Choice: the newest stable release published on or before October 13, 2026 (14 days before K4), except when pip-audit reports a known vulnerability fixed only in a later release, which is then pinned. Under this rule pillow is 13.0.0 (pip-audit 2.10.1 found 38 known vulnerabilities in 12.3.0, all fixed in 13.0.0), and PyYAML moves from D-036's 6.0.2 to 6.0.3 (same API). The pinned list is `docs/architecture.md` section 11. Reason: a short delay lets bad releases be yanked or flagged before they reach the lock file (threat T10, R10); a security fix outranks the delay.
- **D-097** New libraries beyond `BUILD_BRIEF.md:103`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:118` (purpose; license from PyPI or npm metadata): **uvicorn 0.54.0** (ASGI server that runs FastAPI; BSD-3-Clause); **psycopg[binary] 3.3.6** (PostgreSQL driver for SQLAlchemy; LGPL-3.0-only, used unmodified as a library); **pydantic-settings 2.15.0** (startup-validated settings, SEC-002; MIT); **resend 2.49.1** (Resend SDK for the email adapter; MIT); **lob-python 5.2.1** (Lob SDK for the letters adapter; MIT; the older `lob` package was last released in 2022); **cryptography 50.0.2** (AES-GCM vault and HMAC, PRIV-002; Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause); **argon2-cffi 25.1.0** (owner password hashing, SEC-005; MIT); **webauthn 3.0.1** (owner passkeys, D-098; BSD-3-Clause); **pyotp 2.10.0** (owner TOTP, D-098; MIT); **python-multipart 0.0.32** (form and photo uploads in FastAPI; Apache-2.0); **pillow 13.0.0** (photo checks and re-encoding without EXIF and GPS, PRIV-014; MIT-CMU); **tzdata 2026.5** (time-zone data for quiet hours and Arizona time regardless of the base image; Apache-2.0); development only: **pytest-cov 7.1.0** with **coverage 7.16.2** (the coverage rule, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:481`; MIT, Apache-2.0); **pytest-playwright 0.10.0** (Playwright fixtures for pytest; Apache-2.0); **httpx 0.28.1** (needed by FastAPI's test client; BSD-3-Clause); **types-PyYAML 6.0.12.20260906** (type stubs for `mypy --strict`; Apache-2.0); **axe-core 4.14.0** (JavaScript file injected into pages by Playwright, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:115`; MPL-2.0). pip-audit result: `pip-audit 2.10.1 --no-deps --disable-pip` on the full 118-package set that `uv pip compile` resolved for Python 3.12 from every pin in `docs/architecture.md` section 11, run in this session on October 27, 2026: "No known vulnerabilities found" (after the pillow change in D-096). axe-core is an npm package and pip-audit does not cover it; no npm audit was run. Reason: each fills a need the brief states and its stack table does not name.
- **D-098** Question: the owner's second factor is "a passkey or TOTP", with at least two registered (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:316`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:317`). Choice: support both; Casey may register any mix, at least two in total, plus offline recovery codes. Reason: two passkeys on one phone ecosystem can fail together, while a TOTP in a password manager survives a lost phone; passkeys resist phishing.
- **D-099** Question: which ASVS version is "ASVS Level 2" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:293`), and where does the brief's design fall short of it? Choice: ASVS 5.0.0 (May 2025), mapped at section level in `docs/threat-model.md`. All cookies use the `__Host-` prefix (ASVS 3.3.3), alongside `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` (SEC-009). Three accepted deviations, kept as residual risks R1, R2 and R11: homeowners use SMS codes with no stronger method offered (ASVS 6.6.1; PICK-013, SEC-008); the business portal on a known device needs only the emailed link (ASVS 6.3.3; SEC-004); Railway variables are the secret store (ASVS 13.3.1; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:62`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:65`). Reason: 5.0.0 is the current release; the brief decides how sign-in and secrets work (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:7`), so the gaps are recorded rather than redesigned.
- **D-100** Question: a phone number typed into web chat or business sign-up makes Ask Hava text or call it (CON-025, BIZ-002), which attackers use for SMS pumping to costly destinations; the sources set no limit. Choice: outbound texts and calls go only to numbers whose area code is in the area-code table used for quiet hours (D-025), and that table covers only the US (the 50 states, DC and US territories such as 787, 939, 340, 671, 670 and 684) and Canada (all provinces, including Newfoundland's 709 at UTC-3:30). Other North American Numbering Plan codes are excluded, for example 876 (Jamaica), 809, 829 and 849 (Dominican Republic), 284 (British Virgin Islands), 242 (Bahamas) and 268 (Antigua), as is every non-+1 number; such a number is refused with a message to use email. Twilio's geographic permissions are set to the US and Canada in the account checklist. Reason: the non-US, non-Canadian +1 codes are where SMS-pumping fraud concentrates, and the service area is Lake Havasu City (D-086); Canadian numbers stay because many Lake Havasu City visitors use them. Texts to Canadian numbers also bring Canada's anti-spam law (CASL) into scope; the consent-first flow is the same, and CASL is added to the items the terms task lists for attorney review (research-based judgment, not legal advice, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:233`).

## K5 (test design; the twin made hard-failing), October 27, 2026

K5 writes `docs/test-design.md` and `docs/twin-warnings.md`. The twin was run from a scratch copy outside the repository with its own simulated data only; every count below comes from those runs, which reproduced the committed `sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/results/console.txt` exactly.

- **D-101** Question: D-001 promised Given/When/Then cases per requirement ID in K5, while stress-test F17 warns that one test per requirement is easy to satisfy with trivial tests (`sources/04_reviews/ChatGPT_Build_StressTest_Report.md:76`) and the Kit Plan asks for tests per behavior, edge and failure (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`). Choice: cases are written per behavior, boundary, failure or invariant (TD entries), each naming every requirement ID it covers, and every ID in `docs/spec/` appears in at least one entry; tests carry `td` and `spec` markers; Hypothesis runs derandomized in `Ci` and randomized nightly, with failing examples pinned. Boundaries the spec leaves open are fixed as: a time window "from 8 a.m. to 8 p.m." includes 08:00:00 and excludes 20:00:00; every "within N" or "first N" duration (minutes, hours or days) means less than N from the starting instant, so something that lasts N expires at exactly N (a 10-minute code is refused at 10:00, a 24-hour quote window refuses a quote at 24:00:00, a 15-minute direct-reply window is closed at 15:00). Reason: the Kit Plan outranks D-001's wording on what K5 produces; half-open windows never send at 8 p.m. (legally safest, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`) and give every edge one testable answer.
- **D-102** Question: the twin's six "qualified business left out" warnings (`normal_fixed`, seed 1; `sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`). Choice: **resolve.** All six have one cause: after the homeowner answers the trade question, the twin's `_job_types_for` drops the plain-keyword job type and stores `<trade>_other` (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:722`–`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:726`), so AC-only licenses and businesses that didn't tick "other" miss the card. The build keeps the chosen trade's plain-keyword job types after a trade answer; `<trade>_other` is used only when no reading or keyword gives a job type of that trade, and then "Which fits best?" lists that trade's job types. Observed in a scratch copy (not a committed test): the six warnings and the whole cause disappeared; the stage 1 twin task proves it through the gate. Reason: plain keyword rules are an independent second opinion on the job type (AI-011; `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:119`) and every qualified business gets the card (CARD-001).
- **D-103** Question: two more intake gaps the twin exposed (causes C2b, C2c): the trade re-ask "Sorry, which one? Reply 1 or 2." doesn't repeat the options (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:511`), and word answers are matched by substring, so "just a small repair" reads as heating/AC through "rep-air" (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:507`). Choice: every re-ask repeats the numbered options; word answers match whole words against the trade's keywords and the words of the option labels the question offered; after two unusable answers the fallback is the keyword trade if exactly one trade matched, otherwise the first reading, and the job keeps the license-boundary keyword job types (D-105) so the fallback can't route outside a license. Reason: INT-012 accepts word answers and INT-015 asks "once more", which means the same question; observed in a scratch copy for C2b and C2c (not a committed test; the stage 1 twin task proves it through the gate).
- **D-104** Question: when the two AI readings name different trades and the keyword trade is a third, the twin offers only the two readings (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/app.py:591`), so the right trade can't be chosen (cause C2d). Choice: the trade question offers every distinct trade from the two readings and the keyword rules, up to three, numbered. Reason: INT-013 gives "1) plumbing or 2) heating/AC" as an example, not a limit; offering the keyword trade is what makes the keyword an independent opinion (AI-011).
- **D-105** Question: when both readings agree on a wrong trade and the keyword trade check also matches that trade, no question is asked ("Water heater not getting hot" matches plumbing and heating/AC), and the job goes to the wrong license (cause C2e). Choice: job types whose keywords mark a license boundary (the catalog's per-job-type keyword lists, LIC-001) stay candidates whatever trade is read or answered; when the candidates then fall under different licenses, "Which fits best?" is asked (INT-014). Reason: legally safest; CARD-001 already requires the license to cover every candidate, so a licensed job named in the homeowner's own words can't reach a license that doesn't cover it on a reading or a single answer.
- **D-106** Question: some warnings come from the simulated homeowner deliberately answering a question wrongly (about 5% of trade answers, `sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/twin.py:258`, and 10% of job-type answers, `sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/twin.py:250`; causes C2a, C3). Choice: **document**: the homeowner's own answer decides, and the Twin gate waives such an instance only with per-instance evidence (the simulated homeowner recorded the wrong answer and the stored job type is the one it sent) and under a per-scenario ceiling. Reason: the homeowner is asked because they know their job (INT-013, INT-014); the app can't know the truth the simulator wrote, and after D-105 one wrong answer can't route a keyword-named licensed job outside its license.
- **D-107** Question: what makes the `Twin` check fail (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:475`; F03, `sources/04_reviews/ChatGPT_Build_StressTest_Report.md:62`)? Choice: `twin/gate.py` (built by K8) runs every `_fixed` scenario with seeds 1–5 and fails on any instance of any check not covered by an entry in `twin/waivers.json` that names a decision here. Two waiver kinds: **pinned** (one exact instance, for failures explained in `docs/twin-warnings.md` and fixed by the stage 1 twin task, which must end with none left) and **class** (only D-106's evidence predicate, with ceilings). A stale pinned entry, an unknown key or a missing decision also fails. The v9.1 scenarios (names without `_fixed`, which run a superseded design) are **rejected** as gate inputs and kept as a negative control that must report matching failures. The gate checks licensing against its own license table, never one imported from the code under test: in a scratch copy (an observation the gate's self-tests turn into a test), giving the AC-only sub-license `heating_repair` sent 81 heating cards to AC-only licenses while the twin's outside-license count moved only from 2.8 to 3.4 per run and flagged none of them, because `twin.py` imports `CLASS_ALLOWS` from `app.py` (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/twin.py:853`, F07). The gate's license table is `twin/oracle/license_scope.json`, written from v9.3's table (not copied from `CLASS_ALLOWS`) and checked in the gate's self-tests against `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml` (D-110). The whole of `twin/` (checks, the simulated people's wrong-answer evidence, gate, waivers, license table) becomes a protected path, so the stage 1 twin task is a `harness` task with an Opus review; the gate also keeps a frozen list of required check keys and exits 2 if one is missing, and refuses to run unless `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`. Class-waiver ceilings are integers per scenario and seed, set by K8 and re-measured in stage 1. **Deferred:** the Kit Plan makes the twin hard-failing a K5 deliverable (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`), but the files that do it (`.github/workflows/twin.yml`, the runner's protected-path list, `twin/`) are protected and change only in a `harness` task (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`), so K5 specifies the change (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 6.3) and K8 builds it; until then the `Twin` check stays the informational smoke run. Reason: makes "any failed check makes the run fail" true without hiding known failures behind a blanket ignore; determinism (identical output under different hash seeds) makes exact-instance pins reliable.
- **D-108** Question: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:472` puts mutmut on `payments`, `catalog` and `licensing`, but consent, opt-out and quiet-hours rules are as easy to break and as costly. Choice: two mechanisms: named mutants (`tests/mutation/mutants.toml`, protected; each must turn named tests red, checked on every PR touching its file and nightly) and nightly mutmut over `app/payments/rules.py`, `app/catalog/rules.py`, `app/licensing/rules.py`, `app/quotes/rules.py`, `app/messaging/rules.py` and `app/intake/rules.py`; every survivor is killed by a new test or listed as equivalent with a reason after an Opus review. Reason: the brief's list is a minimum; named mutants give a fast, deterministic per-PR check that mutmut's full run can't.
- **D-109** Question: what fake data do fixtures use (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:66`; PRIV-019), given the PII scanner fails the build on real-looking phones (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:37`) and the twin generates real-looking ones such as `+1928700` plus four digits (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/twin.py:411`)? Choice: one fixture generator: phones in the fictional `555-0100` to `555-0199` block under area codes in the quiet-hours table (for example `+1 928 555 0142`), `example.com` emails, streets like "1 Test Way", names from a fixed fake list, plus fixed sentinel values used to prove nothing leaks (TD-PRIV-02). The PII scanner allowlists the twin's generator patterns explicitly, never the `twin/` path. Reason: `555-01xx` numbers are reserved for fiction in every area code, so they pass area-code rules without being anyone's number.
- **D-110** Question: the fee rules have worked examples the app didn't write (`docs/catalog/worked_examples.yaml`), but matching has none, so the app and the twin could share one licensing mistake (F07, `sources/04_reviews/ChatGPT_Build_StressTest_Report.md:66`; `sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:35`). Choice: an Opus task writes `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml` (at least M01 to M16 in `docs/test-design.md` section 7.2), checked by `scripts/check_catalog.py`, before any routing code; the app's matching tests and the Twin gate's license table are both checked against it. Reason: an oracle written before and apart from both implementations is the only guard against a shared mistake.

**Follow-ups for K6 to queue** (files K5 does not own):
