1. K8 harness: the Twin gate, `twin/` copy, waivers file, workflow change and gate self-tests (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 6.3, D-107); the named-mutant runner and mutmut nightly (D-108); `tests/coverage_floor.json`; the test control router guard; the PII scanner allowlist (D-109).
2. Before any routing code: `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml` with its `scripts/check_catalog.py` check (D-110), alongside K3 follow-up 1 (per-job-type license scope).
3. Stage 1 twin task, typed `harness` with an Opus review because `twin/` is protected: fix causes C1 to C2e (D-102 to D-105) in the twin, triage the items in `docs/twin-warnings.md` section 7, re-measure the class-waiver ceilings, and pass `gate.py --no-pinned`.
4. A spec task may add D-102 to D-105 to INT-012 to INT-015 so the spec lines name them; the rules already follow from AI-011, INT-013 to INT-015 and CARD-001, so no requirement changes meaning.

## K6 (task graph), October 27, 2026

K6 writes `kit/tasks.json` and its validator `scripts/validate_tasks.py` (tests in `tests/test_validate_tasks.py`). Each item below is a *how* choice the brief and the Kit Plan leave open, decided by the §16 rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`).

- **D-111** Question: what does each task record, and what does "a script checks it" mean (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:35`)? Choice: each task in `kit/tasks.json` has an ID `T###`, slug, title, goal, type (`build`, `harness`, `docs`, `cleanup`, `audit` or `gate`), phase and stage, module, dependencies, worker model and effort, reviewer model, review areas, owned paths, files it reads, requirement IDs, test-design entries (TD-…) and journeys (J1–J4) it must make pass, decisions and source lines it rests on, a size budget, the required checks (`Ci`, `Twin`, `Ally`), its branch `claude/task/T###-<slug>` and its prompt `kit/prompts/T###.md`. The validator fails on: a malformed task; an unknown or self dependency; a cycle; any requirement in `docs/spec/` (read through `scripts/trace_spec.py`), TD entry in `docs/test-design.md` or journey left uncovered, or an unknown one named; an owned path under `sources/` (always), or under a protected path unless the task is `harness`; two tasks that can run in either order owning the same path; a test file taken over by a later non-harness task; a budget over the limits in D-119; a model or reviewer that is not a full model ID; a money, privacy, security, recovery or harness task without an Opus reviewer; high effort on a non-Opus task; Haiku on money, privacy, security or recovery work; a read that doesn't exist and isn't made by an earlier task or the kit (K7 to K9); a decision missing from this file or a citation that isn't a non-blank repository line; a missing, doubled or misplaced owner gate, a gate whose waiting or conditional flag differs from D-116, or a dependency on a gate that nothing may wait on (D-116); a dependency on a later stage; review areas that leave out an area implied by the owned paths (`app/payments/` money; `app/privacy/` and `app/picks/` privacy; `app/portals/`, `app/core/` and `app/adapters/` security; `app/recovery/` recovery; a directory implies everything in it); a directory owned by anything but a `cleanup` or `harness` task, or without an Opus reviewer; and a budget file count that differs from the owned files (for a task owning a directory, the runner's path check holds the diff to `budget.files`). Protected paths (which include this validator, `scripts/trace_spec.py` and their tests), shared paths, the kit's outputs (paths K7 to K9 create, which the file must list exactly), gate flags and limits are constants in the script, so the task file can't loosen them; a kit-output entry without a trailing slash matches only that exact path. Reason: the Kit Plan's checks (no cycles, every requirement and test covered, no two parallel tasks own a file) plus the brief's rules a runner must be able to trust (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:249`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:485`); standard library only, so no new library.
- **D-112** Question: the build plan puts license checks and the three portals in stage 4 (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:235`), but stage 2's job cards can't be sent without a license status (LIC-008, CARD-001) or business records (towns, ticks, pause). Choice: stage 2 also builds the daily list imports and the one lookup at the pick (`licensing-imports`), the business records that matching reads (`businesses-records`), the portals' security base and the quote page; stage 4 keeps sign-up and identity, the business, owner and homeowner portals and calls. The pick's Confirm window comes in stage 3, after the charge it starts. The twin is linked to the app's rules at the end of stage 3, once fees, matching, quiet hours and the lifecycle exist. Reason: dependencies decide order; each stage still ends with what the build plan says it delivers.
- **D-113** Question: the task that writes a rule writes its named mutants (`docs/test-design.md` section 4), but `tests/mutation/` is protected (D-108). Choice: named mutants and coverage floors are added by one `harness` task per stage that has mutation targets (`mutants-stage2` for the license filter, consent and opt-out, quiet hours and the privacy floor; `mutants-stage3` for the 4-month rule and fee tiers), each with an Opus review, after the code it mutates. Reason: keeps the protected-path rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`) without leaving any rule unmutated.
- **D-114** Question: Phase 2 fixes "any differences in the adapter and stand-in together" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:191`), and the ZAP scan needs a workflow, but `stand_ins/` and `.github/` are protected. Choice: each vendor-sandbox task and the ZAP task is a `harness` task with an Opus review. Reason: the brief allows protected changes only that way (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`).
- **D-115** Question: which model does each task get? The build plan plans about 10% Opus sessions (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:289`), while the brief says "Opus 5.5: spec, architecture, money, privacy, security, recovery, reviews of those, and the final audit" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:498`). Choice: the brief's line applies as written: every work task whose review areas include money, privacy, security or recovery, declared or implied by its owned paths (D-111), runs on Opus with an Opus reviewer, and the validator fails otherwise. That is 105 of 139 working tasks, including the stage cleanups (which own all of `app/`), the end-to-end journeys (they assert privacy and money invariants) and the Phase 2 adapter tasks (they own `app/adapters/`). Sonnet writes the 31 tasks with none of those areas (templates, quiet hours, intake, licensing card lines, site pages and the like), each with a Sonnet or Opus reviewer; Haiku does the docs fix, the twin chaos run and the Phase 2 journey run (3); test runs inside each task go to Haiku subagents (`docs/test-design.md` section 3). Reason: the brief outranks the build plan on how (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:7`). This uses far more Opus than the plan's 10%; K9 measures usage per model (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:510`), and narrowing the rule (for example letting Sonnet write test-only tasks with an Opus review) needs a logged decision that quotes line 498 and says what it narrows.
- **D-116** Question: how are the owner stops in `BUILD_BRIEF.md:546`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:551` and the "Finished" message represented? Choice: as tasks of type `gate` (no model, no owned paths, no checks): `account-checkpoint` after every Phase 1 task; `stage-0.5-go` after it, with nothing depending on it; `real-texts-casey-phone` after the Twilio sandbox task, marked `conditional` (the runner marks it `SKIPPED` when Twilio test credentials work, and its dependents continue); `finished` after every Phase 2 task (it waits for no one); `gate-b` after `finished`; `gate-c` after `gate-b`; and `first-live-charge` after `gate-c`. Only `finished` has `waits_on_owner` false; only `real-texts-casey-phone` is conditional; nothing depends on `stage-0.5-go` or `first-live-charge`; only `gate-c` depends on `gate-b`; and `real-texts-casey-phone` comes after the task that owns the Twilio messaging adapter (`app/adapters/twilio_sms.py`). A `cleanup-stage6` task (Opus reviewer) runs after the Phase 2 work and before `finished`. Publishing the Stage 0.5 page is not a Claude task: the static page set is built in stage 5 (`site-stage05-dns`), Claude holds no hosting key and the live project deploys only when Casey presses Deploy (OPS-022), so after his go the runner sends one hands-on message with the exact steps, which is the publication. Hands-on stops (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:547`) are raised by the runner when a task needs them and are not tasks. Reason: puts every stop in the graph the runner already reads, so "blocked holds only its dependents" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:234`) applies to gates too.
- **D-117** Question: v9.3's build stages name "build tests T01–T24" (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:673`), but no source defines them. Choice: test coverage is checked against the TD entries of `docs/test-design.md` and the journeys J1–J4, which cover every requirement; task IDs use three digits (`T001`) so they can't be confused with those names. Reason: an undefined list can't be checked; the TD entries are the build's test design (D-101).
- **D-118** Question: every PR updates `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/decisions.md` (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:489`), and `app/main.py` registers every module's routes and handlers (`docs/architecture.md` section 2), so no single task can own them. Choice: these three files and `docs/for-casey-review.md` are shared paths any task may edit, and are left out of the ownership check; nothing else is shared. The kit's own tests (`tests/test_trace_spec.py`, `tests/test_check_catalog.py`) count as protected, so the two catalog follow-up tasks add new test files instead of editing them. Reason: one task runs at a time, so shared edits never collide; everything else keeps a single owner at any moment.
- **D-119** Question: what is a "small" task (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:485`; `sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:35`)? Choice: at most 14 owned paths, a budget of at most 14 files and 800 changed lines, at most 25 requirement IDs and 6 test entries, and a context budget of at most 20% of the window (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:264`), estimated as 6% plus 1% per spec file read plus 1% per 100 budgeted lines. Reason: the build plan's 15–20% context target; the other numbers keep each PR reviewable in one fresh-eyes pass, and K9's measurements can change them by a logged decision.
- **D-120** Question: Gate P (PR #14) inserted a line in `BUILD_BRIEF.md` §16, shifting every later line by one and leaving `validate_tasks.py` with a citation on a blank line (T143). Choice: add one to every `BUILD_BRIEF.md:5xx` citation from line 544 on in `kit/tasks.json` and regenerate the prompts. Reason: the tasks must cite the lines they mean. Source: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:539`.

## K8 (harness, runner, stand-ins, scanner), October 27, 2026

K8 builds the runner's logic, the stand-ins, the PII scanner and the failure drills as a `harness` task (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`); everything is standard library, so no new library. Left for later K8 follow-ups, which D-107 to D-109 already assign: the Twin gate and waivers, named mutants and the mutmut nightly, `tests/coverage_floor.json`, the test control router guard.

- **D-121** Question: how is the runner tested without GitHub or a cloud session (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:205`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:292`)? Choice: `runner/` is pure Python over the real `kit/state.json` shape (read from `origin/claude/kit-state`, October 27): `schema` 1, `tasks` as a list of objects with `id`, `deps`, `status` (`QUEUED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `DONE`, `SKIPPED`, plus the brief's `MERGE_PENDING`, `BLOCKED`, `RETRY_AFTER_RESET`, `RETRY_LATER`, `WAITING_FOR_OWNER`), `attempts`, one top-level `lease` of `task`, `session`, `expires`, a text `build_hold`, and `owner_answers` and `inbox_processed` for Casey's answers; every other key is kept untouched, and the one key the runner adds is `main_red`. Tasks from `kit/tasks.json` join the list as `QUEUED` (`sync_graph`). A copy with fake values is `tests/fixtures/kit_state_real_shape.json`, and tests read and round-trip it. Git, GitHub and the worker session sit behind the `runner.cycle.Env` protocol. A state push is accepted only if the state it was based on is still the stored one (git's non-fast-forward rule, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:221`; the real `Env` fetches and retries when only inbox and outbox files moved, K0 review N2). Inbox answers are recorded in the state and pushed first, and only then moved to `inbox/done/` through `ack_inbox`, so an early exit on a live lease or a lost push loses none. Claiming needs no live lease and no `MERGE_PENDING` task. `DONE` comes only from `check_done`: merged, `Ci`, `Twin`, `Ally` each green on the head SHA, and squash tree equal to head tree (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:261`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:263`). An unmerged PR with no failed required check stays `MERGE_PENDING`, and checks still pending, even on a merged PR, mean wait. A merged PR with a red check or a different tree is `BLOCKED` and sets `main_red`. A task whose lease expired but which has a PR is checked through that PR, not redone. Failures follow `BUILD_BRIEF.md:272` and `BUILD_BRIEF.md:539`: three attempts, the same model twice and Opus on the third, the failure log carried into the retry; a usage limit is `RETRY_AFTER_RESET` (hourly when no reset time is known) and a vendor outage `RETRY_LATER` (15 minutes doubling to 6 hours), neither counted as a failure; a blocked permission is `BLOCKED` with no attempt counted and no question to Casey. Gate P opens (hold, outbox message, "gate_open") once K9 is `DONE`; a set `build_hold` holds the `T###` tasks even before then, and kit tasks continue (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:538`). Reason: the Kit Plan's drills need the decisions to run offline; the real `Env` is K9's rehearsal.
- **D-122** Question: which model evidence is inspectable (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:247`)? Choice: the `message.model` field of the assistant records in the worker session's transcript (one JSON object per line); every model seen must equal the full ID the task names, `<synthetic>` records are ignored, a run with no records fails, and only IDs in `kit/tasks.json` `models` are accepted. The subagent's own statement is never an input to the check (`check_model` takes none). **Not yet confirmed in the real cloud environment** (no cloud session was available to K8): K9 must confirm that the transcript is readable from the runner session and records the model, and the full-ID format the subagent call accepts, and log the result here; the fallback is the usage records. Reason: the brief makes K8 establish the evidence, and an unverified assumption has to be written down as one.
- **D-123** Question: how do owner gates behave when a task's `resume` text is not an exact phrase (`kit/tasks.json` gates T127 to T146; D-116)? Choice: a gate clears only on the quoted phrase in its `resume` text (found as a quote not preceded by a letter, so "Casey's 'go'" gives `go`); a gate with no quoted phrase clears only on a whole-word "go", "yes", "approved" or "done"; any other answer is recorded and the gate stays. A gate with no `outbox` title gets "Your turn: <kind>"; the `finished` gate (not waiting) writes its message and is `DONE` at once. Gate P clears only on "approved to build" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:538`) and sets `build_hold` until then. Reason: the live-action goes (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:546`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:551`) must never clear on an ambiguous comment.
- **D-124** Question: what do the stand-ins model, and how faithfully? Choice: in-process classes, written from each vendor's public API reference, that return `(status, body)` in the vendor's envelope. Twilio: message resource, errors 21211, 21602, 21606, 21610, 21612, 21614, 21617 and 21408, magic numbers, Twilio's default opt-out words including "cancel" (English only, exact word, so the app owns the Spanish ones), GSM-7 and UCS-2 segment counts, signature (HMAC-SHA1 over URL and sorted parameters), Verify with an attempt limit. Stripe: stripe-mock's shapes and error envelope, minimum 50 cents, declines and 3-D Secure by test payment method, idempotency keys that replay or conflict, refunds capped at the unrefunded amount, events, `Stripe-Signature` with a 300 second tolerance. Resend: id response, 422 and 409 errors, test addresses, Svix signature. Lob: letter object, required address fields, tracking events, `lob-signature`. AI: keyword rules, `ScrubbedText` only (a runtime check, AI-001), tools refused (AI-006), output must be exactly the listed fields with allowed values (AI-005, AI-010). Fake data only: `+1 928 555 01xx`, `example.com`, "1 Test Way". Where my memory of a vendor doc could be off (exact Lob event names, Resend's idempotency error names), the Phase 2 run against the sandbox is the check (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:191`) and fixes the stand-in and adapter together. Reason: the contract tests only mean something if they come from the vendors' documents, not from the fake.
- **D-125** Question: what does the PII scanner catch (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:37`; D-109)? Choice: `scripts/pii_scan.py` scans every tracked or new file outside `sources/` and fails on: a US phone number not in `555-0100` to `555-0199` (or Twilio's `500-555-00xx` magic block); a street address whose street name lacks a fake word (Test, Example, Sample, Fake, Canary, Sentinel, Placeholder); a labelled name field (`first_name`, `homeowner_name` and similar) holding a full name that is not on its fake-name list; and vendor-key shapes (Stripe, AWS, GitHub, Slack, Anthropic, SendGrid, Resend, Twilio SIDs other than all zeros, private-key headers). Findings print as path, line and rule, never the match. It is wired into `ci.yml` as the PII scan step. **Limits, stated plainly:** names are caught only in labelled fields and addresses only in the usual "number, capitalised words, street type" shape, so it is a tripwire next to gitleaks, not proof; the twin's generator patterns (D-109) are allowlisted when the stage 1 twin task copies the twin in, by pattern and never by path. Reason: fake data only is a rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:66`), and a rule needs a check that fails.
- **D-126** Question: what does K8 leave to K9, and what runs while an owner gate waits? Choice: the three cloud checks `BUILD_BRIEF.md:286`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:289` list (can Playwright's browsers be installed in a session, can `stripe/stripe-mock` be pulled, does the merge trigger fire on auto-merge) need a real cloud session and are K9's to run, each with its fallback already in the brief; K8 only builds the logic and fakes. While an owner gate waits (account checkpoint, Stage 0.5, real texts), tasks that do not depend on it stay eligible and continue, because a waiting gate holds only its dependents (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:234`); Gate P is the exception and holds every `T###` task. Reason: K8 had no cloud session to test in, and the brief's blocking rule is narrow on purpose.

## Simulation round 2 fixes, October 27, 2026

Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2 (Oct 27, 2026). The round-2 simulation of the current spec (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v2/Ask_Hava_Sim_v2_Findings.md`, outside this repository; every number in it is simulated) listed 22 ranked issues, 17 contradictions and a set of gaps. Each entry below is one fix, decided under the §16 rule (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`) on Casey's instruction; "Issue n" and "Cn" are the findings' numbering. Entries that change something Casey had decided or seen before are listed in `docs/for-casey-review.md`. Gate P and `build_hold` are unchanged: nothing here starts a build task.

- **D-127** Question: replies to the consent text other than OK, Yes, Sure or Sí ("okay", "yes please", "Y", "si") were ignored, and D-095 forbade any reminder before consent, so about 11% of simulated requesters were never heard from again (Issue 1, C11). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: consent is any reply whose first word, punctuation and accents ignored, is ok, okay, yes, y, yep, yeah, sure, sí or si (CON-005); any other reply to the consent text that is not an opt-out or HELP gets the consent text once more as a direct reply, and a request still waiting for consent at 24 hours gets the consent text one last time, inside quiet hours (CON-027); the 72-hour silent close stays. This changes D-021 (more words) and D-095 (one consent re-send at 24 hours). Reason: v9.3 added Yes, Sure and Sí because real people's wording was being ignored (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:608`); re-sending the consent text itself sends nothing but the consent text, so consent-first holds (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:113`), and every waiting state needs a timeout pattern with a reminder (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`).
- **D-128** Question: what counts as "safe" after the safety text, and what happens to an "OK" sent when both the safety text and the consent text wait for an answer (findings section 5)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the safety text ends "Reply SAFE when you are safe."; safe, seguro, segura and "we're safe / ok / fine" count as safe, and so do a plain OK or Yes once consent is recorded; a single reply that records consent while the safety question is open is followed by one direct reply asking "Are you safe now? Reply SAFE." (INT-025). Reason: the job can't move until the safe reply (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`), so the question has to be answerable in plain words and asked again once.
- **D-129** Question: a homeowner who texts "CANCEL" to cancel a job is opted out (CON-008), the job carries on, and in the simulation a business still got the address and paid after the cancel (Issue 2). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: "cancel" alone stays an opt-out and still never closes a job (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:112`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:159`); in addition (a) an opt-out from a number with a live job tells the homeowner the request is still open and how to close it, in the confirmation where the texting provider allows it, and otherwise by email and on the quote page (CON-028); (b) a sole-word CANCEL or CANCELAR from a number with a live job marks the job "paused by the homeowner": a pick waiting for Confirm is withdrawn (that business is told), no new pick, Confirm, charge or release happens, quotes still arrive, timers keep running, and the pause ends when the homeowner picks or closes on the quote page or by email (CON-029). This refines D-005 (an opt-out still changes no job status; the pause is a flag). Reason: the sole word is ambiguous between "stop texting" and "cancel the job"; the safest reading of both is to stop texts and stop money and release until the homeowner says which.
- **D-130** Question: who sends the opt-out confirmation for the texting provider's own keywords, and what happens to an opt-out while texting is off or suspended (findings section 5, Issue 17 T3)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the provider's automatic reply is used for its own keywords, set to the CON-009 wording, and the app sends the confirmation only for words the provider does not handle, so exactly one confirmation goes (Phase 2 checks this on the sandbox); while texting is off or suspended an opt-out is recorded at once, confirmed by email if one is on file, and never confirmed by a late text (CON-030). Reason: CON-009 allows exactly one confirmation within 5 minutes (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:158`); a late text to an opted-out number is the thing the rule forbids.
- **D-131** Question: CON-001 (consent text first) vs CON-018 (HELP from a number never texted), and CON-013 ("no text of any kind until START") vs CON-018 (HELP answered within 5 minutes): planned tests TD-CON-05 and TD-CON-07 can't both pass (C2, C3, Issue 17). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a first message that is only HELP, INFO or AYUDA gets the HELP reply, and the consent text follows when the person sends a request; "help" with other words ("I need help with my sink") is a request and gets the consent text (CON-001, CON-018); an opted-out number that texts HELP, INFO or AYUDA still gets the HELP reply (CON-013). Reason: v9.3 answers HELP on every number (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:259`) and its quiet-hours list keeps STOP and HELP replies at any hour (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:264`); a HELP reply carries the disclosures the consent rule protects.
- **D-132** Question: CON-001 makes the consent text the first message to every new number, so a business owner who texts "I'm a contractor, send me jobs" never gets the sign-up link INT-020 promises (Issue 11, C1). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a first message matching the business-intent list (with the AI second opinion on scrubbed text) gets one text with the business sign-up link, "Ask Hava" and the STOP/HELP line, and opens no job (CON-001, INT-020); the AI second opinion runs only on a message that would otherwise open a new request. Reason: both lines are in the changes file (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:113`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:92`); like D-008, the specific line is the exception to the general one, and the homeowner consent text asks a business owner to agree to homeowner terms for a request he isn't making.
- **D-133** Question: safe mode pauses "charges, releases and outbound texts" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:375`), so in the simulation one unacknowledged delivery alert during a 7-day absence stopped every text for about 105 hours, including safety texts, STOP confirmations and consent texts (Issue 3, C4). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: safe mode pauses charges and releases only; texts keep going, and safety texts, STOP/HELP/START replies and pick codes are never paused by anything but a suspension or the texting switch (OPS-001); when safe mode ends, each paused charge or release goes ahead only after FEE-017's check (OPS-024). Reason: the brief and the changes file conflict on what safe mode stops; the changes file wins on what and says safe mode "pauses risky actions" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:73`); moving money and releasing contact details are the risky actions, and the safety text (INT-003) and opt-out confirmation (CON-009) are legal or safety duties that a pause must not break.
- **D-134** Question: OPS-004's "below 95% for 30 minutes" has no minimum volume, so one failed text out of 10 to 20 in a quiet half hour starts the 24-hour safe-mode clock; and with no backup person (D-024) nobody can acknowledge while Casey is away (Issue 3, findings 6.2). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the delivery alert fires when fewer than 95% of the last 50 texts were delivered (OPS-004); a delivery alert whose rate is back at or above 95% over the next 50 texts is acknowledged automatically, logged and listed for Casey (OPS-023). No backup person is named: that is Casey's call, listed in `docs/for-casey-review.md`; the automatic acknowledgement is the working answer until he decides. Reason: v9.3's threshold (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:457`) needs a sample size to mean anything; the changes file names no backup (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:73`), and inventing one is not Claude's to do.
- **D-135** Question: during safe mode the 1-business-hour card timer still runs, so a business with a good card can lose the pick because charges are paused (C17). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: payment timers (the card-fix hour, FEE-020 and JOB-014, and the 4-hour Stripe release, FEE-018) stop while charges are paused by safe mode or the owner's charging switch, and resume with the time they had left (FEE-038). Reason: a timer meant to measure the business's or Stripe's delay must not measure Ask Hava's own pause (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:191`).
- **D-136** Question: at night a quote for a job the homeowner chose as an emergency waits until 8 a.m., because the first-quote text is not on QH-004's night list (Issue 4, C14). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: for a job the homeowner chose as an emergency, its quote texts (the first-quote text and quote-count updates) are also allowed at night (QH-004). Reason: D-054's reasoning applies unchanged: the homeowner chose urgent service after the safety text, so these texts are urgent in the changes file's sense (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:184`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`).
- **D-137** Question: the emergency keyword list misses "hot", "buzzing", "melting" and "sizzling", so in the simulation 17% of emergencies got a late safety text (Issue 5). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the list is written out in INT-002 and adds the stems buzz, melt, sizzl, propane, carbon monoxide, "co alarm", and Spanish zumb and derret; "hot" and "caliente" count only in a message that also names an outlet, plug, cord, switch, breaker, panel, wire, meter or their Spanish words, and "pilot" goes on the gas list (D-157) rather than the emergency list; the AI emergency flag also runs on the scrubbed first message before consent, where AI is allowed (INT-026). Different from the suggested wording: bare "hot" would fire on every "hot water heater" request and bare "pilot" on every "pilot light won't stay lit", sending a needless 911 text and holding routine jobs until "safe"; "breaker box is hot to the touch", the findings' example, still matches. Reason: the safety text must go within 5 minutes whatever the AI returns (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:290`); scrubbed text carries no personal details (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:58`).
- **D-138** Question: INT-007 says a 1–2 word message never opens a job, but "gas smell" gets the safety text, and the SAFE and urgent steps need a job to belong to (C6). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: an emergency-keyword message opens a job whatever its length (INT-007). Reason: the safety path (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`) outranks the short-message guard, which exists to stop "ME" or a bare ZIP opening jobs (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:214`).
- **D-139** Question: QH-003 and D-025 give an "unknown zone" number an all-US-zones window, but D-100 refuses every number outside the area-code table, so the rule is dead (C5). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: every area code in the table has a zone, and a number not in the table is never texted (QH-003). D-025 is withdrawn. Reason: one rule instead of two, and the table check is already the refusal point (D-100; `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:121`).
- **D-140** Question: JOB-025 closes the job when a failed pick leaves no choosable quote, even while other businesses' 24-hour quote windows are open (Issue 6, C7). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the job closes only when no choosable quote is left and every card's 24-hour window has ended; otherwise it waits in Quotes ready for the next quote, with the usual 3-day timer, and the homeowner is told more quotes may still come (JOB-025). This refines D-045. Reason: each business keeps its own 24-hour window (D-042; `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:83`), and the homeowner was told "more may come" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:82`).
- **D-141** Question: a business whose calls were answered but lasted under 45 seconds was refunded automatically as "homeowner unreachable", which a business that knows the rule can game (Issue 7, C9); FEE-024 says a cancel refunds only if the business "has not talked to the homeowner" while CALL-007 needs 45 seconds. Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: any call in either direction answered by a person, whatever its length, means the business has talked to the homeowner; the unreachable refund needs 2 business call attempts at least 3 hours apart within 72 hours, no call in either direction answered by a person, and no DONE or PROBLEM reply from the homeowner (FEE-022); the cancel refunds follow the same "talked" test (FEE-023, FEE-024); machine answers never count; the 45-second mark is logged for reporting only (CALL-007); calls the homeowner makes are not business attempts. Different from v9.3's 45-second connection (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:334`): the changes file's own test is "haven't talked to the homeowner" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:98`), and the changes file wins.
- **D-142** Question: does a job stay Connected after the automatic unreachable refund, and do check-ins continue (findings 6.2)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the refund moves the job to Closed, closes the call line, stops check-ins and tells the homeowner "<business> couldn't reach you. Text us any time to start again." (JOB-027). Reason: every waiting state needs an end (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:111`); a refunded introduction has ended.
- **D-143** Question: a homeowner who chose email contact had the job re-opened as a no-show at 72 hours while the business was emailing them (Issue 8, C8); and what does a day-3 NO mean (findings section 5)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the 72-hour "never called" return to Quotes ready and the no-show record apply only when the homeowner chose a call; for email contact the day-3 check-in decides: DONE or PROBLEM as usual, and NO ("they haven't reached me") records a no-show and returns the job to Quotes ready (JOB-015, FEE-035). For call contact, NO keeps v9.3's meaning and closes the job. The unreachable refund (FEE-022) still uses the call log for every contact method, as D-049 chose. Reason: the email choice is the homeowner's own (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:408`), and the no-show rule judged from calls can't see email.
- **D-144** Question: during a Twilio suspension an emergency message gets no safety text and raises no critical alert, the banner waits for Casey, and businesses with no email never hear that they lost (Issue 9, C13). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: while texting is off or suspended, any emergency-keyword message raises a critical alert, gets the safety text by email when an email is on file and in the web chat, the status banner turns on automatically, and texting is switched back on only by Casey after Twilio confirms the number is restored (TXT-024); business sign-up requires an email, and every business text is also shown in the business portal and, while texting is off, emailed (BIZ-019). Reason: the safety text duty (INT-003) does not depend on the channel; the banner exists for exactly this case (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:134`); no second number is used (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:209`).
- **D-145** Question: every business sign-up waits for Casey's approval (D-060, 15 minutes each in the simulation); a normal launch took a median 3.7 days to go live and a crowded one 18 days, and Casey's time roughly doubled (Issue 10). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a sign-up whose identity code is completed and whose license or city-list match passes, with no flag (record mismatch, name mismatch, the letter path, a fraud signal, a sign-up for switched-off categories only), is activated automatically; flagged sign-ups wait in needs review for Casey's approval, which still never activates alone (BIZ-003); Casey can pause any business afterwards, and every automatic activation is listed for him (BIZ-016). A waiting sign-up is emailed its status when it starts waiting and at least every 7 days (BIZ-020); sign-up offers only switched-on categories, and a business whose only categories are off goes on the waitlist and is told why (BIZ-021). This replaces D-060. Reason: the changes file requires only that approval never activates on its own and that the identity code and license or city-list match are in place (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:115`); v9.3's review modelled Casey reading every sign-up (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:617`), which the round-2 numbers show he can't sustain.
- **D-146** Question: the scrubber misses lower-case "im steve" and hyphenated phone numbers, and the name-and-address answer reached the AI business-intent check (Issue 12); and when are the street address and first name collected (findings section 5)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: AI-002 removes up to two words after this is, my name is, I'm, im, it's, soy or me llamo (any case), unless the first of them is on a common-words list kept in settings, so "I'm having a leak" keeps its words; any phone-shaped number (7 or more digits with spaces, dots, dashes or brackets); street-number patterns; and emails. The first name and street address are asked as the last intake question, after consent and the area check, and that answer never goes to the AI (INT-027). Different from the suggested wording: removing any two words after "I'm" would strip "I'm having a" from most requests and hurt the reading the AI is there for. Reason: the scrubber must remove names before any AI call (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:58`).
- **D-147** Question: "2 YES" after "1 YES" gets "no quotes waiting for a pick" because the job is in Awaiting confirm, and the first business confirms and is charged (Issue 13); what is the reply to "<n> YES" when nothing waits (findings section 5)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a "<n> YES" for a different quote while the first pick waits for Confirm replaces that pick, and the first business is told the pick was withdrawn (PICK-032); once the business has tapped Confirm the reply says the pick is confirmed and how to close; with no job waiting for a pick the reply says where the request stands (PICK-036). Reason: a homeowner can pick any quote (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:82`) and no charge happens before Confirm (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:221`).
- **D-148** Question: businesses naturally reply "YES 1042" to the pick text, which is not a Confirm, and lose the 2-business-hour window while they find the link (Issue 14); does the window count from the pick or from delivery of the pick text, what does a business that answers an expired pick hear, and how does a business see why a $125-a-month job costs $75 (findings 6.2, 6.4)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the window counts from delivery of the pick text (PICK-019); YES <job number> (or YES when that business has exactly one pick waiting) from the picked business's own linked phone gets "Fee $X. Reply CONFIRM <n> to accept, or open <link>", and CONFIRM <n> from that phone is a Confirm when a card and the current authorization are on file, otherwise the reply is the Confirm link (PICK-033); a business answering an expired pick is told it expired and can't be picked again for this job (PICK-035); the pick text and the Confirm review step show the recurring fee basis, for example "Fee $75 (ongoing services count as 4 months: $125 x 4 = $500)" (PICK-034). Reason: the business must see the fee before Confirm (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:343`); a texted fee followed by an explicit CONFIRM reply is the same two-step review the brief asks for (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:406`).
- **D-149** Question: the AI read "1270 $633" as $63.30 with over 80% confidence, so the homeowner saw the wrong price and the wrong fee was charged (Issue 15). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a price the AI read from a text is always shown back to the business on the quote form to confirm before the homeowner sees it, whatever the confidence (PICK-006). Reason: the fee tier is fixed from the chosen quote (FEE-005), so a misread price is a wrong charge; the 80% rule (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:292`) is a floor, not a ceiling.
- **D-150** Question: losing businesses hear "This job is no longer active." only at the final status (D-044): a median of 6 days later in the simulation; and read literally, the winner gets it too (Issue 16, C10). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: when the picked business's payment or free win is recorded, every other business that quoted gets "The homeowner picked another quote for job <n>."; when the job ends, every business that quoted except the one whose pick was connected gets "This job is no longer active." (PICK-026). This refines D-044. Reason: the changes file sends the notice "whenever the job ends" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:85`); the earlier notice doesn't claim the job ended, so a later no-show that re-opens the job tells no lie.
- **D-151** Question: a business got a second free first win through a DBA with its own city license and no card on file (Issue 18). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the free-win entity key also includes the identity-code contact on the record (phone or email) and the owner name on the city list (FEE-011). Reason: the changes file's key (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:116`) is a minimum; more keys only make the once-only rule stricter.
- **D-152** Question: "join in the first 90 days" (FEE-010, WEB-005): the sign-up date or the activation date, given activation can take days (C12)? Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the date the sign-up form was submitted (FEE-010). Reason: a business shouldn't lose the launch offer to Ask Hava's own review time (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:67`).
- **D-153** Question: FEE-016 gives each charge attempt its own key, so a timed-out attempt retried at once can charge twice, and reconcile would "match" the second charge to the same job (C15). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a timed-out attempt is not retried until Stripe confirms it failed (FEE-019's direct check), and the nightly reconcile refunds any second kept charge for the same pick (FEE-037). Reason: the per-attempt key exists so a failed attempt never blocks a later one (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:327`), not to allow two successes.
- **D-154** Question: LIC-023's "overlapping scope" includes "the same category", so the same handyman brought back for a second small job within 30 days is always blocked, against "repeat work is always free and welcome" (FEE-013), and the check's timing is not stated (C16). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the split-job check runs when the second quote is submitted and blocks it only when the two jobs share a job type or together reach $1,000 (LIC-023). This refines D-081. Reason: the check exists to stop one job being split to dodge the $1,000 limit (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:208`); two unrelated small jobs under $1,000 together can't dodge it.
- **D-155** Question: the tenant line on cards depends on the AI spotting a rental, which it misses about 15% of the time in the simulation (Issue 19). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: intake asks "Do you own or rent this place?" when the rental reading is missing or unsure, or when no AI is used (INT-028). Reason: the card must carry "Landlord approval may be needed." for rentals (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:89`); v9.3 records the AI misses hinted rentals often (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:614`).
- **D-156** Question: after a restore the replay re-sent "This job is no longer active" before Twilio's log was matched (Issue 20). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: before every send, the replay checks Twilio's sent-message log for that message's derived key and skips any text already sent (OPS-017). Reason: nothing is sent twice (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:482`).
- **D-157** Question: "pool heater won't light" has no gas keyword, so a misread can send gas work to an unlicensed pool cleaner (Issue 21). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the gas keyword list is kept in the catalog and starts with gas, propane, natural gas, pilot, pilot light, "won't light", "heater won't light", furnace, gas line, gas dryer, gas stove, gas range, and Spanish gas, propano and piloto (AI-012). Reason: gas work never goes to handymen (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:42`); the list overrides the AI, so it must be wide.
- **D-158** Question: two update texts held by quiet hours both go out at 8 a.m. (Issue 22). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: held update texts for the same job and recipient are merged into one at the next window (TXT-025). Reason: one update every few hours (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:84`).
- **D-159** Question: D-037 calls a period on a non-recurring quote an error, but no rule says what happens to a recurring-service quote with no period, or a "per visit" price (findings 6.2). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: a quote in a recurring category for a job type not in its `one_time_job_types` must state a period; one without a period is bounced back to the business to state it; a per-visit price with a stated visit schedule is converted to monthly (per visit x visits per month, weekly visits = 52 / 12 a month) before the 4-month rule (REC-010). Reason: the 4-month rule needs a monthly price (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:60`).
- **D-160** Question: the masked-number pool size is a setting with no starting value (findings section 5). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the setting starts at 10 numbers, which Casey buys in his account step; the app still never buys numbers (CALL-017). Reason: the simulation's 10-number pool kept wrong-person calls at zero in normal load; the size stays a setting (D-077).
- **D-161** Question: TERMS-017 was drafted, moved to WEB-017 and never merged, and `scripts/trace_spec.py` doesn't report numbering gaps (findings 6.3). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: `docs/spec/index.md` records that TERMS-017 is unused and never to be reused; a numbering-gap report in `scripts/trace_spec.py`, a protected file, is left for a future `harness` task (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:70`). Reason: smallest change outside protected paths; the gap is harmless.
- **D-162** Question: two pairs of planned tests in `docs/test-design.md` can't both pass: TD-CON-05 vs TD-CON-07 (HELP after STOP) and TD-OPS-01 / journey J3 step 5 vs the 5-minute safety-text tests (safe mode stopping texts) (findings 6.3). Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the spec lines are fixed first (D-131, D-133), then the tests: TD-CON-05 now expects the HELP reply after STOP, and TD-OPS-01 and J3 now expect safe mode to pause charges and releases while safety texts, STOP/HELP replies and other texts still go. New entries TD-CON-11 to TD-CON-13, TD-INT-10, TD-INT-11, TD-PICK-14, TD-PICK-15, TD-JOB-07, TD-FEE-20, TD-BIZ-05, TD-TXT-09 and TD-OPS-06 cover the new lines. Reason: a wrong test changes only through a logged spec change (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:69`).

**Spec changes from the round-2 fixes** (each changed line names its decision): `consent.md` CON-001, CON-005, CON-013, CON-018 changed, CON-027 to CON-030 added; `intake.md` INT-002, INT-007, INT-019, INT-020 changed, INT-025 to INT-028 added; `quiet-hours.md` QH-003, QH-004 changed; `messaging.md` TXT-024, TXT-025 added; `quotes-pick.md` PICK-006, PICK-019, PICK-026 changed, PICK-032 to PICK-036 added; `lifecycle.md` JOB-015, JOB-025 changed, JOB-027 added; `fees.md` FEE-010, FEE-011, FEE-022, FEE-023, FEE-024, FEE-035 changed, FEE-037, FEE-038 added; `calls.md` CALL-007, CALL-017 changed; `ai.md` AI-002, AI-012 changed; `businesses.md` BIZ-016 changed, BIZ-019 to BIZ-021 added; `licensing.md` LIC-023 changed; `recurring.md` REC-010 added; `ops-recovery.md` OPS-001, OPS-004, OPS-017 changed, OPS-023, OPS-024 added; `index.md` numbering note. D-025 is withdrawn by D-139; D-060 is replaced by D-145; D-021, D-044, D-045, D-049, D-081 and D-095 are refined as each entry says.

## Simulation round 3 fixes, October 27, 2026

The round-3 simulation re-ran every scenario against the spec as merged in PR #19 (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v3/Ask_Hava_Sim_v3_Findings.md`, outside this repository; every number in it is simulated). Most round-2 problems were gone, but two of the fixes caused High issues of their own. Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2 covers fixing them, because they come from the same fixes (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`). Gate P and `build_hold` are unchanged.

- **D-163** Question: D-134's delivery alert ("fewer than 95% of the last 50 texts") fires on ordinary noise. At a 1.5% failure rate, 3 failures in some run of 50 texts happens several times a month, and after Casey acknowledges an alert a new one can fire at once. In the simulation the time Casey spent on alerts during a vendor-trouble month went from 23 to 139 minutes a week. Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the delivery alert fires when fewer than 90% of the last 50 texts, or fewer than 95% of the last 200, were delivered (OPS-004). Only one delivery alert is open at a time. After one is acknowledged, by Casey or automatically, no new one fires until at least 97% of the last 200 texts were delivered (OPS-025). The automatic acknowledgement uses the same 97%-of-200 recovery line (OPS-023). Reason: an alert that a normal month sets off is one Casey learns to ignore, and each one can start the 24-hour safe-mode clock. A sustained drop of 5% or more is still caught within 200 texts (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:457`).
- **D-164** Question: with the own-or-rent question (D-155) and the safe re-ask (D-128) added, an urgent emergency reached businesses within an hour less often than before. In the simulation, 52% of urgent emergencies in the night-emergency scenario took over an hour, against 32% in round 2, mostly waiting on the own-or-rent and name-and-address answers. Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: for a job the homeowner chose as an emergency, job cards go out as soon as consent, the safe reply, the urgent choice, the ZIP and the area check are done. The own-or-rent and name-and-address questions are asked right after the cards. When the picked business confirms before the address is in, the homeowner is asked for it again at once (allowed at night for that job, QH-004), and the release waits for the answer (INT-029). If the homeowner then says they rent, every business that got the card is sent "Landlord approval may be needed." for that job. This refines D-146 and D-155 for emergencies only. Reason: the address is needed only at the release (PICK-022), and the changes file puts after-hours emergency service first once the homeowner is safe (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:87`).

**Spec changes from the round-3 fixes:** `ops-recovery.md` OPS-004 and OPS-023 changed, OPS-025 added; `intake.md` INT-027 and INT-028 changed, INT-029 added; `quiet-hours.md` QH-004 changed. Tests: TD-OPS-01, TD-OPS-06, TD-INT-11 and TD-QH-02 changed; TD-INT-12 added.

## Simulation round 4 fix, October 27, 2026

Round 4 re-ran everything against the spec after PR #20 (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v3/Ask_Hava_Sim_v3_Findings.md`, outside this repository; every number in it is simulated). It found one more High issue. It was already there in round 2 but went unnoticed, and fixing it falls under Casey's "Fix it all" on sim round 2 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:537`). Gate P and `build_hold` are unchanged.

- **D-165** Question: the emergency word stems (INT-002) match street names. "smok" matches Smoketree Ave, a real Lake Havasu City street, so a homeowner who answers the name-and-address question with "Pat, 100 Smoketree Test Ave" gets the gas-and-911 safety text, and the job is held until they reply SAFE and is offered after-hours emergency service. In the simulation's street mix this happened about 28 times per 60 days, roughly one homeowner in eight. Casey: "Fix it all" on sim round 2. Choice: the emergency keyword check runs on each message after street-address patterns are removed, and never on the answer to the name-and-address question. The AI emergency flag (INT-026) still reads the whole scrubbed request (INT-002). Reason: the safety text must mean something (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:86`), and an address can't describe a hazard. A real emergency message still matches, because its hazard words are outside the street name (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:290`).

**Spec changes from the round-4 fix:** `intake.md` INT-002 changed. Tests: TD-INT-01 changed.

## K9 (rehearsal), October 27, 2026

The runner ran the rehearsal with three practice tasks in `kit/rehearsal/` and no app code. The full write-up, with PR numbers and evidence, is `docs/rehearsal.md`; the schedule set from it is `kit/schedule.json` (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:510`).

- **D-166** Question: K9 has no task prompt in `kit/prompts/`, and workers get only their task prompt (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:245`); who runs the rehearsal? Choice: the runner runs it itself, as it ran K1 to K8: `kit/prompts/` holds only the `T###` build prompts and `TEMPLATE.md`, and K1 to K8 ran from their Appendix B rows (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:595`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:603`). Three practice tasks: P1 passes (PR #18, merged as 1cac5d7, tree matches), P2 fails on purpose (PR #17, `Ci` red, closed unmerged, `BLOCKED`, branch kept), and P3 is claimed twice (19:10Z, bf39373 accepted, the second push rejected) and given a simulated usage-limit rejection, then resumed after the 20:00Z reset (PR #21, merged as 65e3f6d, tree matches; done by sessions that ignored the live lease, D-171, so the runner's resume path is shown only by `tests/test_failure_drills.py`). Deviations, logged: P1 and P2 skipped the fresh-eyes reviewer (one 6-line rehearsal file each, read by the runner; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:249`); P2's retry was not spent live, because a deliberately failing test fails the same way twice and the retry and Opus-escalation path is covered offline by `tests/test_failure_drills.py` (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:271`); P1 and P2 were in flight together, safe only because P2 could never merge (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:224`). The one-round Opus review of the K9 report is the Kit Plan's "Opus reviews" for K9. Reason: Claude decides and logs instead of waiting (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:533`), and the Kit Plan's done line is met: all three ended correctly, the failing one is blocked and nothing merged (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:38`).
- **D-167** Question: can `stripe/stripe-mock` run in a session (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:288`)? Choice: no. The session has no Docker daemon (no `docker.sock`), so stripe-mock runs in CI only and sessions use the twin's fake Stripe, the brief's fallback. Reason: the payments stand-in is stripe-mock in Docker plus the twin (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:160`), and the fallback keeps both, each where it can run.
- **D-168** Question: can Playwright's browsers run in a session (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:287`)? Choice: yes. Node Playwright launches the pre-installed Chromium 153 from `/opt/pw-browsers`; the Python `playwright` package is not installed, and the first task that adds browser tests installs it through the uv lock file. It is already on the decided stack, so it needs no new-library entry. `Ally` still runs in GitHub Actions. Reason: the stack names Playwright (Python) for tests (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:115`), and the browsers it needs are already present.
- **D-169** Question: does a merge start the next runner session (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:260`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:289`)? Choice: treat it as unverified and let the hourly run carry the chain. The routine kept as the scheduler (D-171), "Ask Hava build runner", runs hourly at 50 past the hour (confirmed by the trigger listing); whether it also has the "pull request closed, merged into `main`" trigger is unverified, because the listing does not show GitHub event triggers (fresh sessions at 20:11Z and 20:14Z, right after merges, are consistent with one but don't prove it). The API-created "Ask Hava build runner (hourly)" has none. Expect about one task an hour (`kit/schedule.json`); Casey is asked to confirm or add the trigger on the routine, which would allow back-to-back cycles, but nothing waits on it. Reason: the brief's own fallback (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:289`); every kit step took about 10 to 30 minutes, inside one hourly cycle.
- **D-170** Question: is D-122's model evidence real, and what model-ID format does the worker call accept (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:247`)? Choice: confirmed. Worker transcripts at `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` are readable from the runner session, and each assistant record's `message.model` carries the full ID (P1: 6 records, P2: 10 records, all `claude-haiku-5-5`). The worker call accepts only the short names `haiku`, `sonnet` and `opus`, not full IDs, so the runner passes the short name and checks the full ID in the transcript; that check, not the call, decides the attempt. This closes D-122's open point; the usage-records fallback is not needed. Reason: the brief says the model is passed as a full ID (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:244`), which the call can't take; the brief's purpose, a model check from evidence the runner inspects itself (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:247`), is fully met.
- **D-171** Question: two schedulers were firing runners: (a) "Ask Hava build runner (hourly)", created through the API at 15:02Z, cron `7 * * * *`, waking the persistent session ...W8Ex... (Opus), and (b) "Ask Hava build runner", created at 14:49Z from the claude.ai routines page, cron `50 * * * *`, starting a fresh `claude-sonnet-5-5` session with the Appendix A prompt each time. The K9 lease named ...W8Ex... (bf39373 through dfec090, expiry 22:15Z renewed to 00:15Z), yet two fresh sessions from (b) worked inside it: ...W9x5... pushed P3's commit eafe114 at 20:11Z and ...W6ng... marked P3 `DONE` at 20:14Z (f30d043); the lease holder lost the push race at 20:12Z and stood down (runner observation, not in state). Only git's non-fast-forward rule prevented a double merge. What is the rule? Choice: keep (b), which gives fresh sessions with the repository and GitHub tools on Sonnet as Appendix A intends, and delete (a); a live lease naming another session means stop, with no exceptions. Reason: the runner's logic would have returned `lease_live` (`runner/cycle.py:164`), so the fresh sessions did not follow it; Appendix A's step 6 says only "claim the next eligible task" (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:582`) and K9 was not eligible; the brief has one scheduled task, one session at a time (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:208`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:209`) and a Sonnet runner (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:563`).

## Business-side fix plan, October 27, 2026

The business sign-up friction study (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v5/Ask_Hava_Business_Signup_Friction_Report.md`) and the fix plan built on it (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v5/Ask_Hava_Business_Side_Fix_Plan.md`), both outside this repository, used 29 AI-simulated businesses, not real people; every number marked [sim] there is simulated, and the legal-fit notes are research-based judgments, not legal advice. Casey, Oct 27, 2026: "Make all the changes you recommend" on the business-side fix plan. That applies every section 3A fix (D-172 to D-187) and every section 3B question with its recommended answer (D-188 to D-207): B7 and B14 are "no", B5 keeps the 2-hour window, B9 caps ongoing services at $75, B10 keeps $25 / $75 / $199 with a 12-month lock for founding businesses and does not adopt $39, and B19 adds the safety voice call with a lawyer's check left open. The plan numbered its decisions from D-166; K9 had already used D-166 to D-171, so they start at D-172. Section 3C items need real contractor interviews and are not applied; they are listed in `docs/for-casey-review.md`. Every number the interviews may tune is a setting. Changes to the prototype site and the go-to-market pitch, both outside this repository, are pinned in the spec so the build cannot copy the errors. Gate P and `build_hold` are unchanged; nothing is built.

- **D-172** Question (plan A1): the prototype site and the pitch carry errors the build could copy: ongoing services counted at the superseded month count instead of 4, a "What a lead can cost elsewhere" table, a pest row, the free-win line without its 90-day rule, a "Skip tenant requests" box, an identity "Email me" choice, and "no bidding wars". Casey approved the plan. Choice: WEB-018 makes the business and pricing pages take every number and offer from the spec, never from the prototype; the pitch and any flyer are fixed by hand outside the repository (listed for Casey). Reason: the changes file beats the prototype on wording (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:15`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:143`), lead-cost claims need a cited source (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:193`), pest is never advertised (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:239`), the tenant option is dropped (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:251`), and codes go only to record contacts (D-059).
- **D-173** Question (plan A2): businesses sign without a plain summary of what they will be charged. Casey approved the plan. Choice: BIZ-030: a plain-words box on fees, refunds and card use above the Terms link at sign-up and as "How you're charged" on the business page, including "It's an introduction fee for a homeowner who chose you." and "A new fee never applies until you accept it.", with a test that its numbers match the fee settings, as TERMS-005 does for the terms. Reason: the business terms must state the money rules exactly as coded (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:212`), and the fee is a fixed introduction fee by job size (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:238`).
- **D-174** Question (plan A3): a business can lose its refund under the 72-hour no-call rule (FEE-035) without warning. Casey approved the plan. Choice: TXT-026: a business with a released job and no call attempt gets a reminder 24 and 48 hours after the release, in English and Spanish, under quiet hours. Reason: the no-call rule stands (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:176`); a reminder is an account notice about the business's own job (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:181`).
- **D-175** Question (plan A4): waitlisted businesses fear losing their place, and the free win reads as small-jobs-only. Casey approved the plan. Choice: the waitlist message says "Your join date is kept." (BIZ-021) and the free-win line says "on any job size" (WEB-005). Reason: FEE-010 already counts the sign-up submit date (D-152, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:67`); copy only.
- **D-176** Question (plan A5): some trades learn only at the end of sign-up that they are waitlisted. Casey approved the plan. Choice: BIZ-021: sign-up starts with the category list marked "Open now" or "Coming later", before any license number; a business choosing only coming-later categories joins the waitlist first. Pest control appears only as a neutral item in that list, never on public pages. Reason: categories are switched on and off by setting (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:35`); pest is never advertised (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:239`).
- **D-177** Question (plan A6): a no-license business not yet on the city list hits a dead end. Casey approved the plan. Choice: LIC-011: it waits as pending and is activated automatically, with the identity code, when a later daily list shows a name match, keeping its join date. Reason: the city list is used only to verify the business's own name (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:37`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:250`); approval still never activates alone (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:115`).
- **D-178** Question (plan A7): identity codes go to stale record contacts with no way forward. Casey approved the plan. Choice: BIZ-026: before sending a code, sign-up shows the masked record contact and its kind, calls a landline by voice first, and offers "That number is old", which explains how to update the ROC or city record, saves the sign-up and resumes it automatically when the next daily list shows a new contact; BIZ-020: needs-review status also goes by text to the record contact, within 1 business day. The code still goes only to public-record contacts (BIZ-002, D-059). Reason: the identity chain is unchanged (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:93`); only a resume step is added.
- **D-179** Question (plan A8): office managers can't help with sign-up. Casey approved the plan. Choice: BIZ-024: anyone may fill in the form, the code still goes only to the record contact, and the screen says "Someone else filling this in? The owner just reads you the code." Reason: identity rests on the record contact, not on who types (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:115`).
- **D-180** Question (plan A9): Spanish-first owners get English-only business pages. Casey approved the plan. Choice: WEB-019: the business page, the business sign-up and a business-terms summary exist in Spanish, marked for native-speaker review. Reason: the site has Spanish pages (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:140`) and key terms appear in Spanish wherever Spanish copy markets the service (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:195`).
- **D-181** Question (plan A10): how to get founding businesses when nobody can sign up before Gate C? Casey approved the plan. Choice: no spec change. Casey keeps a short founding interest list himself (business, trade, how they'd like to be reached, their OK to be contacted once at launch) and signs up with each in person during launch week; the list is never built from the ROC or city lists. Reason: sign-ups and texts wait for Gate C (D-019, D-041), Claude does no outreach (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:61`), and the lists are for verification only (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:250`).
- **D-182** Question (plan A11): businesses can't see what a card for their trade looks like. Casey approved the plan. Choice: WEB-020: the business page shows a sample card per switched-on trade, from fake data, never for pest control. Reason: copy only; pest is never advertised (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:239`); fake data only (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:66`).
- **D-183** Question (plan A12): "Who is Casey?" Casey approved the plan. Choice: WEB-021: a "Who runs Ask Hava" block with his name, photo, "lives in Havasu", the LLC mailing address (registered agent or PO box, never the home address) and the help number. It does not mention police work; whether to is interview item C8. Reason: the home address stays private (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:227`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:228`).
- **D-184** Question (plan A13): the pitch says "no bidding wars" and doesn't address the fear of never winning. Casey approved the plan. Choice: WORD-017: business-facing copy says "You quote once. No bidding rounds, no paid placement." and "Every card says how many businesses got it.", never "no bidding wars"; the go-to-market pitch, the five objection answers and the recruiting pace of about 2 to 4 businesses per trade are edited by hand outside the repository. Reason: no ranking or paid placement (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:189`); Ask Hava never runs bidding (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:237`).
- **D-185** Question (plan A14): a business quoting a trip fee doesn't know its fee until the pick. Casey approved the plan. Choice: PICK-039: the quote form shows the trip-fee tier for that category before the business submits. Reason: trip-fee quotes use the category's set tier (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:61`).
- **D-186** Question (plan A15): the handyman certification feels like extra steps. Casey approved the plan. Choice: LIC-036: the certification box sits on the same screen as the price and is still ticked on every quote; LIC-025 is unchanged. Reason: handymen certify each quote (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:241`); this is layout only.
- **D-187** Question (plan A16): the sign-up done screen promises nothing honest about volume. Casey approved the plan. Choice: BIZ-031: "You're one of <n> businesses in <trade> so far. We'll text you once when requests in your trade start.", counted as in CARD-019; that one text is an account notice under the business's own consent (CON-019), never marketing. Reason: business consent covers account texts (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:181`).
- **D-188** Question (plan B1, card D1): businesses don't know how crowded a job is. Casey: yes. Choice: CARD-019: every job card says "Sent to <N> businesses.", and the portal's job page shows the current number sent and quotes so far. Reason: showing the count raised applications and steered them to less crowded openings in field experiments (Gee 2019; Bhole, Fradkin and Horton 2021, as cited in the plan); it changes no matching rule (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`).
- **D-189** Question (plan B2, card D1): cap, rotate or pace recipients? Casey: yes to keeping the no-cap rule. Choice: no cap, no rotation and no "trade full" waitlist (CARD-003 and D-028 unchanged); crowding is controlled by recruiting about 2 to 4 businesses per trade (go-to-market plan, outside the repository); OPS-027 flags, with no automatic action, a trade with 7 or more active businesses whose median job got 5 or more quotes in each of 2 consecutive calendar months, all numbers settings, so a response cap of 5 comes back to Casey. Reason: a cap or rotation would reverse the changes file's no-cap rule and make Ask Hava choose who sees which job (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:13`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:237`).
- **D-190** Question (plan B3, card D2): price is the only thing to compare on a quote card. Casey: yes. Choice: LIC-037 "ROC license since <year>" from the record's original issue date; LIC-038 the city-license year only if the city list carries a date (checked at build), else "City business license on file"; BIZ-022 a screened 140-character "About us" line in the business's own words; WORD-016 no rating, review, hire count or ranking. Reason: record facts shown for every business in the same format keep arrival order and no ranking (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:189`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:192`); never "vetted" or "best" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:194`); the business's own words are shown as written (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:200`).
- **D-191** Question (plan B4, card D3): the owner alone holds the phone that gets picks. Casey: yes. Choice: BIZ-023 team members with two roles, "Cards and quotes" and "Confirm and billing" (the owner has both); CALL-012 staff phones linked by the owner or a "Confirm and billing" member; PICK-033 a texted CONFIRM counts only from the owner's or a "Confirm and billing" phone; PICK-037 the pick text goes to every such phone and by email, with one reminder at 60 minutes (a setting); SEC-004 sign-in per person; CON-019 each team phone gives its own consent and has its own STOP; BIZ-018 language per phone. Reason: owners link staff phones (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:374`), business texting consent is per number (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:181`), and portal sign-in stays link-plus-code (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:315`); limiting CONFIRM closes "any linked phone can charge me".
- **D-192** Question (plan B5, card D4): 2 or 4 business hours to Confirm? Casey: yes to keeping 2. Choice: PICK-019 keeps 2 business hours, now a setting; the fix is delivery to every Confirm phone (D-191). If late answers stay above 5% after the plan's validation re-run, non-emergency jobs move to 3 hours by changing the setting. Reason: the window is a v9.3 rule (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:174`) and most complaints were about delivery.
- **D-193** Question (plan B6, card D5): losers learn nothing. Casey: yes. Choice: PICK-026: with 3 or more quotes (a setting), the loss text adds "<k> businesses quoted; the picked quote was lower than / about the same as / higher than yours."; PICK-038: firm quotes compare on amount and ranges on midpoint (as FEE-003), "about the same" is within 10% (a setting), a trip fee against another type says "different quote types", and no amount or name is ever disclosed; BIZ-025 a "Your numbers" panel. Reason: feedback thins weak entries and improves the rest (Gross 2017, as cited in the plan); exact prices and names are withheld to avoid aiding collusion (OECD guidance, as cited); the text stays a fixed template with a Spanish version (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:85`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:416`).
- **D-194** Question (plan B7, card D6): an in-person ID check by Casey when the record contact is dead? Casey: no. Choice: no in-person check at launch; the self-repair path (D-178) and the 1-business-day review target (BIZ-027) handle stale contacts. Revisit only if interviews show stale records are common and the ROC online update doesn't fix them (item C1, before the first sign-up). Reason: public-record contacts are the legally safest identity method (D-059), owner judgment never activates a business (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:115`), and ID images are never collected (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:223`).
- **D-195** Question (plan B8, card D7): a business in a trade that opens late can't use its free first win. Casey: yes. Choice: FEE-010: the free first win also goes to a business that joins no later than 90 days after a category it signed up for is switched on, including a waitlisted business that joined earlier; FEE-015 and WEB-005 follow. One per entity still applies (FEE-011). Reason: subsidizing the side that is hard to attract is the usual cold-start step (Rochet and Tirole 2003, as cited in the plan); the changes file's 90-day launch offer is kept and extended (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:67`).
- **D-196** Question (plan B9, card D8): $199 for a weekly cleaning client is most of what that client is worth. Casey: yes. Choice: FEE-039: a recurring service's fee is the tier for 4 months of its price, never more than $75 (a setting); a one-time job in a recurring category is not capped. FEE-015, PICK-034 and REC-009 wording follow, worked example W20 ($400 a month, basis $1,600.00) now pays $75 instead of $199, worked example W33 ($160 weekly, basis $2,773.33, fee $75) and REC-011 are added, and `scripts/check_catalog.py` applies the cap. Businesses accept a new authorization version before the next charge (FEE-007). Reason: still a fixed, published fee by job size with a published ceiling, never a percentage (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:59`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:60`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:238`).
- **D-197** Question (plan B10, card D9 and F): $25, $39, or $25 locked? Casey: yes. Choice: keep $25 / $75 / $199 (FEE-001 unchanged), do not adopt $39, and FEE-040: a business that joins within the first 90 days after launch is charged no more than the amounts in effect on its join date for 12 months from that date. Revisit $39 after 6 months of real data. Reason: the simulated gain from $39 was small and mostly noise [sim]; a lock is still a fixed published schedule, and fee changes already need a new authorization (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:59`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:238`).
- **D-198** Question (plan B11, card D10): a staff STOP pauses the whole account, and busy businesses can only pause in the portal. Casey: yes. Choice: BIZ-028: PAUSE, RESUME and BUSY <n> (1 to 60 days, "Booked until <date>" in the portal), with English and Spanish replies; BIZ-017: an opt-out from a team phone stops only that phone and the owner is emailed, while the owner's opt-out still pauses the account. PAUSE and BUSY are never read as opt-outs, and an opt-out word is never read as them. Reason: STOP still stops every text to that number (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:158`); signaling capacity raised hiring in a field experiment (Horton 2019, as cited in the plan).
- **D-199** Question (plan B12, card D11): a failed license lookup pauses a business after 24 hours. Casey: yes. Choice: LIC-008: the business stays as it was for up to 72 hours (a setting), warned at 24 (a setting), then paused; it can't Confirm while its status is unknown; a pause that clears when the list loads needs no review and shows to Casey as a daily count. Reason: nothing unverified is released, since Confirm is blocked (PICK-020) and end dates still pause on the day (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:194`, `sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:129`).
- **D-200** Question (plan B13, card D12): businesses never see complaints against them. Casey: yes. Choice: BIZ-029: each complaint shows in the portal with its kind and date, without homeowner details, and a reply box open 5 days; Casey reads both; the 3-in-90-days pause (D-016) is unchanged. Reason: the homeowner's identity stays private (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:91`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:191`).
- **D-201** Question (plan B14, card D13): let the homeowner share their number with the picked business? Casey: no. Choice: no sharing; PICK-023 unchanged; PICK-024: the details text adds "Tip: on your first call, ask which number to text." Reason: "Your number is never shared" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:191`) and sharing numbers is what carriers treat as lead generation (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:168`).
- **D-202** Question (plan B15, card D14): cards are thin. Casey: yes. Choice: CARD-007: cards include the category's multiple-choice intake answers as fixed fields; LIC-001: each intake question marks whether it is a card field. The final gate (CARD-008) still runs on every field and photos stay off (CARD-013). Reason: structured answers are safer than free text (`sources/01_spec/Havasu_Home_Quotes_Technical_Blueprint_v9.3.md:291`, `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:35`).
- **D-203** Question (plan B16, card D15): businesses get no promise about their data. Casey: yes.
