## Fix round 12, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026, 10:14: "Go ahead and fix everything that needs to be fixed. Just don't run any simulations right now." Inputs, all outside this repository: the status report of October 28, 2026 (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/Ask_Hava_Status_Report_Oct28.md`, section 5A, the 13 fixes, and section 5B, Casey's decisions), the four AI legal-research memos in `/home/claude/hhq/outputs/legal/` (Memo 1 contractor status and consumer law, Memo 2 messaging, privacy and data, Memo 3 business setup, tax and trademark, and Memo 4 adversarial review, whose Parts B and F override the earlier memos where they conflict), section 4.1 of the v10 business findings (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/sim_v10/business/Business_Angle_Findings_v10.md`) and gap G1 of the v10 owner findings. The plan is `/home/claude/hhq/outputs/legal/Fix_Round_12_Plan.md`. The memos are AI research, not legal advice, and no lawyer reviewed them; for section 5B only the conservative defaults are applied, and every other 5B item stays Casey's decision. No simulation was run; nothing was bought, sent, filed or signed; Gate P and build_hold are unchanged.

- **D-371** Question (Memo 4 Finding 1 and Part F item 1; status report 5A item 1): the memos give three conflicting consent and HELP texts; which one is built? Choice: one text set, rebuilt on CON-031 as merged in round 11. The consent text (CON-031, CON-002 to CON-004) is: "Ask Hava: Got your message. Ask Hava LLC is a local quote service in Lake Havasu City, not a contractor. We pass your request to local businesses, and you hire the one you choose directly. Free for you; the business you choose pays Ask Hava a set fee of $25 to $199, by quote size. Our texts are automated. Msg frequency varies by request. Msg & data rates may apply. You must be 18 or older. Reply OK to agree to the Terms (askhava.com/terms) and to get automated texts about your request. Privacy: askhava.com/privacy. Reply STOP to opt out, HELP for help. The Ask Hava owner gets HELP messages right away and usually replies within 2 days, or within 24 hours if your job is urgent." (684 characters, GSM-7, 5 segments; straight quotes only). Spanish draft for native-speaker review: "Ask Hava: Recibimos su mensaje. Ask Hava LLC es un servicio local de cotizaciones en Lake Havasu City, no un contratista. Pasamos su solicitud a negocios locales y usted contrata directamente al que elija. Es gratis para usted; el negocio que elija le paga a Ask Hava una tarifa fija de $25 a $199, según el monto de la cotización. Nuestros textos son automáticos. La frecuencia de mensajes varía según la solicitud. Pueden aplicarse tarifas de mensajes y datos. Debe tener 18 años o más. Responda OK para aceptar los Términos (askhava.com/terms) y recibir textos automáticos sobre su solicitud. Privacidad: askhava.com/privacy. Responda PARAR para no recibir más mensajes, AYUDA para ayuda. El dueño de Ask Hava recibe los mensajes de AYUDA de inmediato y normalmente responde en 2 días, o en 24 horas si su trabajo es urgente." (828 characters, UCS-2 because of the accents, 13 segments; budgeted, not cut). The HELP reply (CON-018) is: "Ask Hava (Ask Hava LLC), Lake Havasu City: Tell us what's wrong in one text. The Ask Hava owner gets HELP messages right away and usually replies within 2 days, or within 24 hours if your job is urgent. Help: askhava.com/help or help@askhava.com. Can't open links? Reply LIST to get your quotes by text. This number is text-only. If anyone is in danger, call 911. Reply STOP to opt out. Msg & data rates may apply." (414 characters, 3 segments, plus the roaming line for Canadian numbers). Every reply-time promise in Ask Hava's own voice says "usually" (CON-018, CON-027, CON-031, BIZ-047, TERMS-022, WEB-008, WEB-021), and the away setting (OPS-026) still replaces it. WEB-008's "a real person reviews anything flagged" becomes "The Ask Hava owner reads HELP messages and flagged messages, usually within 2 days, or within 24 hours if your job is urgent. Messages are not watched around the clock. In an emergency, call 911."; WEB-022's "Texts run automatically day and night. Safety texts go out at any hour." becomes "If your message mentions danger words, we send an automatic safety reminder. Don't rely on Ask Hava in an emergency: call 911." No safety-call sentence goes in the consent text while the safety voice call is off (D-384). Reason: Memo 4 Findings 1 and 9 (the consent text must keep the round-11 not-a-contractor line; "small fee" understates a fee of up to $199), Memo 4 Finding 10 (the OK reply also serves as an electronic signature under A.R.S. 44-7007, so the text says what OK agrees to), Memo 1 R-3 and Part III.B (specific human-review and response promises are Arizona Consumer Fraud Act "false promise" and voluntary-undertaking hooks).
- **D-372** Question (Memo 2 I.C and VII.F item 1, verified by Memo 4 Part D; status report 5A item 2): the FCC consent-revocation rule (FCC 26-67, 91 FR 67214, FR Doc. 2026-21821) takes effect November 25, 2026; what closes the gaps? Choice: new CON-035: every way a homeowner can revoke (a text, including natural-language opt-outs; an email to the help address or a reply to any Ask Hava email; the help page's form; the homeowner portal's texts-off switch, PRIV-021; and a request the owner records in the owner portal from any other contact) runs the same opt-out detector and feeds one suppression list, applied at once and never later than 10 business days, each event logged with its channel and time (CON-022); turning texts back on is only by texting START (CON-015); the app checks that list before every text and every automated call, since the provider's STOP blocks only texts from that sender. CON-009's confirmation becomes the bare "Ask Hava: You're unsubscribed. You won't get more texts or automated calls from Ask Hava at this number. To use Ask Hava again, text START." (Spanish "Ask Hava: Se canceló su suscripción. No recibirá más textos ni llamadas automáticas de Ask Hava en este número. Para volver a usar Ask Hava, envíe START."). CON-028's "Your request <number> is still open. Close it on your quote page." moves out of the text, to email and the quote page only. CON-030: the texting provider's Advanced Opt-Out is set so YES is removed from its re-subscribe words (START and UNSTOP stay), PARAR, ALTO, CANCELAR and BAJA are opt-out words, and its opt-out, re-subscribe and help replies use the approved wording; START gets "Ask Hava: You can text us again. Send your request to start. Msg & data rates may apply. Reply HELP for help, STOP to opt out.". CON-032 records the verification: "FCC 26-67 verified Oct 28, 2026: 91 FR 67214, effective Nov 25, 2026" (Memo 2 I.C; Memo 4 Part D). No exclusive revocation method is designated. Reason: Memo 2 I.C found the email, form and portal channels and Twilio's YES re-subscribe as gaps; the rule's "merely confirms" wording; Memo 4 Part F item 3.
- **D-373** Question (Memo 4 Findings 2 and 13; status report 5A item 3, 5B item 3): the draft policy says the AI never writes quote wording, but PICK-003 shows AI summaries; and the AI may read business quote text. Choice: AI quote summaries are OFF at launch: PICK-003 makes them an owner-portal setting, "AI quote summaries", off by default, that can be switched on only by Casey's recorded decision and only on the same day the privacy policy carries the summary sentence and every summary is labeled "Summary by Ask Hava's software. The business's own words above control." ("Resumen hecho por el software de Ask Hava. Las palabras del negocio arriba son las que valen."); while it is off the quote page shows only the business's own words and its quote-form fields. AI-002's scrubber runs on every text before any AI call, homeowner or business, including quote text (business and person names typed after the AI-002 phrases, phone-shaped numbers, emails, links and street numbers). New AI-018: one truthful AI description, word for word in the privacy policy, the homeowner and business data pages, the help page and the texting campaign: while AI-008 holds, "No AI reads your messages today. Our texts are automated and come from fixed templates."; once the zero-retention agreement is signed and AI goes live, with the change dated (TERMS-016), "To sort your request (for example, plumbing or electrical, urgent or not), an AI service from Anthropic reads your job description after our software removes names, phone numbers, emails and street numbers. Under our zero-retention agreement the AI provider doesn't keep that text or train on it. The AI doesn't write the texts or emails we send you, never sees your contact details and never makes decisions about money or who gets your details." plus, only while photo screening is on, "If you send photos, an AI check looks for faces and personal details so those photos stay off job cards." and, only while summaries are on, "AI may also shorten a business's quote into a summary shown next to the business's own words."; AI-006 adds that while summaries are off the AI writes nothing a homeowner or business reads. No copy relies on Section 230 for anything Ask Hava's software writes. Reason: Memo 4 Finding 2 (a false policy line is an FTC Act section 5 and A.R.S. 44-1522 deception risk; Section 230 likely doesn't cover Ask Hava's own generated summaries), Finding 13 (quote text can carry a name or phone before PICK-007 catches it); 5B conservative default.
- **D-374** Question (Memo 4 Finding 4; status report 5A item 4): Arizona's do-not-call statute covers texts (A.R.S. 44-1282) and seller registration applies to phone solicitation (A.R.S. 44-1272); how is recruiting done? Choice: new BIZ-048: "Ask Hava never texts or calls a business to recruit it." Recruiting is by posts, the website, email and in person only; no recruiting text or call goes to any number, including numbers from the ROC or city lists (LIC-010); a business is texted only after its own CON-019 text box and only about its own account and jobs; recruiting emails are commercial email, so each carries an honest subject, the LLC's postal address and a working way to stop further recruiting emails, honored at once and never later than 10 business days; and if Casey ever wants to recruit by phone, he registers with the Secretary of State under A.R.S. 44-1272 first and logs a new decision. Reason: Memo 4 Finding 4 (verified text: "an outbound telephone solicitation call or text message"; up to $1,000 per violation); Memo 2 IV.B (recruiting emails are commercial under CAN-SPAM).
- **D-375** Question (Memo 1 Part IV.E and R-8, Memo 4 Part F item 5; status report 5A item 5): the homeowner liability cap is "fees paid", which is $0 for a homeowner. Choice: TERMS-004: "To the extent the law allows, Ask Hava's total liability to you for any claim about the service is limited to $100. This limit does not apply to gross negligence, intentional misconduct, or anything the law does not allow us to limit." with no release of personal-injury claims and the disclaimers kept as statements of fact; TERMS-001 adds that the homeowner terms say the OK reply and the "By choosing" step are electronic signatures with the same effect as a signed paper (A.R.S. 44-7007, Arizona Electronic Transactions Act, and the federal E-SIGN Act); PICK-016 puts the "By choosing, you agree to the Terms" line directly beside the final choose button, in body-size text with an underlined link, under a three-line key-terms summary: "Free for you. You hire the business directly, and Ask Hava isn't part of that agreement. Ask Hava's liability to you is limited to $100, except for gross negligence or intentional misconduct." Reason: a $0 cap works as a release and invites a Phelps-type jury question; a real ceiling is the 1800 Ocotillo kind of clause (Memo 1, unverified case citations marked [M]); Berman's conspicuous-notice test for formation.
- **D-376** Question (Memo 1 R-11 and R-12, Part V.B items 2 and 8; Memo 4 Part F item 6; status report 5A item 6): what about pest control, sale-related inspections and warranties? Choice: LIC-012: pest control (and pre-emergent or advertised weed control) is switched OFF at launch and stays off until the Arizona Department of Agriculture answers in writing that relaying quotes for a fixed introduction fee is not pest-management advertising, soliciting or bid submission, the answer logged in a new decision; until then pest control is not in the sign-up category list (BIZ-021), a pest request closes early as a trade that isn't open (INT-022) and nothing advertises it (WEB-010); the catalog sets `pest_control` off with its reason, and W15 moves to quarterly bin washing (the same numbers). LIC-015: an inspection for a home being bought, sold or refinanced (the intake asks "Is this inspection for a home sale, purchase or refinance?") is declined with "Ask Hava: Sorry, Ask Hava can't take inspection requests for a home sale, purchase or refinance." and the catalog refuses buyer, pre-listing and refinance inspections; only inspections with no sale or refinance (for example a general condition check) stay. New LIC-042: no category, offer or feature ever covers a home warranty, protection plan, service contract or an Ask Hava guarantee, and Ask Hava itself gives no warranty. The catalog check fails if pest control is on without a logged written answer or if the home-inspection category takes a sale-related job type. Reason: A.R.S. 3-3615(A) presumption (Memo 4 rates the Department's argument Real, 30 to 40%); Regulation X lists sale-required inspections and home warranties as settlement services (RESPA section 8); warranties also raise the prime-contracting tax risk (Memo 3, LR 10-004).
- **D-377** Question (Memo 4 Finding 8 and Part F item 7; status report 5A item 7): the memos break their own vocabulary rule ("connect you with", "referral services, namely, matching"); what single description is used? Choice: new WORD-023: every place that describes the business (the site's how-it-works and about copy, the privacy policy, the homeowner and business terms, the texting campaign description, the city business-license form, the trademark application, the Stripe account and any insurance application) says it is "a quote-request service; the business the homeowner chooses pays Ask Hava a set introduction fee", in those words or with only "the homeowner" made "you"; no copy says "connect you with", "connects homeowners with", "referral network" or "lead generation"; the trademark description avoids "referral" and "matching" if an accurate ID Manual entry exists (Casey's choice, D-388). The phrase pattern for "connect you with" joins the one banned list in `scripts/trace_spec.py` (WORD-022), so the spec, catalog and wording scans all fail on it; TERMS-012 still quotes it to ban it. WORD-015 (Stripe) becomes "Quote-request service for homeowners. The business the homeowner chooses pays a set introduction fee." Reason: Memo 4 Finding 8: an honest description beats a euphemism, and contradictory public wording invites a carrier refusal or an ACFA concealment theory.
- **D-378** Question (Memo 4 Finding 5 and Part F item 8; status report 5A item 8): TERMS-024's "limited right to use" wording resembles the data rental ADOR taxed in LR 21-003; how is the fee framed? Choice: new TERMS-027: the business terms, the sign-up summary, receipts, the "Why was I charged?" page and the Stripe description describe the fee only as payment for the introduction service (passing along the request, collecting and showing the quotes, the homeowner's choice and the masked call line) and never as a "license", "licence", "data access", "access to", "lead", "right to use", "purchase of contact information" or "sale of information", and a document test fails on those words in business documents; TERMS-024 becomes "As part of the introduction, Ask Hava gives you the homeowner's first name and the job address so you can contact them about this job. Use them only for this job and follow-up about it. No marketing lists, no resale." Fees stay tax-included (FEE-008); the 7.6% reserve and the ruling-or-license choice stay Casey's (D-388). Reason: Memo 4 Finding 5 rates ADOR's data-rental argument Real (25 to 35%); wording is the cheap part of the defense.
- **D-379** Question (Memo 4 Finding 15; status report 5A item 9): the accessibility statement promises a phone contact, but the number is text-only (CON-018, CALL-020). Choice: A11Y-020: the statement gives the conformance target, known limits and a contact by text, email or the help page's form ("If you use a relay service, you can reach us by text or email."), usually answered within 2 days, the same as the HELP reply (CON-018), and never offers a phone line while the number is text-only. Reason: an unkept promise is Ask Hava's own statement (ACFA).
- **D-380** Question (status report 5A item 10; v10 business findings 4.1): picks arrive only by email and lapse; what stronger nudge, with no calls? Choice: BIZ-042 adds a pick-alert step for every person who gets picks (the owner and every "Confirm and billing" member), not only for after-hours: a "Send a sample pick" button sends "Ask Hava: Sample pick (not a real job). Confirm by <time>.", with "Turn on Do Not Disturb or Sleep the way your phone usually is, then send the test." and asks "Did your phone alert you?", showing steps that give Ask Hava email its own alert (iPhone Mail: VIP with VIP alerts and its own sound, allowed in Focus; Gmail on Android: a filter that labels Ask Hava mail with notifications on for that label, allowed in Do Not Disturb; Outlook: the equivalent), each step checked in T1 before it is written, and "Skip this and you may miss picks. A pick you don't confirm in 4 business hours may go to another business."; a Yes lasts 90 days (a setting), and none of this blocks activation. PICK-037: while no Confirm has happened, a reminder goes every business hour (a setting) after the notice (with the 4-hour default, at 1, 2 and 3 hours, the last titled "Last reminder"), each with the Hold link and its new end time, "Need more time? Hold for 2 more hours: confirm by <new time>.". CARD-022: a person may choose up to two open-cards times a day, and when a pick is waiting the email's subject becomes "Ask Hava: Pick waiting: confirm job <n> by <time>" with the pick listed first. BIZ-030's summary adds "Picks must be confirmed within 4 business hours (8 a.m. to 8 p.m., every day). If you check email only once or twice a day, add a backup address for someone who checks more often." Re-picking a lapsed business is not adopted this round (D-387). Reason: the v10 business findings (27 quitting rows on email-only picks); keeps no calls (BIZ-039) and no business texts (TXT-035).
- **D-381** Question (status report 5A item 11; v10 owner gap G1): the email provider's free plan (100 a day, 3,000 a month) runs out around month 7, and the 80% alert never reaches Casey's phone. Choice: OPS-004: the email-limit alert fires at 70% of the daily limit or 80% of the monthly limit (settings) and, unlike the other email alerts, pushes to Casey's phone at most once a day (OPS-029, OPS-035), saying "Ask Hava email use is at <p>% of the plan limit. Upgrade the email plan in the provider dashboard."; new OPS-038: above 90% of the daily limit (a setting), the open-cards emails (CARD-022), the monthly missed-cards email (BIZ-044) and the weekly digest wait until the limit resets, so job cards, picks, reminders, codes, receipts, card-fix and help emails still go; an email the provider refuses for a limit is never dropped: it is queued, retried after the reset, logged and shown in the notice log (BIZ-045), and any window that counts from "sent" (PICK-001, PICK-019) starts only when it is actually sent. The paid plan (Resend Pro, about $20 a month at the time of the research, price to be checked) is Casey's purchase before the projected month (D-388); SEC-025 says so. Reason: G1; no email silently lost; no purchase without Casey (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:61`).
- **D-382** Question (Memo 4 Part E, "Out-of-state service area", and Part F item 11): may the towns list grow past Arizona? Choice: INT-022 and CARD-002: the service-area towns list holds Arizona towns only; a town outside Arizona can't be added without a new legal review logged as a decision, and the settings check refuses a town whose state isn't AZ. Reason: California's contractor board treats referral companies that negotiate for contractors as contractors (Memo 1 II.C); Nevada and California law were not researched.
- **D-383** Question (Memo 4 Part E, "Minors", and Part F item 11): photos can carry GPS and device data. Choice: PRIV-014: every photo is re-encoded at upload with all metadata (EXIF, GPS, XMP, IPTC and maker notes) stripped before it is stored, screened by any check, sent to any AI step or placed on any card, and the uploaded bytes with metadata are never stored; PRIV-001's vault copy is the stripped one. Reason: Memo 4 Part E; homeowners' photos may show children and their home's location.
- **D-384** Question (status report 5B items 2, 3, 6 and 8): which of Casey's decisions get a default now? Choice: only the conservative defaults. (1) Safety voice call stays OFF: CALL-019's switch and its approval gate are unchanged, and new CALL-022 records Memo 2's six conditions as things that must be built and tested before the switch can be turned on whatever the approval: a narrowed voice trigger (gas phrases, carbon monoxide, smoke, fire, burning, spark and shock words only; never bare smell, odor, buzz, hot, alarm, flood, dizzy or heat-only matches), the triggering message within the last 30 minutes, the under-30-second script naming Ask Hava LLC with "Ask Hava is not an emergency service" and "press 9" to stop all calls, placed from a number other than the main number and reading out the main number, the app's suppression list checked before dialing (CON-035), the consent text's safety-call sentence ("If you report a danger like gas or smoke and we can't text, we may try one recorded call to this number. Don't wait for it: call 911.") added the same day the switch goes on and never before, and every call logged (Memo 2 I.E item 5). (2) AI quote summaries OFF (D-373). (3) Fee tiers shown to homeowners: new WEB-026: the homeowner terms and the how-it-works page say "The business you choose pays Ask Hava a set introduction fee ($25, $75 or $199, by quote size). You pay Ask Hava nothing.", and CON-034's cost answer adds "($25, $75 or $199, by quote size)". (4) Owner identity "Ask Hava LLC, a Lake Havasu City business", no personal name (D-366, unchanged). Reason: Casey's instruction to apply conservative defaults only; Memo 4 Finding 7 and Part F; Memo 1 R-9.
- **D-385** Question (Memo 2 VII.F items 5 and 6, cheap items not listed in Memo 4 Part F): which smaller Memo 2 fixes are taken? Choice: new SEC-028: every business and homeowner email carries a footer with Ask Hava LLC's postal address (a P.O. box or a registered private mailbox, never a home address), a "Manage email notices" link to the portal and "You get these emails because <your business signed up for job requests / you asked Ask Hava for quotes>.", and notice emails never contain promotions; new INT-038: a message or web chat that says or clearly shows the sender is under 18 gets one reply, "Ask Hava: Sorry, you must be 18 or older to use Ask Hava. Please ask a parent or adult to text us.", the request closes, and for a sender under 13 its data is deleted except the consent or opt-out log; CALL-014: the homeowner's code call names Ask Hava LLC and reads the Ask Hava main number as the callback number, never the number placing the call, and is placed to an opted-out number only when that person just asked for it on screen; new CALL-023: no recorded announcement plays to the homeowner on a bridged masked call, and the PIN prompt and the CALL-005 read-back play only on the calling side; CARD-007 and INT-035: the card field reads "Homeowner says gas company checked: yes / not yet / no answer" (Memo 1 R-4). Reason: 47 C.F.R. 64.1200(b) callback-number rule; CAN-SPAM footer as cheap insurance; COPPA actual-knowledge rule; a card field must not read as a fact Ask Hava vouches for.

## Fix round 13, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026, 10:53: "Take the report you had, the reports from Claude, from ChatGPT and Grok, and compose all of the fixes we need, and then put together a list of where we're at and what we need to do before we start building." and "I don't want you to run any simulations until you talk to me about them first." Inputs, all outside this repository: the deep dive of October 28, 2026 (`/home/claude/hhq/outputs/Ask_Hava_Deep_Dive_Oct28.md`, section 7A fixes A1 to A19 and section 7B decisions B1 to B17), the status report of the same day, the four AI legal-research memos (Memo 4 wins where they conflict), the outside research files (Grok and ChatGPT rounds 1 to 3) and the two lens packages. No simulation, persona pass or rehearsal was run. Where a fix rests on one of Casey's decisions, only the deep dive's recommended answer that is a reversible setting or default went in, marked "default pending Casey" here and in `docs/for-casey-review.md`; Casey's instruction named these as examples ("pest on for licensed businesses only; home inspections on with no fee for sale/refinance-related ones; safety call off; AI on scrubbed text disclosed; short consent texts"). Research done this round: the ROC class list (azroc.gov, "License Classification Requirements", the 6.8.20 list) and A.A.C. R4-9-103 and R4-9-104 (Cornell LII), checked October 28, 2026.

- **D-391** Question (deep dive A1, section 3 item 1): the Twilio question in `docs/for-casey-review.md` says businesses get "an automated phone call when they are picked", but since D-344 no automated call ever goes to a business (BIZ-039). Choice: the line now reads "Businesses get job requests and picks by email and on our website. We never call businesses, and we send them no texts unless you tell us that's allowed." No spec change. Reason: the question must describe the model actually run before Casey sends it.
- **D-392** Question (deep dive A2, section 3B part 1): the catalog lists one narrow residential ROC class per trade, so dual (CR-), general (B, B-3, KB-1, KB-2) and broader licensees would get no job cards, and "Broader classes are listed when the ROC scope table is loaded" was never done. Choice: new `docs/catalog/license_map.yaml`, checked by `scripts/check_catalog.py`, maps each license class the plan relies on to the category job types it may receive, citing the ROC class list and A.A.C. R4-9-103 (residential, last amended effective November 5, 2017) and R4-9-104 (dual), saved with the date checked; new LIC-043 states the inheritance rules once: a class gets a job type only where the map lists it; a dual CR- class includes the residential class R4-9-104 pairs it with (for example CR-11 includes R-11, CR-37 includes R-37R Plumbing, CR-79 includes R-39); KB-1 and KB-2 include the B General Residential scope and KA-5 and KA-6 include B-5 and B-6; B and B-3 cover the general repair and remodel job types (handyman work, painting, roof repair, insulation, gutters, epoxy floors, paver patios and walkways) and never electrical, plumbing, air conditioning, boiler, pool or spa job types, which R4-9-103 says they must subcontract; R-61 and R-62 cover handyman job types with their class limits ($50,000 and $5,000, new LIC-045); a row marked unclear is never inherited until a later decision confirms it; and a business never gets a job type its class doesn't cover, whatever it ticks (CARD-001); new LIC-044: commercial-only classes (A, B-1, B-2 and every C- class) cover no home job types, so a business holding only those is told at sign-up "Your license is commercial-only. Ask Hava sends home jobs only, so it can't send you job requests. A residential or dual (R-, CR-, B or KB) license can." and is not activated; LIC-035 now reads the R-37R and R-39R subclassification named on the state record (R4-9-103 lists them; "R-37R Plumbing" is general plumbing, "R-37R Sewers, Drains and Pipe Laying" drains only, "R-39R Air Conditioning and Refrigeration" cooling only, "R-39R Warm Air Heating, Evaporative Cooling and Ventilating" heating and evaporative cooling, an unknown subclass nothing until mapped); and `hvac_repair` gains the job type "evaporative (swamp) cooler repair". Reason: Casey's 10:42 message (#3) and the deep dive; R4-9-104 says dual classifications "combine commercial and residential contracting licenses in one license", and R4-9-103 lists what B and B-3 must subcontract.
- **D-393** Question (deep dive A3, section 3B part 1): business capability setup has a checklist (BIZ-004) but no pre-fill from the license, no job-size limit, no recurring answer and no re-check when a license changes. Choice: new BIZ-049: after the license lookup (or city-list match) the job-type checklist arrives pre-ticked with every job type the license covers (LIC-043), grouped by trade; the owner can untick any and can add only no-license job types; then four short questions: towns served (BIZ-010), the largest job the business will quote (no limit, $1,500, $5,000 or a typed amount; a card is skipped when a homeowner answer gives a size or budget band entirely above it (CARD-001), and otherwise the quote form warns before a quote above it is sent), recurring work yes or no (a business answering no gets no recurring job types), and after-hours emergencies (BIZ-007); the same steps work as checkboxes on one page or as a guided sequence with one question per screen on a phone, and the sign-up summary reads the answers back; new BIZ-050: when a daily license list shows a class added, removed or changed, the map is re-applied: removed job types drop at once with an email saying which and why, newly covered ones are offered by email and never ticked automatically; BIZ-004 points to BIZ-049; INT-032 adds that when no single business's license covers every candidate job type of a request, the homeowner is offered a split into one request per trade, and a card never goes to a business that covers only part of a job (CARD-001). Reason: Casey's 10:42 message (#3); the deep dive's section 3B.
- **D-394** Question (deep dive A4, section 3B part 2): intake has 2 free-text questions per category and multiple-choice card fields for 8 categories only, so job cards are hard to quote. Choice: new `docs/catalog/question_bank.yaml`, checked by `scripts/check_catalog.py`: every category that can be switched on (pest control included, D-402) has a versioned set of 3 to 6 questions in the order that most improves a business's ability to quote, multiple choice wherever possible, each with an English template and a Spanish draft marked for native-speaker review, a `card_field` flag (multiple-choice answers only; free text is never a card field), an optional `ask_if` condition on an earlier answer and a `source` line ("draft from trade practice; confirm with 2 or 3 businesses in recruiting"), with Havasu-specific sets: boats (engine make, outboard, inboard or I/O, year band, hours band, symptom, in the water, on a lift or on a trailer, length band), canvas and upholstery and trailer work as boat job types, pool and spa (pool or spa, size band, surface, symptom, equipment brand, water condition), car, truck and RV detailing (the category renamed "Car, truck and RV detailing", with RV detailing as a job type), and vacation and seasonal homes (home watch visit schedule and tasks, turnover cleaning bedrooms, linens and timing, "who lets the business in"); LIC-040 now points to the bank for every category; new INT-039: intake asks the bank's questions for the chosen job type, one at a time, after the INT-030 timing question and before the name-and-address question, skips a question whose answer the homeowner already gave, accepts "not sure" on every question, and never asks more than 6; new INT-042: the bank holds trade-specific safety prompts that are sent as fixed templates when an answer matches (for example a boat fuel smell gets "Ask Hava: Don't start the engine or use switches near a fuel smell. Get everyone off the boat and call 911 if anyone feels unwell." from English and Spanish templates marked for the safety desk check), the INT-002 keyword safety check still running first on every message; a question is added or changed only by a catalog change logged as a decision, and a business's answer in recruiting that a question changes its price is logged in the question's `source`. Reason: Casey's 10:42 message (#4); every question is a fixed template, so no AI-written text reaches a homeowner (AI-006, TXT-004).
- **D-395** Question (deep dive A4, section 3B parts 3, 5 and 7; section 2, "Intake"): how sure must routing be, and does the homeowner see what goes out? Choice: new INT-040: the keyword rules (and, where AI is allowed, the AI reading, D-412) each give a trade and job type with a confidence level; when both agree at high confidence (a setting) intake goes on; when they disagree or confidence is low, the INT-013 trade question and then the INT-014 job-type question are asked; still unsure after both, the card goes to every business covering any candidate job type in that trade, marked "Job type not certain", and never to a business outside that trade; the license class is never guessed (AI-017); new INT-041: before cards go out, one summary text (or web-chat message) "Ask Hava: Here's what we'll send for request <n>: <trade>, <up to 3 key answers>. Reply OK to send it, or EDIT to change it." with no contact detail in it, an EDIT reply asking which answer to change, and no reply within 2 hours (a setting) sending the cards as summarized, while a job chosen as an emergency skips the summary; new OPS-040: a monthly owner-portal report lists cards that got no quotes and quotes saying "need more info", by category and question set, and a golden set of about 20 sample requests per launch-open trade (`docs/catalog/intake_golden.yaml`, written by a build task before the routing code) is run on every catalog change, with a proposed target of at least 90% reaching the right job type with no human, the result shown in the report. Reason: the deep dive's section 3B; the summary step catches a wrong trade before businesses see the card.
- **D-396** Question (deep dive A5, section 5; B2, default pending Casey): the catalog switches on 36 categories, but the plan is a pilot of 3 or 4 trades. Choice: each category gets a `launch_open` flag (LIC-046, new): only launch-open categories show "Open now" (BIZ-021) and take homeowner requests at Gate C; every other enabled category shows "Coming later" and takes business sign-ups to the waitlist; the default pending Casey is the deep dive's B2 answer: heating and cooling repair and replacement, electrical, handyman, pool and spa repair, weekly pool cleaning and pest control (D-402); a launch-open trade opens to homeowners only once it has 2 or more active businesses (a setting, OPS-006), and closes to new requests (with the INT-022 early close and a waitlist note) if it falls below; switching the flag is a logged settings change (LIC-002); new OPS-039: the owner portal has one "Pilot scorecard" page, built from the ledger and the audit log, showing per trade and in total, for the current week and the pilot to date, each measure in the deep dive's section 5 table with its proposed go and stop lines as settings: requests with 1 or more quotes within 24 hours, median time to first quote, picks per request with quotes, picks confirmed, average fee per paid introduction, the free share of introductions, refunds and bank disputes, businesses paying again within 60 days, Casey's minutes per paid introduction (from a weekly minutes entry on the page) and homeowner complaints per 20 jobs; each measure shows green, amber or red against its lines and the weekly digest (OPS-030) carries the summary; the scorecard decides nothing on its own. Reason: Casey's plan is a narrow pilot (status report 5B item 9, D-388 item 5); the deep dive's section 5.
- **D-397** Question (deep dive A6, section 3 item 14): "Sent to <N>" doesn't say what N counts. Choice: TXT-032 and CARD-019: N is the number of businesses whose job-card email the email provider accepted (or whose card text was accepted), counted when the first homeowner text goes; later quote-count updates subtract any business whose every card notice hard-bounced; the first text adds "Not every business answers."; and the first text follows the voice guide (D-406): "Ask Hava: Your request went to <N> local businesses that take <trade> jobs. Not every business answers. Quotes are open until <time, day>, Arizona time. We'll text you when the first one comes in." Reason: a count must be true when it is said (WORD-016, the voice guide's rule 9).
- **D-398** Question (deep dive A7, section 3 item 12; B16, default pending Casey): the English consent text is 5 segments and the Spanish one 13 (UCS-2 because of á, í, ó and ú). Choice: CON-031: the English text is the D-371 text without its last sentence (the HELP reply-time promise, which stays in the HELP reply, CON-018): 558 characters, GSM-7, 4 segments; the Spanish text is the deep dive's short GSM-7 version (532 characters, 4 segments, "presupuesto" for "quote" because it has no accent), marked for native-speaker review; both counted by a GSM-7 segment counter in the test, and checked again in the provider's segment calculator before registration. Every element carriers and the memos asked for stays: who, not a contractor, free for you, the fee range, automated, frequency, rates, 18 or older, OK means agreeing to the Terms, the Terms and Privacy links, STOP and HELP. Reason: fewer segments cost less and are less likely to be split or filtered; the reply-time promise belongs in HELP.
- **D-399** Question (deep dive A8, section 2 quote types, section 3A rule 2; B7, default pending Casey): hourly quotes and setup charges are undefined, and a trip-fee quote in HVAC replacement costs $199 for a visit that isn't priced yet. Choice: PICK-002 lists the quote types in plain words: fixed price, price range, "site visit needed" (a trip fee plus an estimate on site, the existing trip-fee type) and, in a recurring category, a recurring price per visit, week, month, quarter or year; new FEE-043: an hourly quote must give an estimated number of hours (or a range of hours) and is then treated as a range from rate times the low estimate to rate times the high one (FEE-003); with no estimate it is treated as a site-visit-needed quote; new FEE-044: a one-time setup charge in a recurring quote is shown as the business wrote it and is never added to the fee basis, which stays 4 months of the recurring price, capped at $75 (FEE-039); FEE-004: a site-visit-needed quote pays the category's set tier, which is $25 for every category by default and can be set no higher than $75 (default pending Casey; the catalog check fails above $75); worked examples W34 to W39 pin a range crossing both $500 and $1,500, hourly quotes with and without an estimate, a setup charge on a weekly service and the site-visit tier. Ranges crossing $500 or $1,500 keep the midpoint rule (FEE-003), already shown on the quote form before submitting (PICK-039) and watched for gaming (FEE-033). Reason: the deep dive's section 3A rule 2: paying $199 to visit an unpriced job is the likeliest place a business refuses to confirm.
- **D-400** Question (deep dive A9, section 3A rule 8; B6): 17% of picks lapsed in simulation [sim], because businesses read picks by email. Choice: new PICK-047: on the quote page's pick review step (PICK-017) the homeowner may name one backup quote, "If <Business> doesn't confirm in time, go to:", listing the other choosable quotes in arrival order with none preselected and "No backup" as the default; when the pick lapses (JOB-013), the business declines or becomes ineligible, the pick moves at once to the backup if it is still choosable, that business gets a normal pick notice (PICK-037) and the homeowner gets "Ask Hava: <Business> didn't confirm in time, so your backup choice, <Business 2>, has your pick now. They have until <time> to confirm."; one backup per pick, never chosen by Ask Hava, changeable until the lapse; with no backup PICK-046 applies. Reason: the homeowner's own second choice keeps the job moving with no call and no ranking.
- **D-401** Question (deep dive A18, section 3A rule 8; B6, default pending Casey): what else keeps picks from lapsing without calls? Choice: new PICK-048: each quote form offers, to the owner or a "Confirm and billing" member with a saved card, an unticked "Auto-confirm and pay if picked" box with its own authorization ("I authorize Ask Hava LLC to charge my saved card $<fee> for this quote's introduction if the homeowner picks it, without another tap. I can untick this until the homeowner picks."), stored with the quote; the box is shown only while the owner-portal record "Stripe written answer on charges without the business present" is filed (date, reference, the answer saying yes); a pick of such a quote is confirmed at once by one off-session charge of the shown fee with its own idempotency key, a success releasing details as PICK-022 says and a failure or required authentication falling back to the normal Confirm step and window (PICK-019); FEE-014 adds this as the only off-session charge; BIZ-041: the installable business web app with push notifications is built in the pilot build, as an optional extra channel a person turns on, with email always kept as the fallback (default pending Casey; it stays the last build stage, so dropping it removes only its tasks). Calls to businesses stay off (BIZ-039). Reason: an owner who ticked auto-confirm is confirmed the moment the homeowner picks; push reaches a phone faster than email.
- **D-402** Question (deep dive A10, section 3A rule 5 and the pest deep dive; B3, default pending Casey): pest control is a licensed trade like any other under Casey's one rule. Choice: LIC-012: pest control (including pre-emergent weed control) is switched on for Arizona Department of Agriculture pest-management business licensees only: homeowner-started requests only, listed plainly in "What we cover" like every other trade (WEB-023), never in a pest-specific ad, landing page, tagline or seasonal post (WEB-010), with the license number on every quote card (LIC-034); termite and pre-emergent job types go only to a licensee whose record shows the matching category, an unclear record getting general pest job types only (LIC-043); the short written question to the Department is drafted in `docs/for-casey-review.md` for Casey to send the same week, and if the Department answers that Ask Hava needs a license, pest control is switched off and replaced by a fee-free pointer to the Department's license search; the catalog check now requires pest control, when on, to be licensed-only and not advertised; BIZ-021 and WEB-020 follow. Reason: A.R.S. 3-3615(A)'s presumption is rebuttable ("presumed", not "conclusively presumed"), and a relay that sends cards only to Department licensees supports the Department's purpose; Memo 4 rated the outcome uncertain only if the Department chose to act.
- **D-403** Question (deep dive A10, section 3A rule 6 and the inspection deep dive; B4, default pending Casey): sale, purchase and refinance inspections are declined, but RESPA section 8 (12 C.F.R. 1024.14) bites only if Ask Hava accepts a fee. Choice: LIC-015: home inspections go only to Board-certified inspectors; intake still asks "Is this inspection for a home sale, purchase or refinance?"; a yes is taken as a sale-related inspection, a fee-free job type, as is a wood-destroying insect (termite) report for a sale or loan; new FEE-045: a pick of a fee-free job type charges no introduction fee (a $0 ledger entry of kind "fee-free job type", never using a free first win), the Confirm page says "No introduction fee for this job." and the card says the same; the catalog lists buyer, pre-listing and refinance inspections as job types in `fee_free_job_types`, and the check fails if a sale-related inspection is ever charged; worked example W39 pins it. Reason: with no "fee, kickback or other thing of value" accepted, section 8(a) has nothing to apply to, and inspectors quoting their own fee is not the issue.
- **D-404** Question (deep dive A11): desk research on garage doors and junk removal. Choice: no change; both stay off (LIC-005). Garage doors: A.A.C. R4-9-103 names no garage or overhead door work in any class; the nearest are R-7 Carpentry ("metal doors or door frames"), B, B-3 and R-61, so the class can't be confirmed from the rule text; small no-permit repairs could go to handymen under $1,000 once a class is chosen (later, D20 in the deep dive). Junk removal: the text of Lake Havasu City Code chapter 8.04 (solid waste) could not be read from an allowed source in this round; Casey or a later round asks the city clerk in writing whether a private business may haul junk for pay. Reason: AI-017 and LIC-003: a category that isn't confirmed stays off.
- **D-405** Question (deep dive A12, section 3 item 6): the pitch file still says "no bidding wars", "a text-based quote service", "Text Hava" and promises a phone help line. Reason: WORD-017, WORD-021, A11Y-020, D-379.
- **D-406** Question (deep dive A13, section 4): what voice do texts and emails use? Choice: new `docs/voice.md` holds the deep dive's ten rules and before-and-after examples as a style guide only (nobody is named, quoted or imitated; every spec rule wins over style); new WORD-024 makes the rules a spec wording rule: short sentences of about 15 words or fewer, the situation named first and about the situation, never the person, no blame, one next step at the end, the outcome rather than the system, the likely worry answered, calm and never urgent (no "hurry", "don't miss out", "act now", "limited time" or "last chance"), homeowner and business registers, numbers only from the system, and safety texts exempt and unchanged; the rewrites go into TXT-032 (D-397), JOB-010, PICK-045, PICK-046, INT-019, PICK-037 (the pick email's body and last reminder), BIZ-030 (a plain "when the fee is owed" line added; the TERMS-026 framing stays), BIZ-044 and PICK-026; the HELP reply (CON-018) is unchanged because carriers need its contact details and the legal-document check uses its reply-time sentence; and a test checks the pressure words on every template. The communication persona's facts are not used (quiet hours, "Text Hava", refunds, Stage 4 calls, request IDs, Casey's name). Reason: Casey's guidance: plain, calm, sales-savvy speaking style, without impersonating or naming anyone.
- **D-407** Question (deep dive A14, section 3 item 15): homeowner texts mix "request <n>" and "job <n>". Choice: new WORD-025: every homeowner text, email and page calls it "request <n>" and every business one "job <n>", the number being the same; a template test checks the audience and the word. Reason: one name per audience avoids confusion.
- **D-408** Question (deep dive A15, section 3 item 13): the ChatGPT round 3 Stripe draft calls Ask Hava an "introduction service" and doesn't mention the release. The business the homeowner chooses pays a set introduction fee.", says the homeowner's first name and job address are released only after the payment succeeds, and adds a fourth question about the optional "auto-confirm and pay if picked" charge without the business present (D-401). No spec change. Reason: WORD-015, WORD-023.
- **D-409** Question (deep dive A16; B12, default pending Casey): no owner name online costs some trust [sim]. Choice: WEB-021 and WEB-022: the "Who runs Ask Hava" blocks add, once each exists, a link to Ask Hava LLC's public record on the Arizona Corporation Commission site and the Lake Havasu City business license number, each read from a setting and left out while empty; still no personal name online (WORD-021); Casey may use his first name in person when recruiting. - **D-410** Question (deep dive A17; Memo 3 section 2, Memo 4 Finding 5): the money view could read as profit while the sales-tax question is open. Choice: new FEE-046: the owner money view shows, beside the ledger totals, a "sales tax set-aside" line of 7.6% (a setting) of fees collected while the ADOR question is open, and a manual expenses list (date, vendor, amount, category, note), entered by Casey and never mixed into the ledger (FEE-028), with "collected", "set aside", "expenses" and "left" shown separately and a monthly CSV. Reason: cash isn't profit; the set-aside follows Memo 3's Path A lean.
- **D-411** Question (deep dive A19, section 3 item 16; Casey's 10:46 message): the safety numbers are marked "verify before launch" and were listed as Casey's task. Choice: INT-036: every entry in `app/messaging/safety_contacts.toml` holds its official source URL and the date it was last verified, and verification is a build step, not a Casey task: a launch-checklist task fetches each entry's official page, confirms the number and hours there, and records the date; an entry it can't confirm is removed from the file (texts leave it out, as now) and listed in `docs/for-casey-review.md`; `Ci` fails when any entry lacks a source URL or a verified date, or when the verified date is older than 90 days (a setting) at Gate C; after launch a reminder goes in the weekly digest every 90 days and the same task re-runs. Reason: Casey's instruction that safety phone numbers are a built-in pre-launch check.
- **D-412** Question (deep dive section 3A rule 12; B5, default pending Casey): no AI runs at all until a zero-retention agreement is signed (AI-008). Choice: default pending Casey: AI may run on scrubbed text (AI-002, AI-003; never names, phone numbers, emails, street numbers or photos) before a zero-retention agreement, under the provider's standard commercial API terms, once the account checklist records those terms (URL, date checked, that API inputs are not used for training, and the retention period) and the privacy policy carries the matching AI-018 wording that day; the owner-portal switch "AI on scrubbed text" is otherwise off and launch works on the fixed question bank with no AI (D-394); AI still only classifies and picks the next fixed question, never writes a homeowner text (AI-006); photos stay off job cards until zero retention is signed (CARD-013, unchanged); AI-001, AI-008 and AI-018 follow, and `docs/constitution.md` section 7 records the change. The changes file allows AI "only to the zero-retention Claude API" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:58`); Casey's instruction of this round names "AI on scrubbed text disclosed" as a default to apply, and this logs both and the choice (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:539`). Reason: the scrubber already removes the personal details zero retention protects; zero retention stays the goal.
- **D-413** Question (prelaunch research Part 3; deep dive section 2, "Masked calls"): masked calls may show "Spam Likely". Choice: new CALL-024: each masked pool number is registered in the texting provider's Trust Hub for A-level call attestation (SHAKEN/STIR) with the business profile, carries the caller name "Ask Hava" where the carrier allows it, and is registered with the free carrier-reputation portal the provider points to; branded calling stays optional; the who's-calling text (TXT-027) already names the number; and the pilot scorecard notes answered-call rates (OPS-039). Reason: the research's lowest-cost steps; no new vendor.
- **D-414** Question (deep dive 7B): what happens to the other decisions? Choice: recorded as defaults pending Casey with no spec change, because the spec already does them: B8 keep $25 / $75 / $199 for the pilot; B9 arbitration stays off (TERMS-002; the optional section stays off); B12 no owner name online (D-409); B14 keep PICK-026; B15 safety voice call stays off (CALL-019, CALL-022). Left open for Casey with the recommended answer in `docs/for-casey-review.md` and `docs/PREBUILD.md`: B1 Gate P (approve once this round is merged; build the pilot cut first), B10 sales tax path (ADOR ruling plus the 7.6% set-aside before the first fee), B11 trademark timing (Class 35 right after launch), B13 insurance (tech E&O with cyber, bound before the first paid introduction) and B17 Spanish (one paid native-speaker review before Spanish goes live). Reason: BUILD_BRIEF section 16 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:542`).
- **D-415** Question: which tasks build this? Choice: one test-design row per change (TD rows added in sections 7.1, 7.2, 7.4, 7.5, 7.7, 7.8, 7.10, 7.12, 7.13 and 7.16); existing tasks take the new requirements where they own the module; new tasks: T153 (license map and question bank loader checks, stage 1), T154 (intake golden sets, stage 1, before routing code), T155 (capability setup, BIZ-049, BIZ-050), T156 (question-bank intake and the summary step), T157 (backup choice and auto-confirm), T158 (pilot scorecard and money view), T159 (safety contacts verification, launch checklist), T160 (business push web app, the last build stage, default pending Casey) and T161 (the voice, numbering, consent-segment and record-link checks); T058 takes FEE-043 to FEE-045, T034, T038, T040, T046, T047, T070, T102 and T025 take the matching test-design rows, T141 waits for T159, and each stage's cleanup task waits for the new tasks in its stage; prompts regenerated from the template. Reason: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:489`; every requirement must be owned by a task (`scripts/validate_tasks.py`).
- **D-416** Question: where is the to-do list? Choice: new `docs/PREBUILD.md`, copied from `/home/claude/hhq/outputs/Ask_Hava_Master_Plan_Oct28.md`, is the one list of where the plan stands and what happens before the build: every future change updates it, and an item closed there is reopened only with new evidence named in it. Reason: Casey: "I just want to really make sure that we are not working in circles."
- **D-417** Question: how are pest-control licenses handled, and is a letter sent to the Department of Agriculture? Choice: no letter; a pest business attests at sign-up that it holds a Department pest-management license and gives the number (and attests to any termite or weed-control category), the license is not checked by Ask Hava, and every pest quote card says the business told us it holds the license and that Ask Hava has not independently verified it, with a link to the Department's search (LIC-012, LIC-034, LIC-047; PREBUILD item 10 dropped, B3 decided). ROC licenses are still checked automatically (LIC-007). Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "We're not going to send letters out to the Department of Agriculture. If pest control requires a license, then they just have to attest or say that they have a license when they sign up. But then when we send out jobs, say that these businesses have informed us they have a license, however, they haven't been independently verified or something along those lines."
- **D-418** Question: are the pending defaults confirmed? Choice: yes; B2, B4 to B9, B12 and B14 to B16 in `docs/PREBUILD.md` section 4 are confirmed as written (B3 was decided in D-417). Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026, after the pest change: "I think everything else is okay."

**Spec changes from fix round 13:** listed in `docs/PREBUILD.md` section 2 and in each decision above.

## Consent by texting in, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026, by voice: "I think if they text us, that's implied that they want to talk to us... if you say that okay to continue or something like that, somebody might not read that and then stop talking. So I think their reaching out is their consent, and then we just need to notify them if they want to stop receiving messages or maybe like after they send off their quote [request], a confirmation that they want us to reply with additional quotes, by text, email, or both. That goes to the link that has all the quote information." And on the pick page: "that doesn't need to be like blasted all over a page. That would just be in the terms that they would agree to." No simulation, persona pass or rehearsal was run. Research basis, AI research and not legal advice: the CTIA Messaging Principles and Best Practices treat a consumer who texts a business first as having opted in to a conversational reply about that request; the FCC treats a consumer's own text as prior express consent to informational, non-marketing replies about it (Ask Hava sends no marketing, TXT-008); the opt-in confirmation still carries the brand, the program, message frequency, rates, HELP and STOP, and the Terms and Privacy links.

- **D-419** Question: does a homeowner have to reply OK before Ask Hava keeps talking? Choice: no. The homeowner's first inbound text, or starting a web chat, is the opt-in for automated informational texts about their own request (CON-005); nothing waits for or asks for a reply of OK or YES. The first reply (still called the consent text, so BUILD_BRIEF's "consent text first on every path", `BUILD_BRIEF.md:445`, holds) carries the required disclosures in as few characters as possible and moves the conversation on: "Ask Hava: Got it. Next, a few quick questions. Ask Hava is not a contractor; you hire the business you pick directly. Automated texts about your request. Msg frequency varies. Msg & data rates may apply. Reply STOP to opt out, HELP for help. Terms: askhava.com/terms Privacy: askhava.com/privacy" (295 characters, GSM-7, 2 segments), and the first intake question follows at once as its own text; the Spanish version (303 characters, GSM-7, 2 segments) is built and tested but sent only while Spanish conversations are on (D-427). The emergency keyword check still runs first on every message (INT-002), so the safety or heat-safety text still goes before the first reply (CON-001). The fee line ("Free for you; the business you choose pays Ask Hava a set fee of $25 to $199, by quote size.") leaves the first text and stays in the SMS terms and on the help page; the fee is shown again beside every pick (PICK-016, WEB-026). The "18 or older" rule stays in the Terms, the web chat start and INT-038. A phone number typed in web chat is texted only after a Twilio Verify code proves the person holds it (CON-025), which replaces "until it replies OK" against SMS pumping and wrong numbers. Changed: CON-002, CON-003, CON-004, CON-005, CON-025, CON-026, CON-027, CON-031, INT-019, INT-025, INT-026, TXT-034, WORD-002, and INT-041, whose summary now says "It goes out in 2 hours. Reply EDIT to change it, or SEND to send it now." so no reply is needed there either; CON-006 withdrawn; `scripts/check_legal_docs.py` now requires "Msg frequency varies", the new first-reply line and "Texting Ask Hava, or starting a chat on askhava.com, is your OK to get automated texts about your own request." in place of "Reply OK to agree..." (its tests updated); the SMS terms, homeowner terms and privacy policy drafts updated. This is Casey's own decision, so it overrides the changes file's "Reply OK to agree to the Terms and start" (`sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:213`), which stays unedited because `sources/` is read-only; the requirements cite this decision instead. Supersedes the reply-OK parts of D-021, D-051, D-095, D-127, D-237, D-371 and D-398 (B16). Reason: Casey's decision above; a required OK loses people who never read it.
- **D-420** Question: with no reply-OK contract, when are the homeowner Terms accepted? Choice: on the private quote page, by picking (clickwrap): directly beside the Pick button, "By picking, you agree to the Terms" with an underlined link, and the acceptance is logged with its timestamp, the terms version and the request ID (TERMS-016); a pick by text ("<number> YES", PICK-015) is accepted the same way because every text that offers it ends "By picking, you agree to the Terms: askhava.com/terms" and the pick is logged with the channel "text". Changed: PICK-016, TERMS-001; the homeowner terms' electronic-signature section now names only the pick. Reason: Casey's decision above; the pick is the first moment anything binding happens.
- **D-421** Question: should the $100 liability cap be shown beside the Pick button? Choice: no. Beside the Pick button only "By picking, you agree to the Terms" (linked) appears; the "short version" line and the key-terms summary with the cap are removed from the pick page and from the homeowner terms' top; the cap stays, word for word, in homeowner terms section 10 under the bold heading "Limit of liability", so it is easy to find from the link. Changed: PICK-016, TERMS-004 (the cap's place), the homeowner terms draft and `scripts/check_legal_docs.py` (the PICK-016 key-terms phrase is no longer required in the homeowner terms). Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "that doesn't need to be like blasted all over a page. That would just be in the terms that they would agree to."
- **D-422** Question: how does the homeowner choose how quote updates arrive? Choice: TXT-018 replaces "Want updates by email too?": after the INT-041 summary step, once the job cards go out and the TXT-032 text is sent, one question is asked once: "Ask Hava: How should we send quote updates? 1) Text 2) Email 3) Both. Every update links to your private quote page."; no answer, or an unclear one, keeps text (the default) and the question is never asked again; 2 or 3 asks one follow-up, "What email should we use? We never share it with businesses.", checked for format and confirmed by the first email's delivery, a hard bounce switching updates back to text with a note on the quote page; email updates follow the existing email rules (SEC-028 footer, an unsubscribe link in each update email, one suppression list across text, email and portal, CON-035); safety texts, codes and direct replies to the homeowner's own texts still go by text. Reason: Casey's decision above ("by text, email, or both. That goes to the link that has all the quote information").
- **D-423** Question: is a separate opt-out notice needed? Choice: no; the first reply already says "Reply STOP to opt out, HELP for help", and the STOP and revocation rules stay as they are: FCC 26-67 (effective November 25, 2026) handling (CON-032), one stop list across text, email and portal (CON-035) and the bare STOP confirmation (CON-009). No spec change beyond D-419. Reason: Casey's "we just need to notify them if they want to stop receiving messages".
- **D-424** Question: what does the Twilio question say now? Choice: the short version in `docs/for-casey-review.md` and `/home/claude/hhq/outputs/send_drafts/Twilio_question.md`: subject "Eligibility check before A2P 10DLC registration – customer care texts"; the homeowner texts first, the first reply carries the disclosures, only that homeowner's own request is texted, no marketing, businesses get job requests by email only and are never texted; two questions: eligibility as Customer Care (or conversational) with the inbound text as the opt-in, and whether the privacy sentence "Your first name and address go only to the one business you choose" triggers errors 30871 or 30951; signed "Ask Hava (LLC being formed)" with no personal name (WORD-021). The business-texting questions are gone. PREBUILD item 6 sends this version. No spec change. Reason: businesses are email-only; a shorter question gets a clearer answer.
- **D-425** Question (PREBUILD B11): trademark. Choice: skip for now; revisit if Ask Hava grows beyond Lake Havasu City or another "Ask Hava" appears. No spec change; the WORD-023 rule still governs any later application's wording. Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "We can skip trademark for now."
- **D-426** Question (PREBUILD B13): insurance. Choice: later, after the business is fully working; it is no longer listed as a step before the first paid introduction. No spec change. Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "We'll [skip] insurance for now. We'll work all that out after we have a fully working business."
- **D-427** Question (PREBUILD B17): Spanish. Casey doesn't speak Spanish and knows no one who does: "your recommendation is going to have to be best". Choice: launch English-only for conversations. New TXT-039: an owner-portal setting "Spanish conversations", off at launch; while it is off every homeowner text, web-chat message and email comes from the English template even when the message reads as Spanish, and business notices are English (BIZ-018's Spanish choice hidden), except that the Spanish safety texts stay on: the safety, heat-safety and other emergency texts (INT-004, INT-031, INT-034, INT-037, INT-042 prompts), plus the AYUDA reply and the Spanish opt-out confirmation, which carriers expect for Spanish keywords; Spanish opt-out words are always honored (CON-008). Before launch each Spanish text that stays on is checked by back-translation through two separate AIs (ChatGPT and Grok), each translating the Spanish back into English on its own; the check passes only when both back-translations match the English template's meaning, and the date and result are logged in `docs/decisions.md`; a text that fails is fixed and checked again. The Spanish conversation templates are still built and tested (TXT-001, TXT-003). The setting can be turned on only after a paid professional translation review (about $100) of every Spanish template is recorded in the owner portal (date, reviewer, template versions). Changed: TXT-002, TXT-039 (new), CON-026, TERMS-018, BIZ-018. Reason: the safety texts matter most and are short enough to verify cheaply; everything else waits for a real review.
- **D-428** Question: which tasks build this, and what stays open? Choice: test-design rows TD-CON-01, TD-CON-02, TD-CON-10, TD-CON-11, TD-CON-14, TD-CON-15, TD-CON-18, TD-CON-23, TD-INT-08, TD-INT-10, TD-INT-23, TD-TXT-01, TD-TXT-06, TD-PICK-30, TD-WORD-05, TD-TERMS-01 and journey J1 changed, and TD-CON-24 (no reply-OK anywhere; emergency check first), TD-TXT-23 (the updates question) and TD-TXT-24 (Spanish conversations setting) added; T038 builds consent by the first text, T026 the Spanish conversations setting (TXT-039), T156 the updates question (TXT-018, moved from T047), T055 the pick-page terms line (PICK-016), T103 the web-chat opt-in and code check (CON-025), T161 the 2-segment and no-reply-OK scans (CON-031, CON-004), and T111 and T112 the drafts; prompts regenerated. B10 (sales tax) stays open while ChatGPT researches it. `docs/PREBUILD.md` and the master plan list the change. Reason: every requirement keeps a task and a test row (`scripts/validate_tasks.py`).

## Fix round 14: packaged jobs, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026, after the walkthrough review (relayed in `docs/handoff/HANDOFF.md` section 5): the job going to the business and the quotes going back should be "as well packaged as possible", so homeowners feel the service knows exactly what they need and businesses get a pre-packaged job they'd otherwise pay a well-versed receptionist to gather. On own-or-rent: "If you call a plumber, they don't ask you if you own the home." On heat: "they are well aware the AC went out and it's hot." His four answers on the rework plan (`docs/handoff/rework-plan.md`), Oct 28, 2026: photos "Photos now, AI later"; address "She OKs it at the pick"; tax ruling "Ruling on the later list"; 911 list "Fire-truck list only". Then: "Do everything that you recommend for all the following questions." New inputs: the Grok trade knowledge base (`docs/catalog/trade_kb/`, AI research, not legal advice) and the ChatGPT sales-tax report (AI research, not tax advice; summarized in `docs/for-casey-review.md`). No simulation, persona pass or rehearsal was run. These choices reverse earlier rules on purpose; each reversal is named below.

- **D-429** Question: what is the goal of this round? Choice: every requirement is re-read through Casey's "as well packaged as possible" lens; the plan is `docs/handoff/rework-plan.md` (homeowner H1 to H14, business B1 to B7, owner O1 to O4, AI A1 to A5, gaps G1 to G23), applied in this one round; decisions D-430 to D-449 record each change. Reason: Casey's direction above.
- **D-430** Question: may the AI write words people read? Reverses AI-006, TXT-004 and CARD-007's "never free text" (D-373 for cards; D-080 and D-412 kept for what the AI may see). Choice: yes, inside fixed limits (new AI-019): while "AI on scrubbed text" is on (AI-008), the AI may (a) reword the next question from the question bank to fit what the homeowner already said, or ask one follow-up the bank's expert brief allows, (b) write the job card and the project file from her scrubbed words, answers and photo notes, and (c) write the concept-image prompt (D-433); it never diagnoses, names a cause, prices, estimates cost, judges safety or urgency, recommends a business, promises anything, or says anything about a business or a quote; every AI-written card is shown to the homeowner and approved (or auto-sent after the INT-041 wait) before it goes; code checks every AI output for contact details (CARD-008), prices and dollar amounts, cause words ("probably", "likely", "sounds like", "needs a new") and the WORD-022 list, and a failure falls back to the fixed template; every text keeps the fixed first reply, opt-out, HELP, code, pick and money templates; AI quote summaries stay off (PICK-003, D-373 unchanged). Reason: Casey's "well-versed receptionist"; legal Memo 4's points (no diagnosing or pricing in Ask Hava's own words; the homeowner approves the card) handled by the limits and the approval step.
- **D-431** Question: where do the expert questions come from? Choice: the Grok knowledge base is kept in `docs/catalog/trade_kb/` (37 trade files, foundation files, 370 test messages) as the expert brief for intake; each question set in `docs/catalog/question_bank.yaml` names its knowledge-base trade (`kb_trade`), its photo asks and its quote format; a set may hold 3 to 8 questions (was 6) in price-impact order; the fixed bank stays the fallback whenever AI is off; the 370 test messages join the golden set (OPS-040); the knowledge base's safety steps are not used where they give health or safety advice (D-436); the `intake_questions` fallback lists in `categories.yaml` are removed (G20). Changed: INT-039, LIC-040, OPS-040, `scripts/check_catalog.py`. Reason: Casey's "thinks like a licensed pro in every trade".
- **D-432** Question (rework question 1, G2): how do photos work before a zero-retention agreement? Reverses CARD-013. Choice: photos from day one, never required: intake asks for the job type's 2 or 3 photos (question bank `photos`) by picture text or by an upload link on the homeowner's portal page, says "No photo is fine too", and never asks for one during a 911-list situation (INT-004); every photo is stripped of metadata (PRIV-014) and checked on Ask Hava's own server (no outside AI) for faces, people, house numbers, plates and readable text, and a flagged photo is blurred or left off with a note; the homeowner sees each photo as it will appear and can remove it before the card goes; picture texts are copied off the texting provider and deleted there once stored; photos go on the job card and the job page; the AI reads photos (quality, what they show) only after the zero-retention agreement is signed and the AI-018 photo sentence is live, and until then a photo is never judged for quality, one re-ask being sent only when the file is unreadable or too small. Changed: new INT-044, CARD-013, CARD-014, AI-016, PRIV-013, PRIV-015, PICK-022. Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "Photos now, AI later".
- **D-433** Question: inspiration images, project file and concept images? Choice: new INT-045: in a category whose quote format is a range and that is marked `design: true` (painting, pavers and hardscape, epoxy floors, and any later remodel category), intake asks "Have any pictures of the look you want? Screenshots are fine."; inspiration images are handled as photos (D-432) and, once AI may read photos, read into a short project file (size, layout, style, finishes, for example "standard 5 by 8 bath, this layout, minimalist modern, these textures"), and before that she describes the look in a few words; new INT-046: in those categories, while the owner switch "Concept images" is on, Ask Hava may make one concept image from the written project file only (never her photos), labeled on the image and beside it "AI-generated, not exact, may not be feasible", asks "Is this the kind of thing you want?", allows up to 3 rounds of changes, and puts the image on the card with the same label; the image provider is chosen at the account checkpoint (its terms recorded like AI-008's) and the switch is off until then. Reason: Casey's direction item 2.
- **D-434** Question (G3): what does the homeowner approve? Choice: INT-041 shows the real job card, not a 3-answer summary: by text when the card fits in 2 segments, otherwise a short text with a link to the card on her portal page; EDIT, SEND and the 2-hour auto-send are unchanged; the business job page is defined (new CARD-023) and holds the card, every answer, photos, the project file and any concept image, and details she adds later (JOB-010); CARD-009's "details on the job page" now points there. Reason: her approval covers what businesses see.
- **D-435** Question: coverage messages. Choice: right after the trade is known, the homeowner hears either how many businesses will get it ("We'll send this to the 5 <trade> businesses on Ask Hava.") or, at once, "We don't cover <trade> jobs yet. We've noted your request so we can add businesses." (INT-017, TXT-032); each no-coverage request emails the owner inbox (new OPS-041) with trade, town and the count so far that month, and the weekly digest rolls them up; recruiting stays email or in person (BIZ-048). Reason: Casey's direction item 9.
- **D-436** Question (rework question 4, G13): which situations get a 911 text? Reverses INT-031 (heat-safety text), the 211 entry, INT-034's water and power-line lines, INT-035, INT-037's chirp text and INT-042's trade safety prompts. Choice: only the fire-truck list: a gas or propane smell or hiss, a CO alarm going off, smoke, fire or flames, a sparking, smoking or burning outlet, cord, switch or panel, and a downed or sparking power line; one fixed text, "Ask Hava: Ask Hava isn't an emergency service. If you smell gas, see smoke or fire, a CO alarm is going off, or a power line is down, get everyone out and call 911 from outside. Reply SAFE when you're safe.", from English and Spanish templates; the SAFE hold stays (INT-005); no symptom, health, heat, water, shutoff, utility-number or appliance advice anywhere; floods, burst pipes, AC out and no heat go straight to the businesses in that trade that take urgent jobs, through the normal urgent choice (INT-006); a numbered "smell" answer asks "What kind of smell? 1) Gas or rotten egg 2) Burning 3) Something else", 1 or 2 sending the 911 text; the safety-contacts file (INT-036) is withdrawn because no text names a number; the site's statement says plainly that Ask Hava is not an emergency service and doesn't judge what needs an emergency response (WEB-022). Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "Fire-truck list only"; "they are well aware the AC went out and it's hot."
- **D-437** Question: keep own-or-rent? Choice: no. Withdrawn: INT-028, TXT-028, CARD-011; the rental flag leaves INT-018, INT-029 and AI-010. Research (AI research, not legal advice): no Arizona or federal rule requires a quote-request service to ask; landlord permission and lien exposure fall on the business that does the work (an Arizona appeals court let a contractor hired by a tenant recover against the owner's property, *Wang Electric, Inc. v. Smoke Tree Resort, LLC*, 2012); the EPA lead-renovation rule puts the pamphlet duty on the renovator. Reason: Casey: "If you call a plumber, they don't ask you if you own the home."
- **D-438** Question: how long are texts, and what is on the portal? Reverses PICK-041's "no reviews" and the long first-quote text. Choice: new TXT-040: every homeowner update text is one or two short sentences plus her portal link (her private quote page, PICK-010); the portal holds every quote, each business's name, license numbers and record link, its Google and Yelp review links (as links only: Ask Hava shows no stars, counts or ranking), the card, and the not-a-contractor statement; TXT-016 shrinks to "Ask Hava: Your first quote is in for request <n>. More may come. See it here: <link>" plus the LIST line; G1: the portal top and the first-quote text say "Free for you. The business you pick pays Ask Hava a set introduction fee." (CON-003), PICK-016's terms line beside the button stays alone. Reason: Casey's direction items 6 and 7.
- **D-439** Question: what does sign-up collect? Choice: new BIZ-052: the Google Business Profile link and the Yelp page link (each optional, checked to be on the matching domain and to open), and every license number the business holds (ROC, Department of Agriculture, Board of Technical Registration), shown on every quote card and the portal; changing one is checked the same way; free-text directions to other channels stay banned (BIZ-022). Reason: Casey's direction item 7.
- **D-440** Question: does Ask Hava schedule anything? Choice: no. Withdrawn: PICK-025 (who lets the business in, on-site contact, lockbox); the call-window choice leaves PICK-018, PICK-031 and TXT-030 (no TIMES); the window is fixed at 8 a.m. to 8 p.m. Arizona time ("Now" on a job chosen as an emergency), which the PICK-021 refund line's "inside their window" means; homeowner texts drop "has until <time> to confirm" detail (PICK-045 becomes "Ask Hava: Done. <Business> has your pick. We'll text you when they accept."); after acceptance the business reaches her and they set the time. Reason: Casey's direction item 8.
- **D-441** Question (G4, G17): how do quotes compare? Reverses PICK-002's free choice of quote type. Choice: new PICK-053: each category names one quote format in `categories.yaml` (`quote_format`): `range` (replacements, remodels, installs), `monthly` (ongoing services), `tiers` (up to 3 named packages, for example detailing or cleaning), `firm` (small fixed jobs) or `diagnostic_visit` (repairs that need a look: a visit fee plus an estimate on site), and a job type may name its own (`job_type_formats`, for example faucet replacement `firm` inside plumbing repair); every quote for a job type uses the same format, so they line up; a site-visit fee is a quote type only in `diagnostic_visit` categories (FEE-004), so a site visit can't stand in for a range on a big job; a business that can't price without seeing it still gives a range and ticks "Range to be confirmed on site"; a tiers quote's fee is set by the tier she picks; PICK-038's mixed-type sentence becomes "the picked quote was a different type of quote from yours." Reason: Casey's direction item 1.
- **D-442** Question: what may a business write beside its quote? Then we share their first name and job address, as they asked."; the Confirm button keeps its name (the FEE-015 authorization quotes it) Reason: Casey Oct 28, 2026: "She OKs it at the pick"; the report's data-recharacterization risk (ADOR LR 21-003; *ADP, LLC v. ADOR*, 2023).
