(PICK-016), and the first-quote text's link line reads "Terms (18+)"; G9 journeys J1 and J2 rewritten; G10 OPS-028's owner call becomes an owner-portal "Call from the Ask Hava number" button that rings Casey's phone and bridges, never showing his number; G11 the owner-time estimate in `docs/for-casey-review.md` is redone by counting message types; G12 new PICK-050 ("Can't take it" on the pick email and Confirm page, no fee, the backup applies at once) and the backup covers every pick that ends without an acceptance (PICK-047); G14 new PICK-051 (edit or withdraw before any pick, shown as "updated" or "withdrawn"; expiry dropped); G15 new TXT-041 (one Spanish line, "Por ahora seguimos en inglés. Responda AYUDA si necesita ayuda.", back-translation checked like the safety texts); G16 new BIZ-051 (leaving withdraws open quotes, ends waiting picks with the backup applying, removes saved cards, ends sessions, keeps the ledger 7 years); G18 "default pending Casey" removed from AI-008, BIZ-041, FEE-004, LIC-012, LIC-015, LIC-046 and PICK-048 (confirmed D-418); G19 the verb is "Pick" everywhere (PICK-009, PICK-015, PICK-017, TXT-031); G20 by D-431; G21 OPS-033 drops the weekly window and mid-week check and keeps the 4-day digest rule. Reason: Casey's direction item 4.
- **D-448** Question: which tasks build this? Choice: test-design rows added for each new requirement; existing tasks take the changed rows where they own the module; T156 takes the intake script, AI-written questions and the card approval; new tasks T162 (photos and inspiration images), T163 (concept images), T164 (uniform quotes and notes screening), T165 (portal review links, sign-up URLs, coverage email); prompts regenerated. Reason: every requirement keeps a task and a test row (`scripts/validate_tasks.py`).

Choice: nothing for Casey in this round; `docs/PREBUILD.md` lists the round, closes B10, adds the ADOR ruling to the company-setup list and the concept-image provider to the account checkpoint, and keeps Gate P, the Twilio and Stripe questions (the Twilio one updated for D-445) and the pilot steps. Reason: PREBUILD rule 2.
- **D-450** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "So is the build plan now updated fully with everything that we need to do?"): `docs/constitution.md`, which every build session reads first, still held lines that fix rounds 13 and 14 reversed. Choice: constitution updated to match: legal hold 12 (AI-written words allowed only inside AI-019, the card approved by the homeowner, quote summaries still off; D-430), legal hold 9 (pest by attestation; D-417), section 7 item 3 (photos on cards after the on-server check, never to an AI before zero retention; D-432), insurance later (D-426) and sales tax by set-aside and a later ADOR ruling (D-444). The matching lines in the protected `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md` (:58, :86, :89, :196, :247, :248) and in `BUILD_BRIEF.md` (:182 Part 2's TPT license and insurance, :340 photos) are not edited; they are superseded by D-417, D-426, D-430, D-432, D-436, D-437 and D-444, which a build session follows under BUILD_BRIEF section 16 (decide and log). Reason: no build session should meet a guardrail that contradicts the spec.
- **D-451** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026, choosing the darker plaster and brass option from three homepage directions: "This is the design that I want to go with... whatever needs to happen"): what does the build follow for the look? Choice: `docs/design/DESIGN_SPEC.md` ("wall and paper": dark Venetian plaster for navigation and story, cream paper for quote cards and forms, brushed brass for the one primary button, rules and focus; Cormorant Garamond and DM Sans, self-hosted, SIL Open Font License) with four mock-ups in `docs/design/mockups/` (desktop home, phone home, her quote page, the pick review step), the generated textures in `docs/design/textures/` and a reference page in `docs/design/reference/`; WEB-001 now points to the spec instead of the Clearwater Modern prototype (the source lines `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:74` and `BUILD_BRIEF.md:106` stay unedited and are superseded here), new WEB-027 and TD-WEB-11 go to the new task T166 (the fonts, textures and the contrast test, after T101), and every task that owns a page, portal template or email reads the spec. Every contrast pair was computed; the brass button texture was lightened until its darkest pixel gave 6.4:1 with the label. Wording and numbers still come only from `docs/spec/`; the mock-ups use fake names and the spec's copy. Reason: Casey chose the direction; a written spec with measured values keeps every build session on one look and inside WCAG 2.2 AA.
- **D-452** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026, by voice: "we need to figure out how to eliminate AI smell as much as possible and have that embedded in the build process... the word choice in there is very obviously AI", then "do whatever you need to do so that... we have some kind of a checker that addresses all these concerns"): how does the build keep machine-sounding words and pages out? Choice: three layers. (1) A list, `docs/ai_smell.yaml`: brochure words, comfort and gushing phrases, em dashes, "not just X but Y", three-beat slogans, exclamation marks, emoji and page habits (linear gradients outside the design's rules, stripe borders, badges, icon fonts). (2) A scan, `scripts/check_ai_smell.py`, run by the test suite on every push, over the spec's quoted copy, the voice guide, the question bank, the design reference and every template, message file and stylesheet under `app/` (WORD-027); a hit fails `Ci`. (3) A cold read (WORD-028): every task prompt and `BUILD_BRIEF.md` section 7 step 4 add a reviewer that sees only the finished copy, reads it aloud and flags anything machine-written; the standard is a local person explaining it to a neighbour (`docs/voice.md`). Exempt: only legally or safety-fixed wording (the 911 text, the HELP reply) and rows whose quotes are examples or banned-word lists. The mock-up home pages are rewritten ("Get local quotes by text.", no slogan, no "seasoned pro", no "side by side") and re-rendered. T161 takes both rows. Reason: a word list catches the common tells cheaply and every time; the cold read catches what a list cannot.
- **D-453** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026, after sending "Ask Hava — Technology Stack and Programming Language Directive" and asking whether Rust has benefits beyond build complexity: "We will use rust and deal with the more complex build"; then, asked how much and how pages are built, "All of the server" and "Rust builds the pages"): what does the build use? Choice: Rust for all server code, replacing Python 3.12 and FastAPI (BUILD_BRIEF.md §4, :101 to :122, rewritten line for line so every citation still points at the same line). Axum and Tokio; Askama templates checked at compile time and autoescaped, so pages still work without JavaScript (WEB-001); SQLx with queries checked against the schema; SQL migration files `migrations/0NNN_<slug>.sql`; reqwest clients behind one trait per vendor; serde with `deny_unknown_fields`; small TypeScript compiled by `tsc --strict` for browser extras; Playwright for TypeScript with axe-core for e2e and accessibility; proptest, trybuild and cargo-mutants; clippy, rustfmt, cargo-audit, cargo-deny and npm audit. One Cargo workspace with one crate per module, so the layer rules (architecture §4) are compile errors; `automod` declares each crate's files, so tasks add files without editing a shared `lib.rs`; `Cargo.lock` and `app/server/src/main.rs` become the shared files (replacing `app/main.py`). The benefits Casey chose it for are written as requirements: SEC-029 (workspace, `unsafe_code` forbidden, clippy denied), SEC-030 (contact release only with a `ReleaseProof` that only `payments` can create; compile-fail test), SEC-031 (statuses are exhaustive enums), plus SEC-032 (stand-ins served over HTTP by new harness task T167, so one client runs against stand-in, sandbox and live) and SEC-033 (the directive's server-authority and no-secrets-in-the-browser tests); SEC-021 now names the Rust and npm audits. What stays Python: the build tooling in `scripts/`, the runner, the stand-ins, the twin and its gate (which reaches the Rust rules through a `rules_bridge` binary, T074), the document tests and the stand-in contract tests; none of it runs in production. Verified in this session on a scratch workspace with the pinned versions in architecture §11 on Rust 1.99.0: it builds, clippy passes with warnings denied, Askama escaped a planted `<script>`, a trybuild test showed a paid-pick type cannot be built outside its crate, SQLx applied a lower-numbered migration added after a higher one, cargo-audit found no vulnerabilities (one unmaintained build-time crate, ignored with its reason) and cargo-deny passed (architecture §11). Not adopted from the directive: React and Next.js (Casey chose server-built pages), OpenAPI and generated TypeScript types (no separate front end to keep in sync). Every task's owned paths were remapped (`app/<m>/<f>.py` to `app/<m>/src/<f>.rs`, tests to their layer crates, e2e and a11y to `.spec.ts`), and the protected `scripts/validate_tasks.py`, its test, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and the task template were changed in this owner-approved plan change. An independent Opus review of the whole change found that one owner per crate manifest could not work (a crate lists a path dependency only once that crate exists), that four test-layer crates would have no targets, that `.sqlx/` and CI Postgres had no owner, that trybuild needs owned `.stderr` files, that test crates could not reach crate internals, that SEC-033 sat too early, and that two clippy lints were misnamed or too broad; all were fixed: crate manifests, the workspace `Cargo.toml`, `.sqlx/`, `package.json` and `package-lock.json` became shared paths; new T168 (test-layer crates, ahead of every task that followed T009) and harness T169 (CI Postgres pinning, coverage, Playwright in Ally, eslint, the TypeScript build in the image); a per-crate `testing` feature exposes internals to the test crates only; the Postgres service and SQLX_OFFLINE are in `ci.yml` now; SEC-033 moved to T120; `clippy.toml` sets the 50-line limit; the wildcard lint is scoped to status modules plus a source scan. D-451's note that `BUILD_BRIEF.md:106` stays unedited is superseded: that line now names Askama. Reason: Casey chose Rust for the compiler-enforced guarantees on money, contact release and job statuses, and accepted the more complex build.

## Encryption of customer details, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026: "One thing I would like is for all the customer info to be encrypted sent to the buisness and all data we keep will be encrypted." He had ChatGPT propose options, Claude checked them against this plan, and Casey then said "Do what you think is best".

- **D-454** Question: what changes so customer details are encrypted on the way to the business and wherever they are kept? Already in the plan and unchanged: the field-encrypted contact vault with associated data and key versions (PRIV-002, architecture section 8), the blind phone index, audited plaintext reads, encrypted backups (PRIV-018), per-person business sign-in with a new-device code (SEC-004), server-side authority on every request (SEC-033), and the Safeguards-style security program and incident plan in `docs/legal/`. Choice: (1) the release email no longer carries any homeowner detail: it says the job is confirmed and links to sign-in, and the first name, job address and any shared email are shown only in the signed-in business portal (PICK-024 rewritten; the text for businesses that chose text drops "in your email"), because ordinary email is not end-to-end encrypted and sits in forwarded or shared inboxes (threat-model I15, R15); (2) photos are encrypted by the app under the vault key before they reach object storage and are served only through the app's own photo route behind a short-lived signed link, never a direct storage link (new PRIV-023; PRIV-014 amended); (3) every page that shows a homeowner's details or job photos sends `Cache-Control: no-store` and loads no third-party script, and no URL, email subject, push notice or text carries a homeowner detail (new SEC-034); (4) the privacy policy says plainly that texts and ordinary email are not end-to-end encrypted and points people to askhava.com for sensitive details; (5) SEC-001's "plain environment variables" is read as `BUILD_BRIEF.md:62` to `BUILD_BRIEF.md:65` allows: keys live in the app host's secret variables, never in code, logs or files. Not adopted now: an outside key service (AWS KMS or Google Cloud KMS with envelope encryption). It keeps the master key off the app host, but on Railway the cloud login would itself sit in a host variable; the clean form needs Render Pro's AWS workload sign-in (about $25 a month more). It is recorded as an option to decide with the Railway-or-Render choice at the account checkpoint (D-094), behind the existing key-version field so it can be added without re-encrypting by hand. Also not adopted: per-job data keys as a way to erase purged jobs from backups, because each backup also holds that job's wrapped key, so a restore would bring the data back; purged details leave backups only when the backup window expires, which the privacy policy already allows. Two checks added to the account checkpoint: the chosen host encrypts the database, volumes and backups at rest, and the Claude Console workspace has zero data retention in force before AI-008 is switched on. Reason: Casey asked for encryption in transit to the business and at rest everywhere; email was the one place homeowner details left an encrypted, access-controlled system, and the photo and caching changes close the remaining gaps at no running cost.

## Fix round 15: the Hormozi, Martell and Koerner review, October 28, 2026

Casey, Oct 28, 2026, after the review "Ask Hava: Hormozi, Martell & Koerner Plan Review" of commit `6285ec6`: "So do what you recommend and identify what needs to be done before the build starts"; earlier the same day, on the trade list: "We need to run all service buisnesses"; and on recruiting, by voice: "I want the majority of it just to come... the vast majority of them will be through some kind of like Facebook group ad".

- **D-455** Question (the review's top finding: the changes file still says "Build in test mode alongside the hand-run test" at `sources/01_spec/Changes_Since_v9.3_AUTHORITATIVE.md:70`, but no doc, task or decision carries the test out, and Gate P only asks for a presentation review, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:538`): when do real homeowners and businesses get tested? Choice: a 4 to 6 week hand-run test, run by Casey with two web forms, one inbox and one sheet, comes before he replies `approved to build` at Gate P; the playbook, templates, targets and go and stop lines are in `docs/pilot/hand-run-test.md`. Gate P's mechanics don't change: the runner still waits for that reply, and the before-build checklist (`docs/PREBUILD.md` section 3) now puts the test first. This reverses Casey's Oct 27 choice to build alongside the test. Two review recommendations are held for one re-plan pass after the test, so the plan changes once on real evidence instead of twice: the scope cut (postponing team members, the monthly fee limit, the live license lookup, the full restore replay and the features that launch switched off, which would mean withdrawing requirements and rebuilding the task graph) and the fee level. Until that pass no new requirements are added except to fix a defect. Reason: all three lenses (pack 03 section 5.5, 06 a3, 09 section 3.4) put proof by hand before the build, the build is 169 tasks with no real request behind it, and the test costs nothing but Casey's hours.
- **D-456** Question: the specs still say recruiting is "email or in person" (BIZ-048, OPS-041) and D-181 has Casey signing up the founding businesses in person in launch week, while Casey now wants most businesses to find Ask Hava and sign up themselves, mainly from Facebook group posts. Choice: BIZ-048 says recruiting is mostly self-serve: hand-written posts in local Facebook groups and other community sites from Casey's own profile or the Ask Hava Page, each saying he runs Ask Hava, never automated posting, a second account or a paid group admin; plus the website, email to businesses that asked, and a few visits. OPS-041's recruiting line points to BIZ-048 and may cite the true monthly count by trade. D-181's launch-week in-person sign-ups are superseded; visits are the exception. Phone recruiting still needs the A.R.S. 44-1272 registration and a new decision (D-374), so the few contacts Casey makes himself are visits. Reason: Casey's Oct 28 recruiting stance; Meta's rules bar automated group posting and paid posting rights, and the FTC endorsement rules need the tie disclosed (pack 10 sections A and D).
- **D-457** Question: nothing records where a business or homeowner came from, so the one-channel test (pack 03 H7) can't be judged. Choice: new BIZ-053 (an optional "Where did you hear about Ask Hava?" at sign-up, plus any `g` code on the askhava.com link they arrived on) and new INT-047 (a web chat records the `g` code it started from; a text request records "text"). Codes name a group, partner or flyer, never a person, and never reach routing or businesses. Reason: Hormozi's measured channel and Martell's single-channel risk both need per-source counts; no tracking script is added (SEC-034).
- **D-458** Question: the scorecard (OPS-039) can't show whether the profit goal is in reach or where intake and sign-up lose people. Choice: OPS-039 adds requests started per week, requests and sign-ups by source, paid introductions this month against a ladder of 15, 50 and 115 a month (the pilot, $20,000 a year and $50,000 a year paces at about $40 net each), net per paid introduction, intake completion and the step where unfinished intakes stopped, business sign-ups active within 15 minutes and those sent to a letter or review, the free share counted only on picks after each business's first win, leave and pause reasons, and Casey's weekly minutes split into recruiting and everything else; go and stop lines in `docs/PREBUILD.md` section 7. Reason: the review's volume math (about 50 paid introductions a month for $20,000, about 115 for $50,000) and the finding that the free first win would trip the old free-share stop line in a small pilot.
- **D-459** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026, after the review suggested opening fewer trades: "We need to run all service buisnesses"): which categories take requests at launch? Choice: every enabled category is launch-open (LIC-046; `docs/catalog/categories.yaml` sets `launch_open: true` on each enabled category); the two disabled ones (junk removal, garage doors) stay off for the legal reasons in D-404. A category still takes requests only while it has 2 or more active businesses (LIC-046's setting); OPS-006 now alerts Casey only for a town, or a trade that got a request that month, below 2, and lists the rest in the weekly digest, and the pilot's marketing pushes one or two seasonal trades at a time (`docs/pilot/hand-run-test.md` section 6). Supersedes B2's six-trade pilot list (D-418). Reason: Casey's call; the thin-quotes risk is handled by recruiting from real request counts and by the honest coverage and "Quotes may be slow" lines already in INT-017 and TXT-032.
- **D-460** Question: three intake steps slow the first quote or waste effort: the town is asked after the trade questions and photos (intake order step 10), the card waits 2 hours for SEND, and an extra "text, email or both" question follows the cards. Choice: the ZIP and area check moves to right after the first reply, before the trade check, so the coverage line counts businesses in her town (new step 4; INT-016 updated); INT-041's auto-send default is 30 minutes (a setting, read into its text); and TXT-018 no longer asks: updates go by text, and the quote page offers email instead or as well, with the same email checks. The privacy policy's text-messages section says so. Reason: Hormozi's speed and effort points ("The only thing that beats free is fast", pack 01a section A3); a Kingman homeowner no longer answers 8 questions before learning the town isn't covered.
- **D-461** Question: a Quotes ready job with no pick closes after 3 days with no text saying the quotes are in (JOB-012), and picks are the only revenue. Choice: new TXT-042 sends one text when every card's quote window has ended ("All quotes are in for request <n>: <k> of <N> businesses quoted..."), and JOB-012's close sends one closing note. Both are about her own request and follow quiet hours; QH-007 gains an exception so a held closing note is sent rather than dropped; both have Spanish templates. Reason: all three lenses on follow-up (pack 03 M7, 01a section B4, 09 heuristic 27); covered by the consent she gave by texting in (CON-005).
- **D-462** Question: a new business's first days are setup steps rather than a first job (pack 01b section B1). Choice: BIZ-042's sample pick step is offered the first time a person opens a real job card, not during sign-up (the emergency test already runs only when someone turns on emergency emails); BIZ-031's done screen, once the business is active, leads with the open jobs it can quote now (CARD-018 already sends them). Casey's personal welcome email to each early business is in the playbook, not the build. BIZ-030's long fee summary stays, because the legal check holds its exact lines. Reason: Hormozi's first 24 to 48 hours and Martell's time to first value.
- **D-463** Question: nothing records why a business pauses or leaves. Choice: new BIZ-054: one optional multiple-choice question when a business leaves (BIZ-051) or pauses longer than 14 days, with "Busy for a while" first (pointing to BUSY, BIZ-028); answers go to the weekly digest and change nothing else. Reason: Martell's cancellation process (pack 02 section 8) and Hormozi's talk to unhappy customers (01b section B1).
- **D-464** Question: with self-serve sign-ups, crowding in a trade isn't paced by recruiting any more, and OPS-027 flags it only at 7 businesses and 5 quotes for 2 months. Choice: the flag fires at 5 or more active businesses and a median of 4 or more quotes in a calendar month, all still settings, still with no automatic action. A neutral join-date waitlist that reopens when quotes per job fall below about 3 is written down as Casey's option if the flag fires; D-189's rejection stands until then. Reason: Koerner's "10 bidders for every bid" complaint (pack 09 section 12) and the review's business-side finding 7.
- **D-465** Question: three fee rules work against the review's money findings. Choice: (1) FEE-040's price lock for early joiners is 6 months, not 12, so a later fee change can reach pilot businesses sooner, and the business terms say so; (2) TERMS-026 adds that Ask Hava may refund any charge at its own choice, and that no refund is owed because the work wasn't done or paid for, which keeps the fee tied to the introduction only; (3) FEE-036's free first-win Confirm also shows the unticked save-card box, so the second paid pick is one tap. Fee amounts don't change now; the hand-run test asks whether $25 or $35 would be worth it, and the re-plan pass sets them (D-455). Reason: pack 01a sections A5, C1, C2 and 02 section 8 (raise in steps, refund people who ask, a payment signal); Koerner's low introductory price while value is unknown (09 section 11).
- **D-466** Question: Ask Hava has no public proof and nothing helps a homeowner come back or tell a neighbor. Choice: JOB-017's private feedback page adds one unticked box letting Ask Hava show the words on askhava.com with first name and town, asked of everyone, with no reward and no ticked feedback left out for being negative; new WEB-028 shows three real numbers on the home page (requests sent, median time to first quote, average quotes per request) once 20 requests have gone out, computed, never typed; new PICK-054 adds "Add Ask Hava to your contacts" on the quote page and accepted-quote email and a "Know someone who needs quotes?" line after Done, never in a text. Reason: proof over promises (pack 01b section E3), referrals by design (03 M9); FTC review rules (06 c4) and the no-marketing-text rule (TXT-008, TXT-009).
- **D-467** Question: the rest of the review. Choice: (1) the AI receptionist "Pro" plan is cut; any later recurring option is a prepaid credit pack spent only on introduction fees, with no auto-renew and no placement advantage (`docs/PREBUILD.md` section 8); (2) the Ruby answering service is declined, and fixed running costs are capped at about $150 a month, not counting the Claude plan; (3) the handover pack gains a one-page "changing the live service" guide, because Claude Code is the only maintainer of the Rust service (`BUILD_BRIEF.md` section 15); (4) company setup starts when the hand-run test hits its quote line, not after the build; (5) $20,000 a year is the Lake Havasu City target and $50,000 assumes Bullhead City and Kingman. Not changed now, with reasons: night job-card emails (QH-005, D-344) wait for the test's email-speed numbers; AI-drafted HELP replies (OPS-036, D-348) wait for the AI switch and come with the support bot; holding uncovered requests for a few days would add a job status, so uncovered demand is used in recruiting posts instead. Reason: the review's operations and money findings; Hormozi's subtraction (pack 01b section E2).

## Fix round 16: ChatGPT's stress test of the hand-run test, October 28, 2026

Casey sent the plan, the playbook and fix round 15 to ChatGPT for an outside attack ("Ask Hava Pre-Build Stress Test: Deep Research Review", Oct 28, 2026) and brought the answer back; his standing instruction is "So do what you recommend and identify what needs to be done before the build starts".

- **D-468** Question: ChatGPT found that the hand-run test (D-455) could not answer its own money question, because a stated "it would be worth it" isn't payment (stated willingness to pay runs well above real buying); that jobs from people Casey knows would inflate demand; that one quote proves routing, not choice; that a 4-hour median hides slow tails; that Casey's own nudges could make the test look better than the system; that about 25 to 40 requests give wide error bars; that a November start tests winter Havasu only; and that 8 hours a week is already near the monthly time limit. Choice: `docs/pilot/hand-run-test.md` is rewritten: four questions (demand, choice, payment, time); a rules tab fixed before the test (request, source class Seeded, Partner or Organic, routable, pushed trade, valid quote, active business, nudge, season); weeks 1 to 3 helped and free, weeks 4 to 6 hands off and paid, with picked businesses in the pushed trades paying the real fee by payment link before the details, and no free first win in the test; a scorecard on Organic requests, 2-or-more-quote share, median and 75th-percentile first quote, picks among requests with 2 or more quotes, active (quoting) businesses, self-serve activation, real payments by 5 or more businesses, hands-off versus helped quote rates, and minutes per request; results reported as a winter test; homeowner details deleted 90 days after it ends. Company setup (the LLC, EIN and a live Stripe account) moves to the start of the test, so the paid weeks can run; this supersedes D-467 item (4). In the build: OPS-039 adds the 2-and-3-quote shares, the 75th-percentile first-quote time, picks among requests with 2 or more quotes, active businesses per trade, and quotes per win; OPS-027's crowding flag also shows quotes per win; WEB-028 waits for 50 requests and shows its date range and trade count. All service categories stay open (D-459, Casey's call); the pushed trades are the ones judged. Not adopted: dropping the free first win at launch (D-465 keeps it and judges the pilot on businesses paying again within 60 days; the test itself has no free win), and moving the Twilio question later (an answer takes days and costs nothing to ask). The phone-recruiting rule is restated as a legal uncertainty under A.R.S. 44-1271 and 44-1272, still with no calls. Reason: payment, organic demand and choice are what the build depends on, and the test now measures each with something a person did rather than said.
- **D-469** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "Want to have all set before we go live. We can fill them all with a test environment of fake buisnessed and responses to make sure it all works"): how is the hand-run test rehearsed before any real person is involved? Choice: `docs/pilot/hand-run-tracker.xlsx`, the test's tracking workbook (Settings, Rules, Businesses, Requests, Quotes, Picks, Nudges, Posts, Weekly and a computed Scorecard with the playbook's section 10 lines), loaded with fake data only (example.com addresses, "Test" names), formulas filled down 300 rows and checked against an independent recount; and `docs/pilot/dry-run.md`, a 90-minute rehearsal of six scenarios (sign-ups; two quotes, a pick and a Stripe test-mode payment; an over-limit handyman quote and a late quote; an uncovered trade; no pick; a refusal, the backup and a refund) using Gmail "+" addresses so one personal inbox plays every fake business and homeowner. The software build keeps its own fake environment (stand-ins and the twin); this rehearses the manual test only. Reason: Casey wants everything proven before the first real post; the rehearsal costs nothing and moves no real money.
- **D-470** Question (ChatGPT's "Ask Hava Legal Research Report", Oct 28, 2026, which Casey asked for and brought back): what has to change before the paid test weeks and before launch? Its findings: no Arizona statute, ROC rule or decision bars a neutral fixed introduction fee, so it is likely lawful but not settled (A.R.S. 32-1101, 32-1154); Ask Hava stays outside contractor status only while it never bids, prices, negotiates scope, schedules, supervises, guarantees or takes payment for work; TPT on the fee is likely not owed but unresolved, and a city business license is a separate requirement; a sign-up note plus a payment click can form a contract but is fragile; the future-state privacy policy and SMS terms describe tools the hand-run test doesn't use; job-card emails should meet CAN-SPAM anyway; and the FTC Safeguards Rule likely treats Ask Hava as a finder, while whether one-off homeowners are "customers" is unclear, and the under-5,000 exception removes only four specific requirements. Choice: before the paid weeks, (1) new `docs/pilot/paid-pilot-terms.md`, one page, accepted by every business on a form with an unticked box, version and time, before any paid-week card; the payment link and a receipt repeat the version; (2) new `docs/pilot/pilot-privacy-notice.md`, describing only Google Forms, Sheets, Gmail and Stripe, linked from the request form; (3) a CAN-SPAM footer with the postal address and an unsubscribe on every job card; (4) the Lake Havasu City business license (and home-occupation form if run from home) before week 4 at the latest; (5) refund wording that doesn't assume a phone call, since the test shares email only; (6) a licensed-only rule for permit-heavy work and no splitting of a project to fit the handyman limit. Before launch: CON-005 and the SMS terms make web-chat text consent explicit (typing a mobile number and its code), the privacy policy's breach line follows the law, the business terms' tax line says tax owed is included rather than implying none is owed, and the security program gains an applicability review, vendor-contract safeguards, a data inventory and a label on voluntary controls. Not changed: the tiered fee (a single flat fee would be cleaner, which goes to the re-plan pass with the fee level), and the tax path, which stays the 8.6% set-aside while Casey chooses between an ADOR private ruling (the report's advice) and his own research (his earlier call). Reason: the report found no legal blocker to the paid test on these facts, and each change makes a promise provable or a document true for the day it's used.
- **D-471** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "We have time if there is absolutely anything we can check or do research on"): are the playbook's setup steps and costs right? Findings: the Lake Havasu City fee schedule (S-110, effective Feb 1, 2026) charges $196 for a new business license and $140 to renew, applied for on the city's online portal; the home-occupation ordinance bars the home address in ads; an Arizona LLC filing is $50 ($85 expedited), and the newspaper notice is skipped only with a Maricopa or Pima County agent (vendor sources; confirm on the Commission's site); Stripe's restricted list has no entry for introduction fees but prohibits "sales of online traffic or engagement" and no-value-added resale, so the live account opens early; and the playbook's quotes template lacked LIC-024's "Not a licensed contractor" line for unlicensed handymen, which Arizona's handyman exemption expects in advertising. Choice: the playbook's cost line now says about $300 to $450 before week 4; section 9 gives the license, LLC and agent costs and opens Stripe in week 0 or 1; template E carries the LIC-024 line. Casey dropped the Grok pushed-trades task; the trades stay weekly pool cleaning and handyman, chosen at week 1 under section 7. No requirement changes.
- **D-472** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "What do we need to do on the technical side to make sure all the coding is right"): do the build's own safety checks switch on in time? Checked the CI workflows, the protect-main ruleset (Ci, Twin and Ally required; no deletion or force-push) and the task order in `kit/tasks.json`, and ran the paper rehearsal (PREBUILD item 15, under Casey's standing "do what you recommend"): `runner/cycle.py` over the real task list from the live `kit/state.json`, with fake GitHub and workers, once all green and once with T058 failing. Findings: (1) T169, which makes Ci measure branch coverage against `tests/coverage_floor.json`, sat at position 81 of 162 claims, after T056 and T073 set the 95% floors and after the fee code (T058), so those floors would go unchecked for about 25 tasks (BUILD_BRIEF.md:481); (2) no task put cargo-mutants into any workflow, so the named mutants were proven once and never re-run, against BUILD_BRIEF.md:472's nightly run; (3) the real-texts gate T130 clears only on 'yes', so Casey answering 'no' would hold T131 and everything after it, Finished included. Choice: T169 moves to right after T053 and T056 and T073 depend on it (rehearsal: T169 now claimed 58th of 163, before T056, T058 and T073); T056 also owns `.github/workflows/nightly-main.yml` and adds the nightly cargo-mutants run; new harness task T170 (after T008, before the account checkpoint) makes T130 clear on 'yes' or 'no' and pass the answer on. Rehearsal results: all green reaches "Finished" in 171 hourly cycles with only the stops in PREBUILD section 6 (Gate P, accounts done, Stage 0.5, real texts, Gate B, Gate C, first charge) and no deadlock; with T058 failing, it blocks after 3 tries and only its dependents wait (every queued task left had an unfinished dependency). The rehearsal script stays outside the repo. No requirement changes.
- **D-473** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "We need to make sure that we have a full scaffolding for all of the code... The whole code base is gonna be handled entirely by AI. So an AI that's not familiar with it needs all the documentation in it in order to make any kind of adjustments or understand what's going on"): does the plan make the code explain itself to a session that has never seen it? Found: `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/decisions.md` are kept current by every PR (BUILD_BRIEF.md:489) and tests carry their test-design IDs (`docs/test-design.md` rule 2), but nothing required a doc comment on any item, a module guide per crate, requirement IDs in the code, or a start-here map, and nothing tested whether a stranger could actually change the code. Choice: new standard `docs/code-docs.md`; every crate's `CLAUDE.md` (six fixed headings, at most 120 lines, loaded by Claude Code in that folder) becomes a shared path and is updated with every behavior change; `//!` on every Rust file, `--` on every migration, `///` on every public item with the workspace lints denying `missing_docs` (T009); requirement IDs on rule code, and every finished build task's requirements named in its own files; `scripts/check_code_docs.py` with `tests/test_check_code_docs.py` enforces all of it in `Ci` from the first crate on (it passes trivially until code exists); the task template adds the rule and its done line to every prompt; T141 adds `docs/README.md`, the start-here map with the common change recipes; T125 and T140 audit the docs against the code; new audit task T171 (after T141, before T142) has a fresh Sonnet session with no history make three real changes on a throwaway branch and fixes every gap it reports, then adds a 'Changing the code after the build' section to the root `CLAUDE.md`. Casey's request is a how-to-build rule, so it is not a new requirement under the D-455 freeze.
- **D-474** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "we need to create a brain of all of the best copywriters... make a persona of all of them, and then have a checker that goes through this as it's written to not only make sure it doesn't smell like AI, but make sure that it's good copy"): how is copy quality, not only AI smell, checked as the copy is written? Choice: `docs/copy/copy-panel.md`, a panel of eight writers' methods, each taken from a cited source (Harry Dry's three questions, Joanna Wiebe's voice-of-customer research, Eugene Schwartz's stages of awareness, David Ogilvy's concrete facts and plain words, Joseph Sugarman's first sentence and raised objections, Eddie Shleyner's specificity, Alex Cattoni's trust and empathy, Ann Handley's one reader and read-aloud), with Nielsen Norman Group's finding that most users scan and plain-English targets (20-word sentences, grade 9; Ask Hava uses grade 8 for homeowners); a ten-test scorecard with a passing rule; and the reviewer persona prompt the task template's cold read now runs alongside the AI-smell read. `scripts/check_copy.py` measures what a script can (sentence length, reading grade, superlatives) on `docs/copy/site-copy.md` and, once they exist, the page templates under `app/*/templates/`, and `tests/test_check_copy.py` runs it in `Ci`. `docs/copy/voice-of-customer.md` collects real customers' phrases from the hand-run test (phrases only, nothing identifying), for the panel's test 4 and the re-plan pass. New requirement WORD-029, owned by T161. Casey asked for it directly, so it is added despite the D-455 freeze. Nobody on the panel is named, quoted or imitated in customer copy; the spec's rules and fixed legal wording beat the panel.
- **D-475** Question: the copy panel's first review of the home page (D-474) restored "Yard and landscape maintenance" to the coverage list, and "landscape" is on the AI-smell list (`docs/ai_smell.yaml`), so a page built from the catalog would fail `scripts/check_ai_smell.py`. Choice: the category's customer-facing name becomes "Yard maintenance" (its id `landscape_maintenance` and keywords stay), which is also plainer. The home page draft in `docs/copy/site-copy.md` waits for Casey's OK before the rest of the site is written to it; the example quote's business is now "Example Heating & Air", because close variants of the earlier fake name belong to real Arizona businesses.
- **D-476** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026: "what does this project need now before you are 100% confident in the build out"): has the build's Rust path ever run? It hadn't: D-453 moved the stack to Rust after the K9 rehearsal, so no session or `Ci` run had compiled the workspace. Checked: a session like the runner's has Rust (rustup installs the pinned 1.99.0), reaches crates.io, and has PostgreSQL 16 installed but stopped, with password-only local logins. A throwaway branch (`claude/spike-rust-ci`, never merged) holding a minimal workspace at the section 11 pins (axum, tokio, askama, serde, sqlx), a `server` crate, `deny.toml` and an integration test against Postgres passed `Ci` end to end on GitHub: `cargo fmt`, clippy with warnings denied, `cargo build -p server`, `cargo test` against the Postgres service, `cargo audit` (no vulnerabilities) and `cargo deny` (advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok); the run took about 12 minutes, 13 of a cycle's minutes going to compiling cargo-audit and cargo-deny until T169 installs prebuilt binaries. It found three defects, fixed here: (1) the "smoke test" that every task runs first (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:240`, the task template's step 4) was never defined, so new `scripts/smoke.sh` runs the same checks as `Ci` and starts a local Postgres with a fresh local-only password each run (`--full` adds the dependency audits); (2) `.gitignore` didn't exclude `/target/` or `node_modules/`, so `pii_scan.py` scanned build output (88 false findings) and a worker's `git add -A` would commit it, which the spike itself did by mistake before it was removed; (3) `scripts/check_code_docs.py` (D-473) looked for `app/<crate>` in `docs/architecture.md`, which names modules as `` `core` `` and `core/`, so the first real crate would have failed `Ci`. The spike branch could not be deleted from this session (the proxy refuses branch deletion); it holds no secret or personal data and can be deleted on GitHub.
- **D-477** Question: does the spec contradict itself after fix rounds 8 to 16? A fresh Opus sweep of all 646 requirements against D-001 to D-476 (not seen by the rounds' author) found 16 conflicts and 11 wording mismatches that the repo checks can't catch; each was verified against the files. Choices, the later decision winning each time: $0 picks (free first win, repeat customer FEE-013, fee-free job type FEE-045) release details like paid ones (PICK-022, PRIV-005, SEC-006, PICK-021, the constitution, TERMS-025 and the legal drafts); a quote-page email gets updates only and never a code (TXT-018, keeping PICK-029 and D-050); the auto-confirm charge (PICK-048) is named in FEE-016 and the FEE-015 authorization text; a "Job type not certain" card goes only to licenses covering every candidate job type, else the INT-032 split (INT-040, keeping CARD-001); card photos are a screened copy, not a vault field, readable by `quotes` (PRIV-001, PRIV-004 now listing D-093's readers, PRIV-006, SEC-034, TERMS-025); constitution hold 8 and lines on Twilio pre-clearance, business consent, pest attestation, the Stripe description, the liability cap and the first text's fee line follow the spec; web-chat consent is a mobile number and its code, and a chat request with no mobile number needs no text consent (TXT-034, CON-007); an opted-out homeowner's "2 YES" gets no text, and the Terms line goes to the quote page and email (PICK-030); the email sending-limit warning never starts safe mode (OPS-001); the AI's emergency and urgent fields are recorded only and never send the 911 text (INT-026, AI-010); verification codes go only to the person's own phone or own email, never the owner's phone, an email-only Confirm being logged as an ASVS deviation (SEC-004); HOLD, Away and a bounce extension send the homeowner no text (PICK-043, BIZ-034, PICK-019, keeping PICK-045); an emergency job opens when its cards go first (JOB-008); the ledger gets the two $0 kinds (FEE-028); business email replies follow BIZ-032's filter, the sample-card OK never opening a help request (BIZ-047, OPS-036, SEC-025); and the smaller fixes: "usually" on BIZ-032 and TXT-029's reply times; AI-018's off-state wording per D-445; WORD-014's line excepted in WORD-020; TXT-040's exceptions; "emergency", not "urgent", for the night label (INT-033, PICK-017); the "Ask Hava:" prefix on JOB-027, JOB-023, PICK-033 and texted PICK-026; LIC-015's stale "default pending Casey"; the emergency window in BIZ-030's summary; the owner's help reply by email to businesses (OPS-036); restore replay checking the email provider too (OPS-015, OPS-017); stopall and optout in CON-008. `scripts/check_legal_docs.py` and its test follow the changed FEE-015, TERMS-025 and AI-018 wording. These are defect fixes, not new requirements (D-455).
- **D-478** Question: did D-477 resolve the sweep's findings without new conflicts? The same reviewer checked the branch and found 20 of 27 fully resolved, 6 partly and one open, plus 8 new or newly exposed problems; all were verified and fixed: the last $0-pick lines (FEE-036, PICK-037, PICK-026); `docs/architecture.md` section 8 now lists `quotes` as a card-photo reader and the owner realm's help-request conversations (H), and PRIV-001, PRIV-004 and PRIV-006 say a card photo is kept and encrypted by `privacy` but is not a contact field; stale test rows TD-LIC-17, TD-PRIV-06 and TD-BIZ-27; TXT-040 says "short" rather than "one or two sentences"; the "Ask Hava:" prefix inside the PICK-026 and PICK-038 quotes and the voice guide; (A) an opted-out homeowner's pick completes only from the emailed one-time button or with the PICK-029 code, never from the quote page alone, so a forwarded link can't pick; (B) when no license covers every candidate job type, no card goes, the homeowner is asked for more detail or a photo, and the owner gets the OPS-041 email, instead of an INT-032 split that only fits two trades; (C) TERMS-025, the privacy policy and its check now say the picked business also gets full-size photos with location data removed (PICK-022); (D) CON-007's exception covers a chat whose number was never verified; (E) a business's new help email and its reply to an Ask Hava email are told apart by the In-Reply-To and References headers; (G) a person without a texting phone must link one before marking an inbox shared (SEC-027). Defect fixes, not new requirements (D-455).

## Pre-build engineering audit, October 28, 2026

Casey: "make Ask Hava's existing development blueprint as complete, internally consistent, secure, efficient, and executable as reasonably possible before writing production code … establish an engineering governance system that future AI coding sessions must follow … Do not declare Ask Hava ready to build while significant unresolved planning risks remain." Five independent Opus reviewers (Rust architecture, task graph and sequencing, governance and runner resilience, spec consistency, security and privacy) each saw only their own scope; every finding was verified against the files before it was acted on. The full report is `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md`.

- **D-479** Question: which rules of the blueprint does a machine enforce, which does only a reader enforce, and how is progress measured? Findings: the runner's path, model and done checks exist only as code with no live caller (the scheduled session reads Appendix A prose and has skipped them, D-171, D-166); the three required checks run from the branch they test; owner gates cleared on negated replies ("don't go live yet"); skipped Rust steps counted as passed; `docs/architecture.md` sections 3 to 6, the spec, the brief and the task graph could be rewritten by any task; `docs/decisions.md` was append-only on paper; a new crate needed only a document entry; `#[allow]`, wildcard status arms and TODOs were reader-enforced; `main_red` could never clear; the `Twin` check ran a smoke copy of the old simulator because nothing created `twin/`; no workflow ran the end-to-end journeys; coverage floors landed after the vault and licensing code; cleanup tasks owned all of `app/` with a one-file budget; progress was counted in tasks; ten tasks still named withdrawn requirements; `docs/sources.md:29` still said "stop and ask Casey". Choice: `docs/governance.md` (who may change what, the enforcement matrix, the tightened conflict rule, the red-`main` protocol, progress in verified requirements, reviews and audits, persistent state, risks, readiness); `docs/ai-operating-manual.md`; `docs/risk-register.md`; `docs/build-readiness.md` (verdict NOT READY; stage exit tests); `kit/plan.lock` fingerprinting the locked blueprint, checked by `scripts/check_plan_lock.py`; `scripts/check_pr_change.py` (task-branch path rule, ordered append-only decisions, lock rewrite and decision on any locked change, requirement IDs never removed, `Plan-Change: D-###` trailer on non-task branches, `--no-renames`); `.github/workflows/governance.yml` running `main`'s copy of both scripts on every head through `workflow_run` and posting a `Governance` check; `scripts/progress.py` (a requirement is verified only when every task naming it merged green, every test entry covering it is owned by a merged task, and the nightly run on `main` was green afterwards; `nightly-main` now writes `kit/nightly.json` to `claude/kit-state`; the runner records `merged_sha` and `merged_at`); `scripts/check_rust_rules.py`, `scripts/check_crates.py` with `kit/allowed_crates.txt` (protected), `kit/layers.json` (protected, read by T009's architecture test); `Ci` fails when a file that exists on `main` (`Cargo.toml`, the lock files, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `deny.toml`, `tests/coverage_floor.json`) is missing from the branch; clippy runs with `-F clippy::allow_attributes_without_reason`; `runner/outbox.py` clears a gate only on a short, unnegated reply; `inbox.yml` ignores comments performed through a GitHub App and sessions are denied the issue-comment, status, check-run and ruleset APIs; `check_code_docs.py` enforces the 200-line `CLAUDE.md`; `validate_tasks.py` rejects withdrawn requirement IDs (ten removed from tasks); new harness task T172 (the twin into `twin/`, `gate.py`, waivers, `twin.yml` switched to the gate, before T005) and T074 owns `twin.yml`; T169 split into T169 (Ci coverage, sqlx check, per-crate clippy; now before T022 and T034) and T173 (Ally, the e2e job in Ci, the Dockerfile npm stage); T169 creates `tests/coverage_floor.json`; T056 owns `scripts/run_named_mutants.py` and its Ci step, and the pick-license mutant moves to T073; cleanup tasks are scan-driven over `app/` and `migrations/` with a 14-file budget and must record the stage exit test; T001 removed (its fix made here: `docs/sources.md:29` now states the decide-and-log rule); `kit/plan.lock` is a shared path rewritable on a task branch only by a harness task or the owner of a changed locked file. Left for named work before T001 (`docs/build-readiness.md` section 4): the runner's live entry point and GitHub `Env` with `reviewed_sha` gating of `auto-pr`, the red-`main` drill, `stage_exit.py`, per-attempt history and the deviations register, `check_state.py`, prompts carrying requirement text with byte-based budgets, task splitting at 600 lines, 12 requirements or 10 files, and the stage-boundary audits. Escalated to Casey: Claude under its own GitHub identity with a required owner review; the ruleset taps (`Governance` required, the Actions source on all checks, up-to-date branches, a `claude/kit-state` ruleset); the model allocation. Reason: a rule a tired session can skip is not a rule; everything above turns a reading into a failing check or names the task that will. Source: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:59`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:205`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:483`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:1`.
- **D-480** Question (the audit's security and privacy review against OWASP ASVS 5.0 L2, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:293`): which design gaps must be requirements before code starts? Findings, each verified: the test-control router was switched by an environment variable and the startup key check worked only for Stripe's prefixed keys; vault keys had no escrow, rotation or nonce policy and the blind index no domain separation; link tokens were derivable from `ID_SECRET` and a job number; masked-call PINs had no attempt limit and the read-back trusted caller ID; Verify sends had no caps or enumeration rule; native HEIC decoding would run beside the keys with no dimension limits; sensitive business changes needed only a 30-day session; inbound email was not modelled and held homeowner data outside the vault; the owner account had no bootstrap rule and its recovery factors sat in one vault; a spoofable text pick could fire the off-session auto-confirm charge; the scrubber's tests only generated the patterns it was written for; HELP notifications, the dispute evidence pack, card-email photo bytes, Twilio-retained bodies, Sentry and request logs could carry homeowner data; Resend and Lob webhooks were unverified and Stripe proofs unchecked against livemode, account, amount and pick; client IP and rate-limit atomicity were unspecified; no realm separation inside `portals`; no audit hash chain or storage write-only token; no SSRF rule; CSRF and fixation details missing; owner sessions had no idle timeout or step-up; no vault read-volume alert; no security-event logging; Actions unpinned and gitleaks scanning only the working tree; no independent gate between agent-written code and the live deploy; one-person operations with no break-glass plan. Choice: new requirements SEC-035 to SEC-048, PRIV-024 to PRIV-030, CALL-025, PICK-055, PICK-056, AI-020, AI-021, OPS-042 and OPS-043 (text in `docs/spec/`; each cites the audit section it rests on), with test-design entries TD-SEC-15 to TD-SEC-26, TD-PRIV-09 to TD-PRIV-13, TD-CALL-11, TD-PICK-36, TD-FEE-31, TD-AI-09, TD-AI-10, TD-OPS-19 and TD-OPS-20, each assigned to the task that owns the module (the task reads the spec file and carries a security review area); TD-PRIV-08 no longer expects HEIC decoding (PRIV-025: a HEIC upload is refused with a plain reply, so no native decoder runs in the process that holds the keys; phones send JPEG by default). Three rules change what a person sees and are listed for Casey's confirmation (E-5): no server-side HEIC at launch, HELP notification bodies scrubbed, text picks never auto-charge. Not decided here, left to a plan change (P-1): the photo store contradiction between D-094 and PRIV-023; `pii_scan.py` email and image-metadata rules; database TLS and migration credentials; recycled numbers; request-smuggling and admin-interface items; the threat model's TB12 row. Reason: these are design decisions that are cheap before code and expensive after; the ones that need Casey's hands (identity, ruleset, hardware keys) are escalated, not assumed. Source: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:293`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:146`.
- **D-481** Question (the audit's independent Rust review of `docs/architecture.md`, with every pin checked against crates.io, npm and the Rust release channel): will the Rust design work as written? Findings, each verified: `ReleaseProof` as designed broke the layer rule (the vault read lives in `privacy`, layer 2, which cannot name a `payments`, layer 3, type), and its compile-fail proof ran in a crate with the `testing` feature on, which made the private constructor reachable; the `testing` feature itself could not work as written (`pub(crate)` items stay hidden when their module is made `pub`, and automod gives every file one visibility so `api` was private in production), so the natural import path would have passed every local check and failed only in `Ci`; the `rules_bridge` binary needed `testing` on a production crate, which TD-SEC-10 forbids, and `twin.yml` installed no Rust; the worker binary could not see the composition in `main.rs`; `smoke.sh` set `DATABASE_URL` to an empty database with no `SQLX_OFFLINE`, so the first `query!` would have failed every session; `cargo sqlx prepare --workspace` skips test targets unless told `--all-targets`; sqlx 0.9.0 has no `jiff` feature, so no `timestamptz` column could be read; typescript 7.0.2 conflicts with typescript-eslint 8.71.2's peer range (`>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`); sqlx and reqwest resolved to different rustls crypto providers; edition 2024 makes `set_var` unsafe, which the settings tests needed; `too_many_lines` is pedantic and off unless denied; the named-mutant runner would have rebuilt the workspace per mutant; migration roles, the release step, error handling, the worker loop, shutdown, pool sizing, middleware features, the Askama-axum bridge, shared layouts, static assets and test isolation were undecided; `Ci` repeated the full compile about five times with no cache. Choice: unit and property tests move inside each crate (`app/<module>/src/tests/`, `#[cfg(test)]`, one `mod` line per task in the shared `src/tests/mod.rs`), the `testing` feature and the unit and property test crates are dropped, automod is dropped and `lib.rs` is hand-written with `pub mod api;` and private modules; each `api` carries `pub mod rules` re-exporting the pure decision functions, which the `rules_bridge` (a plain binary, linted, with a JSON-schema contract test) and cross-crate property tests use; `ReleaseProof` and a non-`Clone` `ReleaseMint` live in `privacy::api`, the mint is created once in `server/src/lib.rs` and handed to `payments`, which alone turns a verified Stripe event or a no-fee decision into a proof, with the architecture test failing on any other crate naming the mint and a trybuild case in a crate with no feature proving nothing else can build a proof (SEC-030 unchanged in meaning); `server/src/lib.rs` holds `build_router()` and `register_handlers()` for both binaries and `server migrate` is the release step; roles are created outside migrations with idempotent grants and default privileges inside them, and append-only tests run `SET ROLE`; `Settings::from_iter`; `thiserror` per crate, no anyhow; the worker loop, graceful shutdown and pool sizing as architecture section 5 now states; `#[sqlx::test]` per database test; `app/core/build.rs` (T012) so new migrations are seen; the architecture test fails on same-schema migrations whose number order differs from task order, and each migration names its owning module; a `status.rs` module per crate with the wildcard deny on it alone and `scripts/check_rust_rules.py` refusing glob imports and `==` on statuses elsewhere; a template scan refusing `|safe` and `escape = "none"`; pins: sqlx `tls-rustls-aws-lc-rs` with the provider installed first in both binaries, jiff-sqlx 0.2.0, askama_web 0.16.0 (`axum-0.8`), typescript 6.0.3, tower-http features named, HEIC not decoded (D-480), the coverage nightly pinned by date at T169 over `rules.rs` and `service.rs`; `smoke.sh` builds with `SQLX_OFFLINE=true`, adds `cargo build -p server`, runs `sqlx migrate run` and the `prepare --check` when sqlx-cli is present; T169 adds rust-cache pinned by SHA, prebuilt tools, the `prepare --check`, coverage in its own job and drops the per-crate clippy runs, with a 15-minute warm-run ceiling and the Actions-minutes budget confirmed with Casey before T001; the named-mutant runner mutates in place with a shared target directory and runs only the named tests; 66 owned test paths remapped, T168 narrowed to the integration, security, recovery and fixtures crates. Not changed: one crate per module (Casey chose Rust for compiler-enforced boundaries, D-453); passkeys and TOTP both (D-098). Left to T009 under the new-library rule: the architecture test's helper crates. Reason: each item was either a compile error waiting for the first session that hit it, or a decision the first Rust task would otherwise have made alone and unreviewed. Source: `docs/architecture.md:118`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:101`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:194`.
- **D-482** Question (the audit's independent spec-consistency review, a sample of 40 requirements against their test entries and a sweep of the governance documents): does the plan still agree with itself after fix rounds 8 to 16 and D-477 to D-478? Findings, each verified: three different outcomes for an uncovered request (INT-017 "closes", INT-022 "Closed early", OPS-041 "not covered") with no Intake → Not covered arrow in the state machine; `ops` (layer 1) owned HELP notifications with message text, alert emails, the critical call, the scorecard and the coverage email, all needing modules above it, and `ops.help_message` held homeowner text outside the vault; 20 test-design rows still asserted withdrawn or replaced behaviour (the rental meeting question, a 2-hour Confirm window, a "confirming" text on HOLD, voice calls to businesses, a 1-business-day reply and review target, the call window, "Switch to urgent", the weekly spot-check, the old crowding numbers, "four" push kinds, a safety-word complaint alerting at once, a refusal before the ZIP check, paid-only release) or cited withdrawn IDs; CALL-005, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:151` and the architecture invariant still said "paid pick" only; the section 6 timeout table lacked the HOLD, away and emergency additions; WORD-024 and TXT-040 ("about 15 words") disagreed with WORD-029's 20-word check, which spec-fixed sentences such as WEB-005 would break; CARD-021, CARD-022 and OPS-041 had unclear module owners; the J1 journey ran the trade question before the ZIP check; `docs/PREBUILD.md` still said "no fees" and "starting a web chat is the opt-in"; the constitution still described an identity voice call, held texts dropped on close without the JOB-012 exception, "answered within 2 business days", SMS-only new-device codes and no Gate P; the threat model said weekly spot-check; the storyboard, HANDOFF and the review pack carried superseded lines with no banner; the stage exit rows named task ranges that left out 23 tasks and required journeys whose steps belong to later stages. Choice: INT-017 and INT-022 name one transition, Intake → Not covered, and INT-022 says a duplicate joins the open job (INT-010) unless its 2-hour window has passed; architecture section 6 gains the arrow, the Not covered row and the HOLD, away and emergency timeouts; `ops` records IDs-only rows and enqueues events, `messaging` sends alert emails, HELP notifications and the critical call, `portals` serves the scorecard and intake report from `payments::api` and `intake::api`, `businesses` sends the coverage email through `messaging::api`, `quotes` owns the EMERGENCY card content and the open-cards email's lines come through a `quotes` event, and a help message's text lives in `privacy.message_body`; every listed test-design row now quotes the current requirement, and TD-INT-17, TD-INT-24 and TD-OPS-17 no longer cite withdrawn IDs; CALL-005 and the architecture invariant say "paid or a recorded $0 fee decision" (PRIV-005); WORD-024 and TXT-040 say 15 words or fewer where no requirement fixes the sentence and never over 20, and WORD-029 exempts any spec-fixed sentence marked "fixed"; J1 runs the ZIP check first; the stage exit rows in `docs/build-readiness.md` are built from each task's stage value and name J1 at stage 4 and J3 at stage 5; `docs/PREBUILD.md`, `docs/constitution.md`, `docs/threat-model.md`, the storyboard, HANDOFF and the review pack are corrected or carry a supersession banner. Left to P-1: the per-requirement module map for section 3, the testability rewrites the reviewer proposed for INT-010, INT-015, INT-038, INT-039, INT-044, LIC-040, CON-010, CON-032, CARD-009, CARD-010, A11Y-001, A11Y-005, A11Y-009, A11Y-018, TXT-005, WORD-009, WORD-013, PICK-052, AI-016, LIC-018, LIC-025, CALL-013, SEC-012, PRIV-019, WEB-015 and BIZ-042 (each needs a setting or a decision to pin a number), Gate B and Gate C pass bars, the Stage 0.5 exit row, and the brief's stale lines (R-12). Reason: a test row that asserts a withdrawn rule fails correct code or passes wrong code, and a layer-1 module that must call layer 3 is a compile error waiting for T015. Source: `docs/spec/index.md:3`, `docs/architecture.md:118`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:242`.
- **D-483** Question (Casey Oct 28, 2026, on the audit's six escalations: "I dont want to make the decisions you make them based on best practices"): what is decided, and what is left as a hands-on step for Casey? Choice: (E-1) Claude runs under its own GitHub identity: the GitHub Actions runner (`backup-runner.yml`, the Claude Code action with the subscription token, which pushes and comments as the Claude GitHub App) becomes the primary runner and the scheduled routine the safety net, so the owner-only inbox filter and a required owner review mean something; a `CODEOWNERS` file names Casey for the locked blueprint, `.github/`, `.claude/`, `runner/` and `scripts/`, and the ruleset requires a code-owner review, so a harness task's pull request waits for Casey's approval (one email, one tap) while every other task merges on its own; (E-2) the ruleset taps are decided yes: `Governance` required, GitHub Actions as the source of all four checks, "Require branches to be up to date", and a `claude/kit-state` ruleset blocking deletion and force-pushes; (E-3) Sonnet writes by default and Opus writes money, privacy, security and recovery application code, with an Opus reviewer on every task in those areas and on every harness task: `scripts/validate_tasks.py` now requires the Opus model only for a build task that owns `app/` or `migrations/` paths in a sensitive area, so test-only, docs and harness work in those areas runs on Sonnet; T039, T032 and T036 move to Opus and T040 gains an Opus reviewer; the mix is 113 Opus, 50 Sonnet, 2 Haiku of 165 model tasks; (E-4) OPS-043 stands: the live service deploys only a commit Casey names after an independent Opus security review; (E-5) the three behaviour-changing security rules stand (PRIV-025, PRIV-030, PICK-056); (E-6) `Ci` is designed for cost (caching, prebuilt tools, one boundary build, coverage in its own job, a 15-minute warm ceiling) and the only step left for Casey is to read the Actions minutes quota in the repository's billing page once and reply with the number so the schedule can be paced. Hands-on steps that stay his because only he can do them: `claude setup-token` into the `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret, the ruleset settings, and the quota reading; each becomes one `owner` issue with exact taps. Reason: Casey asked for best-practice decisions, and each of these follows from a rule already in the plan (a reader-enforced rule is not a rule; the brief's "Sonnet: most code"; separate identities for the operator and the automation). Source: `BUILD_BRIEF.md:215`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md:497`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:1`.
- **D-484** Question (an independent Opus review of this audit's own pull request, #54): did the audit's changes introduce defects? Findings, each verified: hand-written `lib.rs` and `api.rs` (D-481) were owned only by each crate's first task, so later tasks could not add their `mod` line or public functions, and `app/server/src/lib.rs` had no owner; `check_pr_change.py` read the task graph from the branch under test, so a task branch could grant itself paths or the harness type; deleting a locked file skipped the decision rule because the locked set came from the head tree; owner-gate phrases were still matched as substrings ("got it" cleared a "go" gate, "yesterday" a "yes" gate), so D-479's claim was not yet true; a `Plan-Change` trailer matched any decision merely cited in a new entry; a missing merge base crashed with a traceback; SEC-029 and TD-SEC-10 still required the `testing` feature D-481 removed, and architecture section 4 forbade calling `rules` that D-481 re-exports through `api`; `smoke.sh` and T169 put `--check` after `--`, where cargo would reject it; the Rust scanner missed `matches!`, `if let` and match arms on statuses outside `status.rs` and flagged rustfmt-split `#[allow]` attributes and "XXX-XXX" strings; the Governance job's own check run would land red on `main`; `progress.py` compared timestamps rather than commit ancestry, and `nightly-main` let any dispatched branch write the record with workflow-wide write access; CODEOWNERS covered paths seven ordinary tasks own (T002, T003, T004, T008, T116, T154, T171), so they would have waited for Casey, against D-483's own statement; TD-TXT-13 contradicted TXT-026 on the calling hours; the brief and the manual still described one scheduled runner; T174 was cited but not in the task graph; "before T001" remained though T001 was absorbed; the brief's model line and the validator did not match D-483 (security omitted from the area set, in-crate tests counted as application code); T169's title still said per-crate clippy; T172 could not read the twin it copies; a renamed dependency (`package = "..."`) evaded the crate allow-list; `kit/layers.json` held a string where a list belonged; a first-party crate named `core` would shadow Rust's built-in `core` and break macro paths. Choice: `app/<module>/src/lib.rs` and `src/api.rs` for every crate are shared paths, and T009 owns `app/server/src/lib.rs`; `check_pr_change.py` reads ownership, type, protected and shared paths from the base's `kit/tasks.json`, counts every file the base lock names, requires the trailer to equal a new entry's ID, and prints a clear error with no merge base; gate phrases match as whole words; SEC-029 and TD-SEC-10 describe in-crate tests and `api::rules`, and section 4 rule 1 allows calls to a lower crate's `api::rules`; first-party packages are named `askhava-<module>` (lib `askhava_<module>`) and the allow-list follows; `--check` moves before the `--`; the Rust scanner adds the outside-status-module checks, reads whole attributes and limits TODO/FIXME/XXX to comments; the Governance job is renamed `governance-runner` and only its published check run carries the name; `progress.py` checks `merged_sha` against the nightly commit by ancestry, falling back to the run's start time, which `nightly-main` now records from the API; the nightly record runs only on `main` with job-level write access; CODEOWNERS is narrowed to `.github/`, `.claude/`, `runner/`, `BUILD_BRIEF.md`, the constitution, the governance file, `docs/sources.md`, `kit/tasks.json`, `kit/layers.json`, `kit/allowed_crates.txt`, the prompt template and the four governance scripts, which only harness tasks and plan changes touch; TXT-026 states the fixed calling hours of PICK-018 ("Call between 8 a.m. and 8 p.m. Arizona time (any hour on an emergency job).") and TD-TXT-13 and TD-TXT-14 follow; the brief (lines 208 and 498, edited in place) and the manual describe the Actions runner first and the narrowed Opus rule; new harness task T174 (the runner's live entry point and everything `docs/build-readiness.md` section 4 item 6 lists) runs first, before every stage-1 task; "before T001" reads "before the first build task" in the documents this audit wrote (this entry corrects D-479 and D-483, which are append-only); the validator requires the Opus model for any build task in a money, privacy, security or recovery area that owns application code outside `src/tests/`; T169's title, T172's reads, the crate allow-list's rename handling and `kit/layers.json`'s lists are fixed; issue closures are recorded but can never clear a gate, since a closure carries no text and the session's API route to close an issue is denied. Reason: the audit's own changes get the same independent review it asks of every task. Source: `docs/governance.md:1`, `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md:1`.
- **D-485** Question (plan change P-1 from the October 28 audit, carried out October 29, 2026: `docs/audit/prebuild-audit-2026-10-28.md`, `docs/build-readiness.md` section 4; Casey Oct 28: "I dont want to make the decisions you make them based on best practices"): how are the audit's plan defects fixed so that a task session can finish inside one context window and the validator proves the graph sound? Findings, each verified by the validator before the change: 93 tasks had a goal equal to their title; prompts were titles with pointers, so a worker loaded whole spec files (one 170 KB file for three lines); budgets guessed 1% per read; 44 build tasks exceeded 10 files, 600 changed lines or 12 requirements; 10 migrations were numbered out of the runner's serial order; 11 tasks named requirements whose spec file they did not read; 18 build and harness tasks had no test and no acceptance line; 26 requirements were not testable as written (A11Y-001, A11Y-005, A11Y-009, A11Y-018, AI-016, BIZ-042, CALL-013, CARD-009, CARD-010, CON-010, CON-032, INT-010, INT-015, INT-038, INT-039, INT-044, LIC-018, LIC-025, LIC-040, PICK-052, PRIV-019, SEC-012, TXT-005, WEB-015, WORD-009, WORD-013, each tagged "(D-485)" in `docs/spec/`); 10 lines of `BUILD_BRIEF.md` contradicted later decisions (110, 143, 182, 340, 375, 421, 449, 461, 465, 569); photos were to be stored in PostgreSQL (D-094) against the 1 MB row and backup-size facts; the PII scanner had no email rule and no image-metadata check; and the threat model had no inbound-email boundary. Choice: `kit/prompts/TEMPLATE.md` and `scripts/gen_prompts.py` paste into each prompt every requirement sentence with its file and line, every test-design row in full, the journey text, the first 400 characters of each decision and each cited line, and derive `budget.context_pct` from the bytes of the prompt, the owned files and the reads (spec, test design and decisions count only through the pasted lines; the cap is 20% for every task, 30% for a harness task); 41 build tasks are split into 83 parts (new IDs T175 to T216, each part with its own goal, files, requirements, tests and test command), T124 folds into T126's acceptance, T217 adds the stage-2 sentinel scan over database rows and logs; every task has a goal that says what done looks like and either tests or an `acceptance` line; `scripts/validate_tasks.py` adds V1 (tests or acceptance), V3 (every mutant's killing test is owned by an ancestor), V4 (the harness comes before the code it guards), V5 (goal differs from title), V6 (a `rules.rs` owner has an Opus reviewer), V7 (a task reads the spec file of every requirement it names) and V9 (migration numbers rise in serial order), with build-task limits of 10 owned paths, 600 lines and 12 requirements (harness, docs, audit and cleanup tasks keep D-119's 14/800/25); every migration is renumbered to its owning task's serial position; the 26 requirements are rewritten as testable sentences with their numbers pinned as settings (INT-010 2 hours and 50% word overlap, INT-039 0.9, INT-044 320 px, CARD-009 5 words, CALL-013 10 seconds, SEC-012 one year), meaning unchanged; the 10 brief lines are corrected in place (617 lines kept); photos move to object storage behind the `storage` adapter with only the key in `privacy.photo` (superseding that part of D-094; `docs/architecture.md:125`, `docs/architecture.md:230`); `scripts/pii_scan.py` fails on any email outside example.com, example.org, `.test`, `.example` and `.invalid` (with the documented role, GitHub, Resend-sink and pilot-placeholder exceptions) and on EXIF GPS or device tags in any image under `tests/` or `docs/design/`, and a file skipped for size fails unless listed; `docs/threat-model.md` adds boundary TB12 (inbound email) and threats S14 to S16, T16, I25 and D11. Not done, recorded: merging T004 into T002 (the merged task would need 22% of the window) and T030 into T029 (over the limits); the stage relabel of T167 and T170 waits for the re-plan after the hand-run test. Output: `scripts/validate_tasks.py` 0 errors, `scripts/gen_prompts.py --check` 0 problems, `tests/test_validate_tasks.py` and `tests/test_gen_prompts.py` green (`docs/audit/process-review-2026-10-29.md` section 6).
- **D-486** Question (the runner-live harness work, formerly task T174, carried out October 29, 2026 on `claude/build-optimization` as a plan change, so that the first build task starts under code rather than prose; `docs/governance.md` section 5; R-04, R-16, R-17): how does the runner run live, how does it keep working between cycles, and how does it pause only before the plan's usage limits (Casey Oct 29: "it just keeps working until we are about to hit limits and then continues when limits clear")? Choice: `python3 -m runner` runs one cycle from code (`runner/__main__.py`; `--after` does the hand-in, `--dry-run` reads only), after `scripts/check_state.py` has validated `kit/state.json` against `kit/state_schema.json`; `runner/github_env.py` is the real outside world over git and the GitHub REST API only (GraphQL is denied); the lease lives on its own ref `claude/lease`, pushed atomically with the state so the ref is the lock, and a lost race exits in under a minute; every state commit carries `runner_sha` and the starter (`Runner-Sha:` and `Runner-Starter:` trailers), regenerates STATUS.md from `scripts/progress.py --markdown` and appends one PROGRESS.md line; the review gate records `reviewed_sha` and the held lease before the branch is pushed, and `auto-pr.yml` (taking `runner/verify.py` from `main`, never from the branch) opens the pull request without auto-merge and says why unless the head is the reviewed one; the runner itself turns on auto-merge for the reviewed head under its lease, so the squash merge is the runner's identity and starts `backup-runner.yml`, which now also runs on `pull_request: closed` merged into `main` (the hourly tick stays as the safety net); red `main` is a FIX-MAIN task whose goal is `git revert --no-edit <sha>` first, owning only the offending merge's files, the only eligible task while `main_red` is set, cleared only by a green smoke run with no failing nightly on the same head, the offending task returning to the queue with one more attempt; `scripts/stage_exit.py` gates each numeric stage on the previous stage's evidence (every task done, nightly green, the stage audit recorded), so a blocked mid-stage task holds only its dependents; per-attempt history, the deviations register and `usage.by_task` are recorded; pacing (`scripts/pacing.py`, `kit/pacing.json`) estimates each task's cost from its budget, model and attempts, calibrates on the first three measured tasks, pauses only when the next task would cross the five-hour or weekly limit less a 10% headroom, and resumes at the recorded reset, reading the plan's real utilisation through `PACING_QUOTA_CMD` (default `scripts/quota_probe.py`, which asks the account's OAuth usage endpoint with the session's own token and prints nothing on failure, when the ledger estimate stands in); the two budgets in `kit/pacing.json` are placeholders until the first rehearsal reports the real figures. The `backup_runner` flag stays off until Casey's `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret exists and one rehearsal cycle has run under it; the Appendix A prompt (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:574`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:586`) is rewritten in place to the entry-point form; T174 leaves the task graph (its work is this entry) and its label in code and docs now reads D-486. Output: `tests/test_runner_live.py`, `tests/test_stage_exit.py`, `tests/test_check_state.py`, `tests/test_failure_drills.py`, `tests/test_pacing.py`, `tests/test_quota_probe.py` green; `python3 -m runner --dry-run` against the live repository prints `runner: idle (dry run)` (Gate P holds every task).
- **D-487** Question (Casey Oct 29, 2026: "If we need any more expertise for any part of the build or program function find out whose info is needed and create the needed brain files"): which expertise does the build lack, who holds it, and how does a session that knows nothing get it? Findings: 53 references in the plan point at `/home/claude/hhq/outputs/…`, research packs outside this repository that no new session can reach (legal memos, the simulations, the GTM pack, the operator pack); no task read anything on Rust production practice, PostgreSQL operations, A2P 10DLC and TCPA rules, Stripe payment flows, WCAG 2.2 form patterns, mailbox-provider sender rules or how a contractor judges a lead source.
