Choice: eight sourced expertise files under `docs/brains/` (`rust-production.md`, `postgres-operations.md`, `sms-compliance.md`, `payments-stripe.md`, `accessibility-forms.md`, `email-deliverability.md`, `contractor-lead-experience.md`, `operator.md`, with `README.md` as the index), each naming the recognised authorities (the Rust API guidelines, Tokio and Axum maintainers; PostgreSQL's own documentation and the operators who run it at scale; the TCPA, the FCC's 2024 to 2026 orders, the CTIA principles and Twilio's A2P rules; Stripe's documentation on intents, idempotency, webhooks and disputes; WCAG 2.2, the GOV.UK Design System and Adrian Roselli; Google's and Yahoo's bulk-sender rules and RFC 8058; the FTC's HomeAdvisor record and contractor forums; Hormozi, Martell and Koerner for the operator), the rules they set with a URL to a primary source for every principle and `[inferred]` on anything unsourced, a requirement map, a review checklist, and proposals where a brain found a gap or conflict. The brains are locked (`kit/plan.lock`), never above the spec (a disagreement is built as the spec says and reported), and wired into the 194 tasks they serve under `reads`; `scripts/gen_prompts.py` pastes into each prompt the brain's review checklist and the lines that name the task's requirements, so a prompt carries the specialist's rules without the whole file's bytes; a proposal is never a rule for a task until a decision adopts it (the first adopted set is D-490); the unreachable packs are risk R-19 (`docs/risk-register.md`). Source counts and line counts: `docs/audit/process-review-2026-10-29.md` section 6.
- **D-488** Question (a throwaway build of the full 19-crate Rust skeleton that `docs/architecture.md` sections 2, 4 and 11 describe, run October 29, 2026 outside the repository so that T009 starts from a layout known to compile; R-01): does the Rust design compile and test as written, and what do the first Rust tasks need to know? Findings, each reproduced in the trial (`docs/audit/rust-skeleton-trial-2026-10-29.md`): every pin resolves and the full set builds, lints and audits clean; the layer boundary, the lint set, trybuild, sqlx offline data, the shared rustls provider, askama_web and both binaries work as designed; but `tests/recovery` cannot be named `askhava-recovery` (the recovery module holds the name); jiff-sqlx timestamps fail in `query!` without a `sqlx.toml` type override and the `sqlx-toml` feature; `payments` could not reach `privacy` under `kit/layers.json`, so section 4 rule 4 could not compile; the capability design stops no crate that depends on `privacy` from calling `release_mint()` itself (only a struct literal, `Default` and `Clone` are compile errors), so SEC-030's "a compile-fail test proves it" was overstated; `wildcard_enum_match_arm` misses a `_` covering one variant and `matches!`; `cargo build -p server` matches no package under D-484 naming, so `smoke.sh` would have gone red on the first session with a `Cargo.toml`; `install_default()` needs an explicit `rustls` dependency and the architecture test needs `toml`, neither on the allow-list; `scripts/check_rust_rules.py` misses thirteen shapes and wrongly flags two; the `sqlx migrate build-script` output fails `missing_docs`; no task owned the mint wiring; and the trial's "resolved 401 crates" is 421 external packages. Choice: the recovery test crate is `askhava-recovery-tests`, the one exception to the `askhava-<module>` rule (TD-SEC-10, `kit/allowed_crates.txt`); sqlx gains `default-features = false` and `sqlx-toml`, and every crate's `sqlx.toml` (a shared path, `[macros.type-overrides]` `'TIMESTAMPTZ' = "jiff_sqlx::Timestamp"`) carries the override; `payments` calls `privacy` in `kit/layers.json` and section 3; SEC-030, section 4 rule 4 and TD-SEC-10 now state what holds: construction is impossible from outside, and the once-per-process `release_mint()` plus the architecture test's source scan for `ReleaseMint`, `release_mint`, `SessionMint` and `session_mint` outside `privacy`, `payments`, `server` (and `portals` for the session mint) stop a second minter, with tests that build the composition sharing one fixture per test binary; every `status.rs` denies `match_wildcard_for_single_variants` too; `-p askhava-server` in `scripts/smoke.sh`, `ci.yml`, TD-SEC-10 and the task goals; `rustls =0.23.46` (`default-features = false`, `aws_lc_rs`, `std`; Apache-2.0 OR ISC OR MIT; audit clean) and `toml =1.1.8` (dev; MIT OR Apache-2.0; audit clean) join section 11 and the allow-list; new harness task T219 (Sonnet, Opus review, before the first `status.rs`) closes the scanner gaps with planted-case tests; T012 generates `build.rs` with `--source ../../migrations` and a `//!` line; T022 owns the capability types and T189 the minter and the `register_handlers()` hookup; T009, T012, T168, T169, T175, T022, T189, T192, T179, T023 and T219 read the trial report, whose section 4 holds each prompt's specifics (workspace members, feature lists, one `TestCases` per binary, `.stderr` files tied to the rustc version, the cache check for `target/tests/trybuild`). Measured on 2 vCPU: cold build 106 s (19 crates) and 127 s (full set), cold clippy 109 s, cold `cargo test` 515 s of which trybuild 406 s, warm one-line change 2.8 s build and 8.6 s test, release server 200 to 301 s, `target/` 3.6 GB; a cold `Ci` pass is 12 to 15 minutes, so the 15-minute ceiling needs a warm cache. The trial exercised neither the worker claim loop, the database roles, `rules_bridge`, the cross-schema checks nor the Docker image; those stay with their tasks. D-092's "runs Alembic" reads "runs the sqlx migrations" since D-453; D-094's photo storage was superseded by D-485. R-01 closes.
- **D-489** Question (Casey Oct 29, 2026: "Optimise this process and then check the build process for any problems or things we can optimize"; `docs/audit/process-review-2026-10-29.md`): where does a build cycle lose time, tokens or certainty, and what is the cheapest change that removes the loss without weakening a check? Findings: up to 59 idle minutes per task between a merge and the next hourly tick; 12 to 15 minutes of Rust compile on every push, including the 41 non-Rust tasks and every plan change; sessions compacting mid-task on prompts that were titles; the runner's rules living in prose; design mistakes left for the first task to hit; knowledge outside the repository; one blocked task holding every later stage; red `main` with no way out in code. Choice: the merge trigger, prompt text and budgets, the live runner, the skeleton trial, the brains, stage gating and FIX-MAIN are D-485 to D-488 and this entry adds the `Ci` change: a "what this change touches" step computes `rust` and `npm` from the diff against the merge base, and the Rust compile, test, audit and deny steps run only when `app/`, `migrations/`, the Rust test crates, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `clippy.toml` or `deny.toml` changed, the TypeScript step only when `package.json`, `package-lock.json` or a `.ts` file changed, while the skip guard, the document checks, gitleaks, the PII scan and the governance checks stay unconditional, so a deleted input or a wrong document still fails and a non-code change finishes in about 3 minutes; the Actions-minutes estimate falls from about 7,000 to about 5,500. Examined and left alone, with reasons in the review: one task at a time (two workers would collide on the shared paths on nearly every pair; revisit if the measured cycle time stays above 90 minutes), the model mix, the retry ladder, the hand-run test before Gate P (D-455; starting stage 1 during the test is recorded as Casey's option, not taken), and the nightly, twin and accessibility runs. Still open: the Actions runner and the routine both starting a cycle (the loser exits in under a minute; the routine is paused after three clean Actions cycles), a worker's cold `target/` (accepted), and Casey's hands-on steps, which `docs/build-readiness.md` section 4 blocks READY on.
- **D-490** Question (the proposals the eight brains of D-487 made where they found a gap or a conflict in the spec; a proposal is not a rule until adopted): which are adopted as requirements, and where? Findings, each cited to its brain line in the new requirement's source: CON-001 sent a business-intent reply from the homeowner number while TERMS-011 told carriers businesses get no texts there (conflict); the campaign samples lacked the embedded phone number of TXT-027; the privacy policy's sharing sentence sat beside the texting section where a reviewer reads it as sharing numbers (Twilio error 30951); no mailing address before registration; opt-out evidence died with the TXT-020 body deletion; no Spanish disclosure step before TXT-039; no segment or encoding check on templates; FEE-016's per-attempt keys left room for two PaymentIntents on one pick; a card saved on Confirm was not saved for the PICK-048 off-session charge; no statement-descriptor length test; FEE-027 auto-submitted dispute evidence that costs a fee whatever the odds; Stripe fees were absent from the ledger view; early fraud warnings had no handling; a webhook-built `ReleaseProof` trusted the event body; Stripe's public business details were nobody's checklist item; axe "incomplete" results, sticky-bar focus, drag-only uploads, input-border contrast, placeholder labels, client-side validation, disabled submit buttons, code-field autocomplete, noisy live regions, email `lang` and a JavaScript-only chat were uncovered accessibility gaps; Gmail complaints are invisible without Postmaster Tools; soft bounces never aged out; a re-verification could hit a provider suppression; email webhooks lacked body verification, dedupe and a silence alert; job-card emails lacked one-click unsubscribe headers; nothing kept notices transactional; inbound-mail placement was undecided; no warm-up panel; the plan never said it buys no requests; business copy had no ban on "ready to hire" and its family; a business had no way to say a card was not a fit; nothing forbade bundled charges; and the scorecard lacked the measures a contractor judges a source by. Choice: 39 requirements adopted with their source lines, each tagged "(D-490)": TERMS-029 to TERMS-032, CON-036, TXT-043 to TXT-046, FEE-047 to FEE-053, SEC-049 to SEC-051, OPS-044 to OPS-047, A11Y-025 to A11Y-035, BIZ-055 to BIZ-057, WORD-030 and PICK-057, with the CON-001 conflict resolved by option B (the one business reply is a registered sample from the homeowner number, TERMS-029; TERMS-011 and TERMS-013 are amended to name that exception, and FEE-027 to defer to FEE-050's evidence condition) and FEE-047 keeping FEE-016's per-attempt keys for the confirm calls while the PaymentIntent itself is one per pick; twelve test-design rows (TD-WEB-12, TD-CON-25, TD-TXT-26, TD-TXT-27, TD-FEE-32, TD-FEE-33, TD-SEC-27, TD-A11Y-01, TD-A11Y-02, TD-BIZ-33, TD-OPS-21, TD-WORD-30; TERMS-031 joins TD-TERMS-04); each requirement assigned to the task that owns its surface, and a new build task T218 (Sonnet, stage 5) for the three scan-style requirements (WORD-030, TXT-046, TXT-044) so that T026, T029, T104, T111 and T112 stay within their limits; T053 hands A11Y-028 and A11Y-029 to T166 and T101. Not adopted, left for the re-plan after the hand-run test: the contractor brain's P-BIZ-6 and every proposal a brain marked as needing a vendor's written answer first.
- **D-491** Question (a cold read of all 209 task prompts in build order by eight independent Opus reviewers, a spec-against-test sweep of all 713 requirements, and the second edition of expertise files, October 29, 2026; Casey: "we need to find the most optimal uses of our tokens and time" before the build starts): what would have made a build cycle fail as the plan stood? Findings, 385 in all over three passes (70 blockers), each confirmed against the files, grouped by cause. (1) Customer words: the two template files were owned by T026 alone, so 40 later tasks that must send a text or email were told "if you find yourself writing words a homeowner or business reads, stop and report". (2) The layer wall: pages and senders were assigned to `ops` (layer 1, which may call nothing): the health page, the scorecard, the intake report, the alert emails, the coverage email, the HELP requests and the owner help page; `ai` was asked to test routing and fees it cannot see; `payments` was asked to close jobs and send texts; `privacy` had no path to object storage or the texting provider's delete endpoint; the intake request limit sat in `portals`. (3) State without a table: 18 tasks had to store new state and owned no migration; `status.rs`, which section 2 names, had no owner anywhere. (4) Order: T219 (the scanner fix "before the first `status.rs`") ran at position 192; card matching (T044) needed the license map of T153, 134 positions later; the AI writer (T163) came after the two tasks that call it; J1 and J2 (T098, T099) exercised features from seven later tasks; the ledger table (T067) came after four tasks whose tests insert ledger rows; the email pipeline (T094) after five tasks that send emails; T051 used T022's capability types two positions early; T064's refund test needed T066. (5) Tests on the wrong task: about 60 test rows sat on tasks that owned none of the files or layers the cases need. (6) Stale text pasted beside current rules: decisions a later decision reversed (D-051, D-052, D-127, D-237, D-371, D-398 against the CON-031 consent text; D-310 against BIZ-046; D-209 to D-274 against D-436; D-363, D-234, D-294, D-410 and others) appeared with no note; `cargo test -p askhava-recovery` named the app crate; e2e commands lacked the Playwright config path. (7) Spec contradictions: TXT-005's `https://` scheme pushed the fixed consent text past two segments; OPS-024 resumed charges the server may never start (FEE-016); TD-TERMS-02 kept the pre-D-490 campaign wording; TD-FEE-25 forbade the PICK-048 off-session path FEE-048 requires; INT-022 contradicted INT-010; TD-TXT-25 tested a sentence count TXT-040 no longer sets; TXT-045 paused a whole business for one address's unsubscribe; SEC-049 had no stand-in rule; FEE-028 lacked the entry types FEE-050 and FEE-051 write; FEE-052's refund had no lifecycle trigger; the legal drafts lacked D-490's sentences; OPS-047 read a quote-form field PICK-040 did not have; eight D-485 boundaries had no test case; TD-FEE-23, TD-WEB-01, TD-WEB-03, TD-WEB-04, TD-INT-22, TD-BIZ-01, TD-BIZ-03, TD-BIZ-11, TD-CARD-09, TD-PICK-15, TD-TXT-09, TD-TXT-16, TD-CALL-05, TD-CALL-08 and TD-TERMS-01 each contradicted the requirement they cover; A11Y-015's "only" list missed the D-480 code windows; WORD-029 had no test row; the Spanish HELP reply was not fixed text; SEC-007 kept recovery codes in a password manager while OPS-042 and PRIV-024 say paper. (8) Missing pieces: no task built team members or staff phones (`businesses.team_member`, `businesses.staff_phone`), the terms, privacy, SMS-terms and data pages the consent text links to, a sandbox mode for the contract tests, or a voice stand-in; AI-020's NFKC fold had no allowed crate; BIZ-015's PDF statements and BIZ-041's push app had no crate and contradicted architecture section 3. Choice, each applied in this plan change: `app/messaging/templates/en.json` and `es.json` and every crate's `src/status.rs` are shared paths (each task adds only its own keys or variants); a task flagged `copy` in `kit/tasks.json` gets the customer-copy steps in its prompt; `privacy` calls `adapters` (`kit/layers.json`, section 3); the `ops`-owned pages and senders move to `portals` and `messaging` files (T106, T151, T158, T200, T204, T210, T215), the `ai` prompt-injection test to the integration crate (T072), the intake limit to `intake` (T089), the unreachable-homeowner decision to `picks` in stage 4 (T191), and `payments.expense` is a table (section 3); 18 tasks gain their own migration and every migration is renumbered to its owner's serial position; T219 follows T009; T044 depends on T153, T051 on T022, T156 and T208 on T163, T064 on T066 (T065 first), T098 and T099 keep their stage-4 slice and a new T222 runs the full J1 and J2 variants in stage 5; about 60 test rows and 20 requirements move to the tasks that own their files (the lists are the diff of `kit/tasks.json` against `main`); `scripts/gen_prompts.py` marks a decision a later one withdrew, superseded, replaced or reversed (read from the decisions' own words and from `kit/superseded_decisions.json`) and pastes the successor, names `askhava-recovery-tests` for recovery tests and the Playwright config for e2e specs; the 20 requirements and 41 test rows named in `docs/audit/prebuild-sweep-2026-10-29.md` are rewritten as the sweep found, including: TXT-005 (scheme optional), OPS-024 and J3 (a Confirm press in safe mode is refused, only releases resume; FEE-017 drops "including retries"; an auto-confirm pick falls back to the Confirm page), TXT-045 (a per-address opt-out), TXT-024 (the safety call from a non-main number, gated by CALL-022), TXT-044 (Spanish templates reported, never failed, except the CON-031 text), FEE-047 (the intent created on the first Confirm-page load), SEC-050 (the Postmaster check in the monthly review TD-OPS-08 describes), SEC-049 (stand-in mode accepts only the stand-in's host), FEE-028 (three entry types), JOB-014 and section 6 (the fraud-refunded path), PICK-040 (an optional minutes field), A11Y-015, CON-018 (the Spanish reply as fixed text), SEC-007 (recovery codes on paper, OPS-042 superseding `BUILD_BRIEF.md:317`), TERMS-002 (an OPTIONAL, OFF arbitration section allowed), BIZ-015 (CSV statements only in V1), BIZ-041 (the push app is Phase 2 work, T160 and T211 move to phase 2 stage 6); new tasks T220 (team members and staff phones, before T079), T221 (the legal and data pages, before T112 and T116), T223 (the session-proof compile-fail cases, after T022); T129 gives the contract tests a sandbox mode; T167 gives the Twilio stand-in a derived-key field, a sent-log query and a voice session; `unicode-normalization` joins `kit/allowed_crates.txt` (MIT OR Apache-2.0; for AI-020); three expertise files join `docs/brains/` (`ai-intake.md`, `legal-terms-privacy.md`, `deploy-operations.md`), wired into 41 tasks, their proposals waiting for the re-plan except the contradictions already fixed here; `docs/build-readiness.md` section 3 moves the `ReleaseProof` compile-fail clause from stage 2 to stage 3, names the reduced J1 (T173), the chaos baseline (T172) and the full journeys (T222), and restates the stage-6 row so T142 can certify it. Not done, recorded for the re-plan: the legal brain's TERMS-001 against the text pick (TXT-031, PICK-046), the FTC dark-pattern note on PICK-049's pre-ticked share box, the incident plan's notice method against A.R.S. 18-552, the deploy brain's Railway findings (Config as Code ends 2026-12-01, no post-deploy health monitoring, rollback restores variables, Cloudflare Full Strict with Railway, no documented proxy ranges, R2's key scoping against PRIV-029, the migration credential in the web service), and the AI brain's INT-021 disagreement. Batch 1 (the first 27 tasks, read last) added: a spec-editing task could not regenerate the prompts its sentences are pasted into, so `kit/prompts/` is a shared path and any task that changes a pasted sentence regenerates them with its decision entry and lock rewrite; the off-database stream writer sat in `ops`, so it is a `server`-registered handler (T017); T168's acceptance borrowed T022's and T192's proofs; the quote-token salts had no migration (T014); T009 listed a workspace member T175 creates; nothing created the `askhava_app` and `askhava_migrator` roles the migrations grant to, so `scripts/smoke.sh` and `Ci` create both before any migration and T012 tests the append-only grant on a `core` table; T002's license-scope check pointed at `categories.yaml` while LIC-043 puts scope in `license_map.yaml`; copying the twin verbatim would fail the PII scan, so T172 owns the scanner's allow-list; the golden set (T154) is sized to about 10 rows per trade; T170 owns the runner files its gate change needs; T007 owns the oracle generator; TD-SEC-13's Rust cases go to T011 and T016; T010's test runs in the fixtures crate; and sixteen smaller reads, deps, acceptance lines and goal corrections. A second pass by three reviewers over the 165 repaired prompts found 82 more (11 blockers), applied the same day: T224 (the business Confirm-and-pay page with Stripe Elements and the Stripe webhook route; PICK-021 and TD-FEE-25 leave T148) and T225 (the append-only ledger table and `api::ledger_append`, before every task that writes a ledger row) are new; T160 and T211 (the push app) leave the graph, since BIZ-041 says it is not built in the pilot and T088 proves no push route exists; T094 (the email pipeline) opens stage 3 so no sender runs before it, and a payments task 'sends' an email only as a `core.task` event that `messaging` handles; `site` may call `ops`, `payments` and `businesses` for the figures its pages show; the shared layout and each crate's `askama.toml` (a shared path) start with T051 and T101 restyles them; INT-044 and CARD-014 state the V1 on-server photo check (format, size, metadata stripped, re-encoded, the homeowner's confirmation; face, plate and text detection with the AI screening after zero retention), and TERMS-025 and the privacy policy say the same; `pulldown-cmark` 0.13.4 (MIT) renders the legal documents at build time for T221; `kit/superseded_decisions.json` loses D-466, D-376 and D-094 (each only partly replaced) and gains D-365, D-323, D-327, D-334, D-335, D-178, D-291, D-294, D-148, D-192, D-062, D-197, D-261, D-187 and the task-assignment decisions D-320, D-341, D-353, D-368, D-415 and D-448; 31 test rows are split or corrected and 16 added so each case sits on a task that owns its files; TD-SEC-10 names the SQL forms SEC-029 forbids. The second Rust trial (`docs/audit/rust-trial-2-2026-10-29.md`) proved the roles, the worker loop (100 tasks of 20 ms across two workers in 1.0 s, each exactly once), the guarded update, `rules_bridge` (about 0.05 ms a call), the migration and SQL checks, typed settings and a scratch image, and changes the plan in these ways: migrations write `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA x` with no `FOR ROLE` and run as the migrator everywhere; `scripts/bootstrap_roles.sql` (T012) creates the roles once per cluster and `scripts/smoke.sh` and `Ci` create `askhava_app` and `askhava_migrator` before any migration; the test role needs CREATEDB, CREATE on the master database and SET on the app role; `ASKHAVA_MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL` is read only by `server migrate`; `core` gives `Guarded<T>` and the dedupe pattern while each module writes its own literal SQL; `-- no-transaction` is line 1 and the owner line follows; migration numbers may have gaps and are frozen once applied to the test project; tokio's `sync` feature is named; `Settings::from_pairs` replaces `from_iter`; the web binary handles SIGTERM and binds `0.0.0.0:$PORT`; cargo-chef 0.1.78 joins the tooling table and the allow-list, the runtime image is `debian:trixie-slim` with `libssl3t64`; the migration and SQL-form checks are T226's second architecture test file. A third pass by three reviewers over the 137 prompts the second round changed found 75 more (7 blockers), applied the same day: business-notice recipients are resolved by the enqueuing module through `businesses::api` and travel in the event payload, so `messaging` sends only to what it is given (T147 owns `quotes/src/card_notices.rs`); `businesses` enqueues a hold event that `privacy` applies rather than writing a privacy table (T092); the fee preview and TD-PICK-37 are T148's; T041 adds the after-hours tick and `businesses::api::night_count` so T039's INT-033 has a source; T094 is a copy task owning the email layout template; the quote-page cases (TD-PICK-38, TD-TXT-23) sit on tasks that own the quote page (T052, T095); `app/server/src/bin/worker.rs` is a shared path; T226 fails a migration order only when the higher-numbered file's task is an ancestor of the lower's; T208 depends on T045 and T044 (the card and the gate exist before the summary step shows them); TXT-042 and JOB-025's window half are T046's; INT-017's count, the coverage branch and INT-032's SPLIT trigger are T184's; BIZ-022 is T081's; T227 (the Playwright webServer config, before T055) and T228 (leaving Ask Hava, split from T165) are new; TD-SEC-10 exempts the test-layer crates and `askhava-server`; 10 test rows added and 68 split or corrected so each case sits on the task that owns its files; 63 migrations renumbered. Corrections to the claims above as the tree stands: 24 requirements were rewritten (the 20 plus TERMS-002, TERMS-025, INT-044 and CARD-014); the three brains are read by 38 tasks; the chaos baseline is T074's; batch 1 read 30 prompts; T160 and T211 left the graph (the phase-2 wording earlier in this entry was the first repair's choice, replaced in the second). Output: `scripts/validate_tasks.py` 0 errors over 223 tasks; `scripts/gen_prompts.py --check` 216 prompts, 0 problems; `scripts/trace_spec.py` 0 errors; `scripts/check_legal_docs.py` 0 errors; cargo-audit on the trial workspace with `unicode-normalization` 0.1.24 and `pulldown-cmark` 0.13.4 added: 0 vulnerabilities (the same allowed RUSTSEC-2026-0374 warning).
